**How to read this document.** §0 is the summary: decisions, guarantees, deviations, what the design does not do. §1 fixes scope, requirements and terminology. §2 states the adversary views and the declared channel leakage LC. §3 and §4 are the library and post-quantum decisions. §5 defines every new wire and storage format byte by byte. §6 is the protocol and its state machines. §7 integrates it with the relay, `:sync` and `:entitlement`. §8 covers ephemeral posts and history. §9 is the schema, §10 the modules and the JNI surface, §11 the invariants, §12 the test and exit-gate plan, §13 gates, CI and dependencies, §14 slices, §15 the exit checklist, §16 risks, §17 owner questions, §18 findings outside the phase, §19 the normative corrections of the review rounds (§19.0a lists every finding). Appendix A holds the ADR drafts (Romanian), B the text changes in other documents (Romanian), C the evidence (verified vs assumed), D the sources. "(normative)" marks text that wins over a summary table. Citations `path:line` are relative to the worktree; `F7`, `F8`, `F9` are the Phase 7, 8 and 9 designs; `TM` the threat model; `INV` INVARIANTS; `om:` the `openmls-0.9.0` crate sources; `spec p.N` the spec PDF.

**Identifier conventions.** Decisions `D-n`; guarantees `P-Cn` (privacy) and `CS-Cn` (correctness); deviations `X-n`; requirements `R10-n`; conflicts `C-n`; commit-ordering rules `CO-n`; seal rules `CS-Sn`; residues `L8-Cn` (new LIMITE section L8) and the declared channel leakage `LC-n`; invariants `T36–T41`; crash scenarios `SCN-CA … SCN-CM`; the exit scenario `SCN-M20`; joins `JC-n`, twins `NI-CHn`; mutants `MM-n` (correctness), `PC-n` (privacy), `ME-Cn` (ephemeral); the exhaustive state model `EM-3`; slices `S0 … S12`; S0 checks `V10-n`; owner questions `Q-n`; review findings `P10-Rn` (protocol), `PRV-Hn`/`PRV-Mn`/`PRV-Ln` (privacy), `FEA-Hn`/`FEA-Mn`/`FEA-Ln` and the proposed questions `FEA-Qa … Qh` (feasibility), mapped in §19.0 and, every one of them, in §19.0a.

---

## 0. Summary

### 0.1 Decisions

| # | Decision | Rejected alternative (why) | Plan / ADR alignment |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | **What the forum is.** The forum is the set of invite-only channels a user belongs to. A **channel is one MLS group** (spec p.7: "An MLS group used as an encrypted forum thread"; FR-3.2). Inside a channel, posts are flat with optional reply references; threads are a Phase 13 rendering of reply references, not separate groups. **No content readable by non-members and no public discovery in Phase 10** (FR-3.1 makes discovery conditional: "if introduced"). | Public channels readable by non-members (a key anyone can obtain is a key the operator can obtain, so FR-3.3 would hold only in form); open joins through a published GroupInfo (external commits weaken PCS, RFC 9750 §5.3, and remove the sponsor that ADR-13 relies on); one MLS group per thread (a namespace, tokens and commits per thread) | FR-3.1, FR-3.2; LIMITE E27 ("canalele rămân pe invitație prin MLS") |
| D2 | **Library: OpenMLS 0.9.0** (MIT; released 2026-08-25, the newest version on crates.io on 2026-09-25) with the **libcrux crypto provider 0.4.0** (MIT), exact `=` pins, no default features, `forbid(unsafe_code)` outside tests (`om:src/lib.rs:142`). GHOST supplies the **storage provider** (a change-set adapter, D5), the **Signer** (the ADR-04 pseudonym key, in Rust) and the random source (the provider's HMAC-DRBG). Forbidden features: `crypto-debug`, `content-debug`, `test-utils`, `targeted-messages-draft`, `virtual-clients-draft`, `sqlite-provider`, `js`, `backtrace`, `0-8-1-storage-format`, `migration-import`; `extensions-draft`, `fork-resolution` and `draft-ietf-mls-pq-ciphersuites` off in P0. **Measured in S0 (V10-1):** the stack does not resolve into the ghost lock as published — OpenMLS's *optional* SQLite provider conflicts with Arti's SQLite on `links = "sqlite3"` — so the workspace carries a manifest-only stand-in for that never-compiled crate (§3.2, S0-1, Q40). | mls-rs 0.56.0 (Apache-2.0/MIT, no public external audit found); DMLS (an expired individual draft); an own MLS (forbidden by FR-2.2 and CP-09) | FR-2.2, spec §7.3, plan:106; amends spec Appendix A "0.8.x" (X-5); ADR-40 |
| D3 | **Ciphersuite: `MLS_128_DHKEMX25519_CHACHA20POLY1305_SHA256_Ed25519` (0x0003) for every P0 channel. No post-quantum claim for channels.** ADR-12's own test ("suita hibridă OpenMLS când e stabilă") fails today: draft-ietf-mls-pq-ciphersuites-06 has only TBD code points and is "Waiting for WG Chair Go-Ahead / Revised I-D Needed"; OpenMLS's X-Wing suite uses the non-standard code point 0x004D behind a draft feature and was outside the SRLabs audit; and the X-Wing commits measured with GHOST's extensions (V10-9, S0-10: a Remove is 28.8 KB and an Add with its evidence 33.0 KB at 20 members in a blank tree) need 64 KiB blobs from 20 members and cap channels near 38 (Welcomes) to 46 (Adds) members. Channels are therefore **not** protected against harvest-now-decrypt-later, unlike DMs (declared, L8-C1). The design is suite-agile (the suite is part of the invite and of the channel record; a change is a new channel incarnation, §4.4) and names the triggers for adopting the hybrid suite. | X-Wing 0x004D now (experimental code point, unaudited path, 64 KiB blobs from 20 members, cap ≈ 38–46, no ReInit API so the choice is sticky); the WG provisional 0x004F (TBD code point; OpenMLS 0.9.0 maps it to the same X-Wing KEM as 0x004D and the libcrux provider accepts it without checking the KEM, so it "works" only as one implementation talking to itself, V10-9); a GHOST-designed PQ PSK layer (new cryptography, CP-09) | ADR-12 unchanged ("când e stabilă"); CP-10; §4; Q2 |
| D4 | **A GHOST outer seal around every MLS byte** (Rule V1 of F9 §1.1 applied to channels). RFC 9420 §6.3 leaves `group_id`, `epoch`, `content_type` and `authenticated_data` of a PrivateMessage in clear, and a Welcome names each joiner's KeyPackageRef in clear. Every channel blob is `salt(16) ‖ hint(16) ‖ ChaCha20-Poly1305(k, 0¹², AD, P)` — the DM shape of F9 §6.2 — keyed per **MLS epoch** from the MLS exporter (posts and commits) or per **generation** (join traffic). Uniform bytes; one AEAD attempt per blob. | Raw MLS on relays (headers leak group id, epoch churn and joins); a static per-channel seal key (joiners and removed members would open every header) | Annex B "namespace opac"; T1, T9; ADR-41 |
| D5 | **Storage adapter with value semantics** (answers SRLabs finding S1-3, "group state and storage get out of step when a write fails"): Kotlin passes the channel's stored key-value rows into each native call; OpenMLS runs over an in-memory map; the call returns a **change-set** that Kotlin writes in the caller's SQLCipher transaction **only on OK**. Values cross JNI only wrapped under the channel state key. The adapter replaces OpenMLS's stored wall-clock `added_at` by the Kotlin-supplied day of the call (a pinned-schema edit, the recorded deviation pattern of F9 §2.1); past-epoch retention is by **count**, computed by Kotlin from its own epoch days, never by OpenMLS's timestamps (§19.4); every KeyPackage lifetime is day-aligned. A **reload oracle** in tests drops and reloads the group after every call. **Measured in S0 (V10-5):** every call parses the channel's whole state, and OpenMLS rewrites every retained past epoch on each post (3.4 MB per post at 100 members with 64 retained); so past epochs are stored one row each and capped at 16 (S0-6), and receive passes and CO-3's trial settles run as **batched calls**, one load per batch (S0-17). | OpenMLS's SQLite provider (non-atomic with GHOST's rows, a second database); the memory provider persisted as one blob (write amplification at every post) | spec §8.1 "transactions … to avoid epoch divergence"; F9 F-10 carry-over; ADR-40 |
| D6 | **Generations.** A channel lives in a sequence of generations g = 0, 1, … Each has a **generation secret** `G_g = MLS-Exporter("ghost/v1/ch/generation", channel_id, 32)` of the epoch that opened it, from which derive its **namespace**, its per-slot **READ** and **DEPOSIT** secrets and its **join seal key**. A new generation opens at every commit that removes a pseudonym (Remove, leave, eviction) or restores a member onto a new device (the replaced device is treated as removed, §19.16), or changes the size class, and at a committer's **rotation flag**, set when an invite of the generation expired unconsumed or the generation is 28 days old (§19.7, Q28). A removed member cannot compute `G_{g+1}` (it cannot compute the new epoch's exporter), so after the rotation it holds no secret for the new namespace. Members switch to the new namespace at an hour the commit fixes, not at the minute they fetched it (§19.8); the old namespace is drained for 14 days. | Namespace per channel for life (a removed member keeps listing volume and timing for the channel's life; a token-bought write capability also reads, F9 §4.5); namespace per MLS epoch (a new write capability per epoch at each relay, and registrations reveal epoch churn); Annex B's `H(channel_public_key)` (computable from public material, against T26's principle) | amends Annex B and F8 D11 (X-1, X-2); ADR-41 |
| D7 | **Shared relay credentials (the T27 analogue).** Every member stores with the generation's DEPOSIT credential v3 and reads with its READ credential v3, per relay slot; the relay sees **one deposit scope and one read scope per (relay, generation namespace)** for all members. **Funders** (the owner and up to two members named in the policy) redeem their own ACCESS tokens for write capabilities used **only** for `RegisterKey`; no member ever stores or reads with a token-bought capability. **The relay needs no change.** | Every member redeems its own write capability (a writer field in substance, F9 §4.1; token use grows with channels); per-member blind rate tokens (a new relay kind and observable; P2) | D11 (F8 §10.7), ADR-25 point 9, ADR-30′; ADR-41 |
| D8 | **Commit ordering without a server (ADR-42).** Relays cannot order commits. Only **committers** (an ordered list of at most 8 pseudonyms in the channel policy: the owner at rank 0, then moderators) may commit membership or policy changes; any member may commit an **empty self-update** (lowest priority). For concurrent commits on one epoch every member picks the same winner — the candidate with the most successor senders, then lowest rank, then lowest SHA-256 of the commit — after a **settle window of 20 minutes** anchored at the earliest of its own sighting and the relay's store hour; a merged epoch is **final and sticky**: a later commit for it is a *late candidate* that OpenMLS cannot even decrypt (valn1307) and that never moves a member; a member leaves its branch only when the branch is shown dead (foreign blobs stay unopenable with no progress), and then recovers by a **pseudonym-signed rejoin** (§6.4, §6.7, §19.1, §19.3). Commit validity is a function of the commit, the group state and the commit's authenticated data only (admission evidence travels in the commit's MLS `authenticated_data`, §19.2), and every leaf a commit installs keeps its member's signature key and credential (§19.14). A member's own leaf may be swapped by any committer, whatever its rank, when the member itself asked (a signed rejoin or an invite-authorized restore), and a committer may resign or the owner transfer rank 0 (§19.13). Non-committers never send MLS proposals, and a received proposal is dropped unprocessed (§19.16): joins, leaves and requests travel as application messages or join blobs, and committers include proposals by value. | A relay compare-and-swap or sequencer (a proto change and one relay with the power to fork or censor); exposing the relay sequence (per relay, and the leak Phase 9 sealed); revert-after-merge (posts processed under a reverted epoch); DMLS (expired draft); member proposals (OpenMLS refuses to encrypt application messages while its proposal store is non-empty, `om:src/group/mls_group/application.rs:92-94`) | RFC 9750 §5.2.2 ("deterministic tie-breaking policy"); amends ADR-13's removal trigger (X-3); ADR-42 |
| D9 | **No reflex traffic in channels (T40).** No store, redemption or registration is caused by a received blob. Committers commit at **scheduled passes** (at foreground session starts, at most every 6 h, and once a day in background, when work is pending; an empty PCS commit every 7 days) or at their own user actions; members self-update every 28 days at a PRF slot; funders fund at the Phase 8 renewal time and the rotating committer at the switch hour its own commit fixes. Declared exceptions (§19.8, §19.22): LC-6 (whether a pass produces a commit depends on pending requests; whether its Welcomes are stored and, for a rotating commit, the new generation funded depends on whether it won; a member's self-update is retried only if it lost), LC-4 (a received rotation changes the listened namespaces at the commit's switch hour or the next session start) and LC-5 (a joiner's listening starts after its Welcome). | Commits triggered by received requests (relays correlate the request's arrival with the committer's store); "the first client that observes the threshold removes" (LIMITE:113) | T28 extended to channels; X-3; ADR-42 |
| D10 | **Invitations by link or QR, admitted by a committer (ADR-43).** A sponsor creates an invite (`ghost://channel/…`, ≤ 302 bytes, §5.9) carrying the current generation's namespace, READ and DEPOSIT bases, the join seal key, a one-time **invite signing key** and a **digest of the channel's committers** (the joiner accepts only a Welcome whose GroupInfo one of those committers signed: the GroupInfo signature alone authenticates only a signer the Welcome's own tree supplies, V10-8, S0-15), and posts `INVITE_ISSUED(invite_id, invite_pk, expiry_day, committers_digest, sponsor_sig)` in the channel. The invitee deposits a **join request** (its KeyPackage, signed by the invite key) sealed under the join key; a committer that holds the `INVITE_ISSUED` admits it at its next pass and copies the sponsor-signed evidence into the commit's authenticated data, so every member checks the Add without history (§19.2); the **Welcome** is posted in the same namespace, one sealed copy per joiner, only after the commit won (§19.2), and carries the current generation bootstrap. Invites are single-use (the first presenter wins: a leaked unused invite **is** an entry, L8-C4), expire within 7 days, die with their sponsor's membership, and may be sent inside a GHOST DM as text. | KeyPackage and Welcome through the sponsor's DMs (the invitee must be a DM contact; a second online round trip for the sponsor, and the Welcome forward is a reflex); an invite without a signing key (any member who saw a join request could redirect the invite to a sock puppet) | ADR-05 (QR in person recommended, `ghost://` only); ADR-43 |
| D11 | **History policy "none" only in P0** (FR-3.8 requires an explicit policy; LIMITE:126,133 and TM S4 name "none" as the default). A joiner decrypts nothing before its join epoch — a cryptographic property of MLS. `since_join` is what "none" means in MLS; `full` or "N days" needs an application-level history transfer (new protocol, weaker FS and PCS, RFC 9750 §6.7) and is deferred to Phase 13 or later (Q11). | Sponsor re-sharing of recent posts now (new protocol, FS reduction, CP-09) | FR-3.8, LIMITE L3; ADR-43 |
| D12 | **Pseudonyms: ADR-04 unchanged** — `seed = HKDF(root, "ghost/v1/channel-pseudonym" ‖ len ‖ channel_id)` (`KeyDerivation.kt:24-30`), an Ed25519 key that is the member's MLS credential (`BasicCredential`, identity = the 32-byte public key). The seed is derived in Kotlin, crosses JNI once per channel context and signs in Rust (`ed25519-dalek`). **Stable per channel:** a person removed and re-added returns with the same pseudonym (THREAT_MODEL:81 "rotește odată cu părăsirea canalului" is corrected, X-4; Q13). The group id is the random 32-byte `channel_id`. | A per-join incarnation index (changes T14 vectors; not recoverable after a restore; a thief of the seed derives it anyway) | ADR-04, FR-3.9, T14; ADR-43 |
| D13 | **Reveal to one member, opt-in** (ADR-04, LIMITE:135). Each leaf publishes an X25519 **box key**, drawn at random for every new leaf (KeyPackage and update path) and deleted with the last epoch that used it, so reveals have forward secrecy per leaf refresh (a GHOST leaf-node extension; §19.17). A `REVEAL` post carries `HPKE-Base(box_pk(M), card ‖ bindings)`; only M opens it; every member sees that the author revealed to *someone*, not to whom. The payload is the author's DM contact card v2 with a two-way binding (pseudonym ↔ `ghost1`), **a proof M can show to anyone** (declared, L8-C8; the deniable alternative is Q35). The card enters the DM contact list as source `link`, trust `unverified`. | OpenMLS targeted messages (the recipient leaf index is in clear to every member, `om:src/targeted_messages/mod.rs:55-72`); a broadcast reveal (every member learns the link); the v1 per-channel `revealed` flag (not per person) | ADR-04, TB-7, LIMITE:135; ADR-43 |
| D14 | **Ephemeral posts per channel (ADR-27 §C, FR-7.15).** The channel timer is in the policy (set by the owner; default 7 d); every post carries its `timer_code` in encrypted content; a change is a policy commit every member processes and the UI shows. Countdown, deletion cascade, WAL checkpoint and unread deadline exactly as ADR-32 points 3–7. **Relay TTL of posts = ADR-27 point 11.** Handshake blobs (commits, join traffic, Welcomes, and the administrative posts `INVITE_ISSUED` and `LEAVE`, which committers must still hold days later, §19.21) use the post TTL in channels with timer 7 d, 30 d or off, and **7 d in channels with timers ≤ 1 d** (declared deviation X-7, Q12). | Handshake at the post TTL for short timers (members offline more than an hour or a day cannot catch up); the whole short-timer channel at 7 d (content on relays longer than on phones, against ADR-27's intent) | ADR-27, ADR-32; T25 extended; ADR-41 |
| D15 | **Size classes and buckets.** Two classes fixed per generation: **small** (≤ 32 members, bucket floor 4 KiB) and **large** (≤ 100 members, floor 16 KiB). A blob's bucket is the smallest of {4, 16, 64} KiB that is ≥ the floor and holds the body. With 0x0003 and canonical KeyPackages and leaves (S0-4, S0-5), every single-proposal commit of a channel within its class cap fits the floor in every reachable tree shape (measured, V10-4: tightest margin 172 B, a restore swap at 32 members in a 128-leaf tree), so a commit and a post are indistinguishable per blob; commits with several proposals are split (§5.7); Welcomes use a larger bucket (declared, LC-5). **P0 limits (NFR-14): 100 members per channel, 9 channels per client (§19.8: 6 read pairs per channel while draining must fit the 64-pair budget), 8 committers, 3 funders, 16 000 bytes of text per post.** | One bucket per message type (leaks commit timing and group size); a 64 KiB floor (16× cost per post); no cap (sparse-tree commits grow linearly: 83 KB at 1 000 members classical, measured) | T9, NFR-14, spec §11.2; ADR-41 |
| D16 | **Module split.** Rust crate `client-core/mls` (`ghost-client-mls`) linked into `libghost_client_net.so`; a closed JNI surface `org.ghost.crypto.bridge.MlsNative` (gate G-M6, the G-R6 pattern); no network, async runtime, logger or clock in the crate; `rayon` pinned to one worker. Kotlin: `:crypto-bridge` wraps `MlsNative`; `:messaging` gains a channel engine run by **the same messaging actor** as DMs (one writer); schema **migration 6** (`:sync`) and **migration 7** (channel tables; v1 channel tables dropped behind a fail-closed guard). The test host library `ghost-dm-host` also exports `MlsNative`. | A second native library (a second Rust runtime in the APK); a second actor thread (cross-feature flows would need a handoff table) | ADR-19 ("și, în fazele următoare, `ghost-client-mls`"); ADR-40 |
| D17 | **Governance scoped to what ordering needs.** Phase 10 delivers roles (owner, moderators = committers), Remove and leave, sponsor attribution (from `INVITE_ISSUED` and Add commits), idle eviction, and the S5 governance simulation over the threshold function. Flags, quarantine, strikes, exclusion lists and verified channels (ADR-13, P1) are specified as reserved formats and delivered with the Phase 13 UX (Q15). | All of ADR-13 now (plan row 10 does not list it; P1) | ADR-13 ("Fazele 10 (primitive MLS), 13 (UX); P1"); TM S5 |
| D18 | **Exit gate proved on the JVM with the real OpenMLS** through the host library, the real `:sync` engine and the conformance-pinned `ModelRelay` with new hostile modes (FORK, WITHHOLD_COMMIT, EXPIRE_LOG, …): **SCN-M20** (20 clients: adds, 200 posts, four kinds of removal, offline resync across ≥ 5 epochs and a rotation, a member offline beyond retention that rejoins, concurrent commits, a relay-induced fork, a re-add), crash enumeration at every SQL statement, an exhaustive commit-arbitration model EM-3, the removed-member and joiner seizure oracles, the reload oracle, the RFC 9420 WG test vectors and OpenMLS's own suite at the pinned tag, the channel privacy world, fuzzing, and mutants registered only with their killers. An emulator job and a device checklist cover what the JVM cannot. **Several of these rest on harness pieces Phase 9 designed but never built** (the seeded native randomness, the redeem lane inside the world, HIGH mode and send delays, circuit timing); they are sized work in S1 and S2 of this phase, not assumed (§19.10). | Emulator-only or device-only gates (the Phase 9 runner has no KVM; STATUS:18) | plan row 10; F9 §15 method |

### 0.2 Guarantees claimed (each has a test in §12; none is a UI claim before the gate is met, CP-10)

**Metadata at relays (AD-1: the operator with every relay and the issuer).**

| ID | Guarantee | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| P-C1 | **No channel identity at relays.** No relay-visible value (namespace, verifier, credential, stored byte, capture field) is computable from `channel_id`, pseudonym or box keys, invite ids, KeyPackages, or any MLS header without a generation secret, an epoch secret or an invite. No MLS header byte (group id, epoch, content type, AAD, KeyPackageRef, sender data) appears outside the seal. | T36: JC-1, JC-2, derivation vectors, header scan, PC-1, PC-7, PC-13 |
| P-C2 | **No writer or reader field.** Every store into a generation namespace at a relay presents the one DEPOSIT credential of that (relay, namespace); every list and get presents the one READ credential; token-bought write capabilities appear only in `RegisterKey`. | T37: JC-3, PC-4, PC-10 |
| P-C3 | **Commits look like posts** in channels with timer 7 d, 30 d or off: every blob of a generation namespace uses TTL = the channel TTL of its timer version (a timer change between 7 d, 30 d and off moves it, as for DMs; a join request sealed under an invite issued before the change keeps the invite's TTL, declared, §19.22) and bucket = f(length, floor), and every commit of a channel within its class cap fits the floor (commits too large for the floor are split, §19.18). | T37: JC-4, NI-CH2, PC-3, PC-8 |
| P-C4 | **Removal.** (a) Cryptographic: nothing a removed member held opens a blob of an epoch after its removal. (b) Metadata: after the rotating commit, the removed member holds no secret of the new namespace (it cannot compute `G_{g+1}`); a device replaced by a restore is treated as removed (§19.16). (c) **Removal hold** (§19.6): no honest member encrypts a post in epoch e after it first holds a valid candidate for e that removes a pseudonym; what the removed member can still read is limited to posts encrypted before their author saw the removing commit (declared, L8-C18). **All three hold for members to which the relays deliver the removing commit within its TTL**; a subset from which the relays withhold it stays in the old epoch with the removed member until it is flagged (CO-12, L8-C17, §19.15). | T38 (with the hold and restore variants): removed-member seizure oracle, PC-2, PC-14, MM-7, MM-8, MM-29 |
| P-C5 | **Joiner history "none"; the joiner joins the channel it was invited to.** Nothing a joiner holds opens a blob of an epoch before its join epoch; the joiner does open the earlier **join traffic** of its own generation (it holds `JK_g` from the invite: other joiners' KeyPackages and pseudonyms, declared, L8-C3, §19.22). A joiner accepts only a Welcome whose GroupInfo is signed by a committer named in its invite (S0-15): a holder of `JK_g` who is not such a committer cannot move it into a group of its own (declared residue: such a committer can, L8-C25). | T39: joiner oracle, PC-2, JN FAKE_WELCOME, PC-19 |
| P-C6 | **No reflex.** Worlds that differ only in what a client received (valid, forged, replayed, join requests, commits) give identical relay views of that client, except the declared LC-6 (pass outcome; Welcome store and rotation funding after a won commit; a member's self-update retry after a lost one), LC-4 (a received rotation changes the listened namespaces at the commit's switch hour or the next session start) and LC-5 (a joiner starts listening after its Welcome) (§19.8, §19.22). | T40: NI-CH1, NI-CH4, PC-5, PC-6 |

**Correctness and local soundness.**

| ID | Guarantee | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| CS-C1 | **Atomic protocol state.** An OpenMLS state change is stored if and only if the calling SQLCipher transaction commits; a failed call, a crash or a native panic leaves the previous state. | SCN-CA … SCN-CM crash enumeration, reload oracle, MM-1 |
| CS-C2 | **Exactly-once display** per `op_id` per device, across crashes, relay repeats, resurrections and re-encryptions after FAILED. | `oracle_displayed` primary key, SCN-CB |
| CS-C3 | **Convergence (T41).** Honest members whose device clocks are within the KeyPackage lifetime margin (§19.4) and that hold the same valid candidates and the same held blobs for epoch e when they settle e merge the same commit, and their `epoch_authenticator`s are equal at every epoch. Commit validity depends only on the commit, the group state and the commit's authenticated data (§19.2), **except the KeyPackage lifetime predicate, which reads the member's trusted day (S0-12)**: every KeyPackage an honest committer admits is accepted by every honest member's trusted day, so honest commits are judged alike; a **malicious committer** can admit a KeyPackage whose lifetime bound falls between two members' trusted days and split them (at most the skew of their trusted days, 2σ; detected by CO-11 and CO-12; declared, L8-C20). A member whose system clock rejects a KeyPackage its trusted day accepts stalls (`clock_blocked`) instead of choosing differently. | EM-3, SCN-M20, SCN-CD, SCN-CM, MM-3, MM-4, MM-27 |
| CS-C4 | **Forks are detected in-band when the branches' traffic meets.** A settled epoch is never left because of a later commit (§6.4 CO-6); a member whose branch is dead (foreign blobs stay unopenable with no progress, CO-11) reaches `resync_needed` and recovers by rejoin while its generation is listened to, otherwise `reinvite_needed`; a persistent split with progress on both sides raises `split_suspected`. **A relay partition that keeps the branches apart is not detectable in-band** (RFC 9750 §5.2: "only by out-of-band comparison"): it is bounded by the committer-liveness rule (`partition_suspected` after 9 days without a committer commit while others post) and, for a member cut off in silence (it missed a rotating commit and its old namespace went quiet), by `stale_suspected` after 9 days with neither a committer commit nor any other member's message (CO-12, §19.15), and by the `epoch_authenticator` fingerprint the UI offers for comparison (L8-C17). Every clause of CS-C3 and CS-C4 holds **while at least one relay serving the member delivers every commit within its TTL**; the flags are what remains otherwise. | EM-3, SCN-M20 FORK variant, SCN-CG, SCN-CL, MM-16 |
| CS-C5 | **No loss inside the declared envelope.** A post whose op ends SENT or DEGRADED is displayed once by every member that lists the namespace within the TTL and holds the post's epoch, unless a declared loss applies (§6.3.6, which since §19.18 includes posts pushed past the sender ratchet's tolerance by a relay that withholds part of a burst). | seeded worlds, SCN-CB, SCN-CE |
| CS-C6 | **Deterministic order** of display on every device (author minute, then `op_id`). | reorder suite RO-C |
| CS-C7 | **T25 bounds** for channel posts. | T25 channel suite, SCN-CF |
| CS-C8 | **Bounded local state** (§6.3.5, §8.3): past epochs, epoch keys, pending requests, join and rejoin state, dedup tags. | introspection, structural invariants after every reboot |
| CS-C9 | **Migration safety** v5 → v6 → v7. | SCN-CI, migration tests |

### 0.3 Deviations from approved or proposed documents (each recorded in an ADR draft)

| # | Deviation | From | ADR |
|---|---|---|---|
| X-1 | Channel namespace derived from a generation secret, not `H(channel_public_key)` | plan Annex B (plan:433); T26's principle (F9 D4) | ADR-41 |
| X-2 | Read (and deposit) keys shared per **generation**, registered with `RegisterKey` (weeks, accumulating); removed members are cut off by the generation rotation, not "la epoch-ul următor" | plan Annex B (plan:434); F8 D11 "per (namespace, epoch)"; ADR-25 point 9 | ADR-41 |
| X-3 | Only committers remove, at scheduled passes or their own user action; "primul client care observă pragul emite Remove" withdrawn | LIMITE:113; ADR-13 | ADR-42 |
| X-4 | A pseudonym is stable per channel for life; it rotates only with a new channel | THREAT_MODEL:81 | ADR-43 |
| X-5 | OpenMLS 0.9.0 (not 0.8.x) with the libcrux provider | spec Appendix A (spec p. 26) | ADR-40 |
| X-6 | `posts` hold the author pseudonym, not an "authenticated author identity" | spec §8 (spec p. 17) | ADR-43 |
| X-7 | In channels with timers ≤ 1 d, handshake blobs use TTL 7 d (relays can tell them from posts there) | ADR-27 point 11 (for those blobs only) | ADR-41 |
| X-8 | `:sync` API: `Funding`, `CapabilityKind.REGISTER`, registration capabilities kept apart from store credentials; schema migrations 6 and 7 | F7 §9 API; ADR-34′ | ADR-40 |
| X-9 | v1 tables `channels`, `channel_pseudonyms`, `mls_state`, `posts` dropped (fail-closed guard) and replaced; history policy values reduced to `none` | schema v1 (`Schema.kt:180-210`) | ADR-43 |
| X-10 | "Autentificare Noise între relay-uri și convergență de date (Faza 10)" is spec v2.0 numbering (its Phase 10 was "Multi-relay metadata protection: three relays, gossip, …", spec p. 23); v2.1 row 10 owes no gossip | STATUS:14; `relay/README.md:20` | erratum (Appendix B.4) |

### 0.4 What this design deliberately does not do

- **No post-quantum protection of channels and no PQ wording for them** (D3). DMs keep theirs (ADR-36); the UI must not imply the two are alike.
- **No public content, no discovery directory, no open joins, no external commits, no published GroupInfo** (D1); every commit calls `create_group_info(false)`, so OpenMLS's exported GroupInfo (with an `ExternalPub` key) is never even built (V10-8, S0-9).
- **No GREASE and no extensibility inside a channel** (S0-4, S0-5): every KeyPackage and every leaf has one canonical form per suite, so an oversized or unknown extension cannot enter the tree; RFC 9420 §13.5 recommends GREASE values, which GHOST therefore omits, and a new capability or extension is a flag day for all members or a new channel (§3.8, R-2).
- **No MLS proposals from non-committers, no targeted messages, no SafeAAD, no AppDataUpdate** (the `extensions-draft` feature stays off).
- **No history transfer to joiners** (D11), no pinned posts (FR-3.7, P2), **no channel media in P0**: spec FR-3.4 requires "text, media up to 50 MiB" in posts, but channel media needs Phase 11's media engine; §5.10 reserves the Phase 11 contract (the descriptor TLV, the key slot, the destination id) and Q38 decides whether Phase 10's exit waits for it. Until then FR-3.4's media half is not claimed.
- **No relay change**: no new RPC, credential kind, observable field or value (§7.8).
- **No fork-resolution feature of OpenMLS** in the shipped graph: recovery uses GHOST's rejoin with `swap_members` (§6.7).
- **No copy of a previous group state** after a merge (RFC 9750 §5.2.2 option 2): with a copy and the recorded commits a seized device would re-derive every later epoch, read posts included; late commits are therefore never validated, only recorded (§19.1).
- **No reflex, no read receipts, no typing, no delivery status beyond Phase 7's outcome.**
- **No second native library, no second actor thread, no new Gradle coordinate, no logger.**
- **No claim that pseudonyms hide who is behind them from other members' behavioural analysis** (ADR-04's unlinkability is cryptographic only).
- **No claim that relays cannot learn channel membership**: session co-occurrence (LIMITE L2.1 #1, L7-D2) rebuilds the client ↔ namespace graph under AD-1; Phase 12 owns the decoupling. Q22 of Phase 9 (no UI claim of channel unlinkability against relays) stands.
- **No claim that relays cannot tell roles apart**: under AD-1 the funders (by default the owner), the rotating committer and the removed member are identifiable by their redemptions, their stores and their listening (LC-8, §19.9).
- **No claim that a partition by the relays is detected in-band** (RFC 9750 §5.2); the design bounds it and offers an out-of-band fingerprint (CS-C4).
- **No deniable reveal in P0**: the reveal's two-way binding is a signature the recipient can show to anyone (L8-C8, Q35), and the MLS signature on every post already proves to third parties which pseudonym sent it.

---

## 1. Scope, requirements, terminology

### 1.1 The exit gate and what it pulls in (normative)

The plan's gate has three clauses. Each maps to a proof in §12:

| Clause | Read as | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| "20 clienți add/remove/resync" | 20 clients in one channel; adds by invitation and Welcome; removes by moderator, by leave, by idle eviction and by the device-revocation swap; resync after restart, after missed commits still on relays, after missed commits past their TTL, and after a fork (FR-2.2, FR-3.2) | SCN-M20 (§12.5) under crash enumeration and seeded worlds; EM-3 |
| "membru scos nu decriptează epoch-uri noi" | P-C4(a) under a seizure model: every secret a removed member held, including retained past-epoch secrets and generation secrets, against every blob of every later epoch (FR-3.8, TM S4, LIMITE:39) | T38 oracle (§12.8) |
| "T25 și în canale" | ADR-27's deletion on every honest device, the relay TTL table, and the relay observing only the bucket | T25 channel suite (§8.5, §12.6) |

The gate does not say whether the 20 clients run in-process, on emulators or on devices. This design runs them **in-process on the JVM** with the production cryptography (as F9 did for its worlds) and states in §12.12 what that leaves unproved (real SQLCipher, real Tor latency, device performance). Spec Appendix A's "RFC 9420 interoperability fixtures" are the MLS working group's test vectors, run against the pinned OpenMLS and through GHOST's adapter (§12.2).

### 1.2 Requirements (IDs R10-n; sources verified in the worktree)

| ID | Requirement | Source | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| R10-1 | RFC 9420 through OpenMLS or another reviewed implementation; no simplified group encryption | FR-2.2 (spec p.8), spec §7.3 (p.15), plan:106 | §3 |
| R10-2 | Group state holds credential binding, epoch, tree, pending proposals and commits; Welcomes encrypted individually as opaque blobs; add/remove/update commits advance the epoch; app messages bind channel id, epoch, content type, operation id; desync triggers bounded resync, never a static key | spec §7.3 | §5.5, §5.6, §6 |
| R10-3 | Versioning, replay protection, bounded parsing, context binding, fuzzing; the four replay cases Phase 7 hands to the MLS layer | FR-2.8; F7:47-52 | §6.3, §12.10 |
| R10-4 | Rust crate `ghost-client-mls` behind JNI, bytes only, no protocol logic in Kotlin, reproducible native build | ADR-19:13,16 | §10 |
| R10-5 | PQ: hybrid suite "when stable"; no standalone KEM | ADR-12; plan:233 | §4 |
| R10-6 | F9's F-10 carry-over: storage adapter (S1-3), day rewrite and clock gate, log gate, `rayon` check, X-Wing size facts, `hpke-rs` MPL decision, SRLabs S0-1 risk-accepted, an ADR for spec Appendix A | `faza9-dm.md:2827` | §3.4, §3.6, §3.9, §13 |
| R10-7 | Invite-scoped channels; discovery only of explicitly public metadata | FR-3.1; LIMITE:233 | D1, §6.2 |
| R10-8 | One channel = one MLS group with persisted epoch and membership | FR-3.2 | §5.2, §9 |
| R10-9 | Posts encrypted and authenticated before the queue; author time at the minute; posts "MUST support text, media up to 50 MiB and an authenticated client timestamp", accepted by "Boundary-size, invalid-media and timestamp-signature tests" (spec p.8; the plan's row, plan:262, keeps only the timestamp) | FR-3.3, FR-3.4, plan:262, T13 | §5.5; media: §5.10, Q38 |
| R10-10 | Incremental sync with opaque cursors, dedup, idempotency (Phase 7) | FR-3.5 | §7 |
| R10-11 | Local filter, mute, block, hide (P1) | FR-3.6 | §10.3 (facade flags; UI Phase 13) |
| R10-12 | Removal blocks future epochs; new-member history follows an explicit policy | FR-3.8; LIMITE:126,133 | §6.6, §8.4 |
| R10-13 | Pseudonym derivation per channel as MLS credential | ADR-04; `KeyDerivation.kt:24-30` | D12 |
| R10-14 | T14: pseudonyms unequal across channels, not derivable without the seed | INV:22 | §11 |
| R10-15 | Reveal only by an explicit opt-in MLS message to a member; nobody can be forced | ADR-04; LIMITE:135; TB-7 | D13, §6.9 |
| R10-16 | Ephemeral per channel: values, default 7 d, timer in content, change shown to all, countdown, deletion + checkpoint, relay TTL = smallest bucket ≥ timer, T25 | ADR-27 §C; ADR-32; FR-7.15 | §8 |
| R10-17 | The relay sees only namespace, hash, bucket, minute, capability or nullifier, one circuit | plan:241; TM:97-98; T1 | §2, §7.8 |
| R10-18 | Padding buckets (T9), a circuit per namespace (T21), fixed-size batches | ADR-01, ADR-09 | D15, §7 |
| R10-19 | Every blob on ≥ 2 operators; withholding caught by readers and the MLS transcript | ADR-11; F7:637 | §6.3, §6.7 |
| R10-20 | Shared read keys per namespace registered by hash; tokens never buy reads | F8 D11; ADR-25 point 9 | D7, §7.3 |
| R10-21 | Read schedule independent of activity; a client reads its own namespaces and its channels | T19, T31 | §7.7 |
| R10-22 | Writes need a token per (relay, namespace, week); a pack gives 16 ACCESS tokens per (slot, week); DMs use ≤ 2 | F8:29; F9 §4.8 | §7.4 |
| R10-23 | Schema: history policy, time columns at minute/hour/day; MLS state out of backups | spec §8; T4, T20, T33 | §9 |
| R10-24 | Governance primitives in Phase 10, UX in Phase 13 (P1) | ADR-13 | D17, §6.10 |
| R10-25 | NFR-2 p95 < 8 s over Tor on a 20-member channel; NFR-14 documented limits enforced by the UI; spec §11.2 group-size and pending-message limits | plan:281; spec pp. 11–12, 20 | D15, §7.7 |
| R10-26 | No GPL, AGPL, LGPL; MPL-2.0 only unmodified with source offered; the Rust allowlist and cargo-deny; OpenMLS in the external audit | ADR-37; plan:319, plan:464 | §3.9, §13.3 |

**Non-goals** (restating §0.4 as scope): public channels, discovery, channel media in P0 (contract reserved, §5.10; Q38), pins, history transfer, multi-device (P2), cover traffic (P2), relay-side changes, the Phase 13 UI, and the ADR-13 P1 governance mechanics beyond D17.

### 1.3 Terminology (normative; resolves the research's terminology conflicts)

| Term | Meaning in this design | Not to be confused with |
|---|---|---|
| **forum** | the set of channels a user belongs to; there is no global forum object | a public board |
| **channel** | one MLS group; `group_id = channel_id`, 32 random bytes chosen by the creator | spec p.7 calls it "thread"; plan and FR-3.1 call it "channel" (same object) |
| **post** | an MLS application message whose content is `ChannelContent` (§5.5); replies are posts with `reply_to` | a DM message |
| **epoch** | the MLS epoch only | the **ISO week** (`period_id` of `RegisterKey`, the Phase 8 access period) and the Phase 8 entitlement **epoch** (13-week pricing); F8 D11's "per (namespace, epoch)" is read as "per (generation, week)" (X-2) |
| **generation** | a run of epochs sharing one namespace and one set of relay secrets (§6.5) | an epoch |
| **history policy** | (a) what a joiner can read (FR-3.8): `none` only; (b) how long past-epoch secrets are kept (§8.3) | the relay TTL |
| **reveal** | an opt-in message that links the author's pseudonym to its DM card, readable by one chosen member (§6.9) | the v1 per-channel `revealed` flag, withdrawn (X-9) |
| **committer** | a pseudonym in the policy's ordered committer list; rank = index; rank 0 = owner | "moderator" (a committer of rank ≥ 1) |
| **funder** | a member named in the policy who pays the relay registrations of the current generation | a writer (all members write with the shared DEPOSIT key) |
| **branch** | the chain of final epochs a member has merged; two members on different branches share a prefix and then differ | a fork signal (evidence that another branch may exist) |
| **late candidate** | a commit-typed blob that opens under the seal of an epoch this member already finalized, with a different hash; recorded as a fork signal, never validated, ranked or merged (§6.4 CO-6) | a candidate (a commit for the current epoch, validated) |

### 1.4 Conflicts found in the sources and how this design resolves them

| # | Conflict | Resolution |
|---|---|---|
| C-1 | Annex B `namespace = H(channel_public_key)` vs T26's principle that nothing relay-visible is computable from public material | generation-derived namespace (D6; X-1) |
| C-2 | Annex B: removed members lose reads "la epoch-ul următor"; but `RegisterKey` registrations are per ISO week, accumulate and live until `start(w+2)+1h` (F9 §4.4), and a write capability grants reads | rotation to a new namespace at every removal (D6); per-epoch keys rejected (they would expose epoch churn) |
| C-3 | Annex B per-writer write capabilities vs Phase 9's finding that a per-writer scope is a sender field | shared DEPOSIT credential (D7) |
| C-4 | LIMITE:113 "primul client care observă pragul emite Remove" vs the no-reflex principle | committers at scheduled passes (D8, D9; X-3) |
| C-5 | TM:81 "pseudonimul rotește odată cu părăsirea canalului" vs the deterministic ADR-04 derivation | stable per channel; TM:81 corrected (D12; X-4) |
| C-6 | FR-1.9 / LIMITE:39 device revocation for groups vs single-device P0 and seed-derived pseudonyms | swap of the member's own leaf by rejoin; a thief with the seed holds the pseudonym key, so real exclusion is leaving that pseudonym (§6.12, L8-C9) |
| C-7 | History "full" needs a transfer protocol MLS does not have; relay TTL max 30 d vs spec FR-5.5's 90-day default | "none" only (D11); FR-5.5 is the relay default for other data and ADR-27 governs channel blobs |
| C-8 | ADR-13 and TM S5 put governance primitives in Phase 10; the plan row does not | D17 |
| C-9 | STATUS:14 assigns Noise gossip to "Faza 10" | spec v2.0 numbering (X-10) |
| C-10 | Spec §8 `posts` "authenticated author identity" vs ADR-04 | pseudonym (X-6) |
| C-11 | NFR-2 (p95 < 8 s) vs the ADR-20 30-second listing interval (estimated ~40 s p95 for NFR-1, LIMITE L2.1 #5) | not met by design; measured in Phase 12 (Q21) |
| C-12 | Spec Appendix A names OpenMLS 0.8.x; 0.9.0 fixes two pre-authentication decoding advisories | 0.9.0 (X-5) |
| C-13 | ADR-12's hybrid suite vs its stability, size and audit status | classical now (D3) |
| C-14 | OpenMLS refuses `create_message` while its proposal store is non-empty (`application.rs:92-94`); RFC 9420 §15.2 only requires handshake changes to be applied before new application messages | no proposals from non-committers; committers use their own proposals by value (RFC 9420 §12.4 allows it) (D8) |
| C-15 | OpenMLS validates every received Add's KeyPackage lifetime against the device's `SystemTime::now()` (`key_package_in.rs:195-197`, `public_group/validation.rs:405,882-887`), switchable off only by a crate-internal test flag (`skip_validation.rs`); GHOST's time model is Phase 7's trusted clock with σ = 3 d (F7:58) and the JVM worlds run on virtual days | lifetimes `[day − 4 d, day + 80 d]`; joins skip OpenMLS's tree-lifetime check and GHOST checks structure; a KeyPackage the member's **trusted** day does not accept makes the commit invalid whatever OpenMLS answered, and only a device-clock refusal of a KeyPackage the trusted day accepts stalls the member (`clock_blocked`) (S0-12); worlds are anchored so virtual days never pass the host clock (§19.4) |
| C-16 | RFC 9750 §5.2.3: "only Welcome messages corresponding to the commit that 'succeeded' are processed"; round 10a enqueued Welcomes at the pass, and OpenMLS deletes a non-last-resort KeyPackage once its Welcome is processed (`creation.rs:706-709`) | Welcomes stored only after the committer's own commit won (§19.2) |
| C-17 | Spec FR-3.4 requires channel posts with "media up to 50 MiB" (P0; spec p.8), the plan's FR-3.4 row keeps only the timestamp (plan:262), round 10b left media to Phase 11, and the Phase 11 design (branch `p11-design`, `faza11-media.md` Q17, RQ-M11) leaves channel media to Phase 10 | Phase 10 reserves the Phase 11 contract in its formats now (§5.10); whether Phase 10's exit waits for channel media is Q38; F-9 records the plan erratum |
| C-18 | RFC 9420 §12.2 makes a commit that removes its committer invalid (OpenMLS: `CannotRemoveSelf`, `commit_builder.rs:808-810`) and CO-2 lets a rank remove only higher ranks, so round 10b's owner could never leave or be restored | resignation, transfer and any-rank swaps (§19.13) |

## 2. Adversary views and the declared channel leakage LC

### 2.1 Effective views (not only T1 captures)

| Observer | What it sees for a channel |
|---|---|
| Relay (AD-2) | per generation namespace: stores (hash, bucket, TTL, minute, the one DEPOSIT scope), lists and gets (the one READ scope, per circuit), checks, funders' `RedeemToken` and `RegisterKey` (write-capability scope, `key_kind`, period), the circuit of every request; stored bytes (uniform) |
| AD-1 | the union over all relays and the issuer; session co-occurrence links a client's namespaces in ~3 sessions (L7-D2), so the client ↔ channel-namespace graph, and the generation chain of a channel (the new namespace starts in the same sessions as the old one) |
| Member (AD-6) | the roster of pseudonyms and box keys, leaf order (join order), every commit's author (who added, removed, changed policy) and its authenticated data (per Add: invite id, invite key, sponsor, expiry day; per swap: the rejoin's claimed epoch and day; the commit minute; §19.2), `INVITE_ISSUED` authors (sponsors), posts with author pseudonym and minute, reveals' authors (not recipients), leaves, KeyPackage lifetimes and binding days (day), each leaf's last refresh epoch (§19.5), the channel policy |
| Removed member | everything it held until removal: the old generation's secrets and namespace, retained past-epoch secrets, the roster; nothing of later epochs or generations (P-C4) |
| Invite holder (before admission, or a leaked invite) | the generation namespace, READ and DEPOSIT bases and join keys of that generation: it can list and store (volume, timing, quota) and open the outer layer of join traffic (KeyPackageRefs, KeyPackages of other joiners); no post or commit content. With another joiner's KeyPackage it can build a group of its own under the channel's id and deposit a Welcome that OpenMLS accepts (V10-8); the joiner refuses it by the committers anchor of the invite (S0-15), and so do rejoiners (§6.7) |
| AD-8 (seized device) | local rows and WAL; OpenMLS state: the current epoch — whose sender ratchets open every message of that epoch the device has not consumed, from the epoch's start (up to 1 000 generations ahead per sender, `sender_ratchet.rs:274-315`; §19.19) — past epochs within retention (§8.3), the tree; epoch seal keys within retention; pending plaintext of posts not yet encrypted; its own box keys still in use (§19.17) |

**Rule V1 for channels (normative).** Every relay-stored channel byte is sealed under keys never presented to a relay: epoch seal keys, the join seal key, KeyPackage and Welcome contents. READ and DEPOSIT secrets and write capabilities are presented; nothing else is.

### 2.2 Declared channel leakage LC (the only information the channel privacy world lets through)

Per generation namespace per relay:
- **LC-1** the minutes of stores, their size buckets (floor 4 or 16 KiB; Welcomes larger) and one TTL class per timer version (two in channels with timers ≤ 1 d: posts and handshake, X-7; a join request under an invite issued before a timer change carries the old handshake TTL, §19.22), and the one DEPOSIT scope; `check` events; in the large class no blob is below 16 KiB, so a relay can tell a large channel's namespace from a DM inbox by its buckets (§5.6 CS-S1);
- **LC-2** each member's list schedule (T19), gets per list (≈ the number of fetching members), hence presence and approximate size;
- **LC-3** funders' weekly redemptions and registrations at the Phase 8 renewal time, and the rotating committer's redemptions and registrations for a new generation at the switch hour its commit fixes (§7.4, §19.8);
- **LC-4** generation rotations: a new namespace starts (every member's first listing of it at the commit's switch hour, or at its next session start if it settled later), the old one drains for 14 days; under AD-1 co-occurrence links them, so the relays learn the count and timing of rotations — removals, restores onto a new device (§19.16) and the periodic and invite-expiry rotations of §19.7, which the relays cannot tell apart;
- **LC-5** joins: a Welcome-sized blob about 20 minutes after a committer pass (after its commit won, §19.2), and a new listener from the joiner's next session start; the joiner's own join request (a floor-sized blob) before;
- **LC-6** whether a committer's scheduled pass produced a commit (it does only when work is pending or at the weekly PCS commit), which reveals that some request was pending, and whether its Welcomes and, for a rotating commit, its funding of the new generation followed (they do only if the commit won); and whether a member's self-update is followed by a retry 24 h later (only if it lost, CO-9) (§19.22);
- **LC-7** the Phase 7 session-start co-occurrence, the grouping of requests per circuit within a session, the result of every request, and an identical hash on an identical-bytes resend (as F9 LD);
- **LC-8** **roles** (§19.9): under AD-1, with LC-3, LC-4, LC-5 and LC-7 together, the relays single out the funders (weekly redemptions for the namespace; by default the owner), the rotating committer (redemptions for a brand-new namespace; the store of the Welcome-sized blobs), co-funders (several capability serials crediting one verifier in `keys.redb`) and the removed member or replaced device (the one listener of `ns_g` that never lists `ns_{g+1}`), and co-occurrence attaches each to the client's DM cluster. A single relay that serves both `ns_g` and `ns_{g+1}` links the generations without AD-1.

Everything else a relay can compute from its effective view is a violation of T36, T37 or T40.

### 2.3 The privacy property the exit gate proves

(a) **Join-freedom:** the exact searches JC-1 … JC-7 find nothing in the effective relay view (§12.7). (b) **Non-interference:** twins that differ only in received content (NI-CH1), in commit versus post at the same moment (NI-CH2), in the number of channels a client funds per week at fixed renewal times (NI-CH3), or in whether a received commit rotates (NI-CH4, with LC-4's declared difference) give identical views. (c) The removed-member and joiner oracles open 0 (T38, T39). (a) and (b) are exact per seed, **on the harness of §19.10** (the redeem lane, send delays and circuit timing inside the world; until they exist, each check says which part of the view it compares); there is no statistical clause in Phase 10 (the channel world is small and the LC attacker saturates, lesson L-4 of the testing research; Phase 12 owns statistics over traffic).

**Scope (as F9 §22.8.8).** These properties are proved for the design and hold against AD-1 only for a build published in the transparency log and reproduced by the auditors (ADR-38, planned); today neither exists.

---

## 3. Library: OpenMLS 0.9.0 (ADR-40)

### 3.1 Why OpenMLS, and what is new relative to Phase 9's evaluation (normative)

F9 kept OpenMLS as the Phase 10 library (Q44) and recorded its costs for DMs. For channels the costs change sign: MLS's single-ciphertext fan-out and tree PCS are what a group needs. Alternatives: **mls-rs** 0.56.0 (AWS, Apache-2.0 OR MIT; no public external audit found — kept only as an optional test oracle, Q22); **DMLS** (draft-kohbrok-mls-dmls-03, expired, weaker FS); an own implementation (forbidden). OpenMLS evidence, checked 2026-09-25:

| Item | Fact | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Version | 0.9.0 (2026-08-25), newest and max stable on crates.io; 0.9.0-rc.1 … rc.4 in August | crates.io API |
| Licence | openmls, openmls_traits 0.6.0, openmls_libcrux_crypto 0.4.0, openmls_memory_storage 0.6.0: MIT; `tls_codec` 0.5.0: Apache-2.0 OR MIT; **`hpke-rs`, `hpke-rs-crypto`, `hpke-rs-libcrux` 0.7.0: MPL-2.0** | local `Cargo.toml` files |
| MSRV | 1.91 (GHOST toolchain 1.98.1, `ghost/rust-toolchain.toml`) | local `Cargo.toml` |
| Unsafe | `#![cfg_attr(not(test), forbid(unsafe_code))]` | `om:src/lib.rs:142` |
| Android | "Unsupported, but built on CI": `aarch64-linux-android`, `x86_64-linux-android` are built, not tested | `om:README.md:34-47` |
| Advisories | GHSA-rrmv-c79f-cf5r (medium, 2026-08-25: out-of-bounds panic in `DeserializeBytes` of `MlsMessageIn`, `PublicMessageIn`, `ProposalIn`, `Extensions`, … "decoded directly from untrusted wire bytes before any authentication"; < 0.9.0); GHSA-w62v-gv48-63rh (medium, 2026-08-25: quadratic duplicate detection in pre-authentication extension decoding; < 0.9.0); GHSA-8x3w-qj7j-gqhf (high, 2026-02-04: improper tag validation; < 0.7.2); GHSA-qr9h-x63w-vqfm (medium, 2025-09-26: secret tree not persisted after processing; 0.7.0, fixed 0.7.1). **None of the four is in RustSec** (no `crates/openmls` in the database at `593df8c1` of 2026-09-24 nor at `e2111519` of 2026-09-25), so `cargo deny` and `cargo audit` cannot see OpenMLS advisories (V10-2, S0-2) | GitHub advisories (`repos/openmls/openmls/security-advisories`) |
| Audit | SRLabs report v1.2 (2026-03-11): crypto and storage providers and the PQ suites out of scope; **S1-3** (state and storage diverge on a failed write, "Acknowledged"); **S0-1** (unbounded allocations, a group member's 50 MiB leaf extension slows every later tree operation, "Risk Accepted") | F9 §20 F-10; `phase9b/src/srl-openmls.pdf`, 42 pages, re-read in full in S0 (evidence Appendix A) |
| Maintenance | Phoenix R&D and CE Labs; `main` active to 2026-09-24; unreleased: `ExportedSecret<T>` zeroized on drop (a breaking API change for the next bump; 0.9.0's `export_secret` still returns `Vec<u8>`, `om:src/group/mls_group/exporting.rs:35-41`) | GitHub, CHANGELOG, local source |
| Production | XMTP and Wire run OpenMLS forks; Air uses the crate | F9 R-libs (not re-checked) |

**Consequences.** Pin `=0.9.0` (the two decoding advisories are pre-authentication and reachable by anyone who can put bytes in a namespace — but GHOST's seal means only holders of a seal key — members, and invite holders for KeyPackages in join requests — can, §5.6); every JNI entry point under `catch_unwind` with the silent panic hook (the F9 §2.6 pattern; `ghost/Cargo.toml` keeps unwinding); the pre-authentication parsers are fuzzed through GHOST's entry points (§12.10).

### 3.2 Pins and features (normative)

```toml
openmls                = { version = "=0.9.0", default-features = false }   # openmls declares no default feature
openmls_traits         = { version = "=0.6.0", default-features = false }
openmls_libcrux_crypto = { version = "=0.4.0", default-features = false }
```

- The `libcrux-provider` feature of `openmls` is not enabled (it only adds the provider crate to `openmls`'s own graph); GHOST depends on the provider directly, uses its `CryptoProvider` type (`openmls_libcrux_crypto/src/crypto.rs:22`) and does **not** use its `Provider`, whose storage is the memory provider (`lib.rs:42`). `openmls_memory_storage` 0.6.0 remains in the graph as a non-optional dependency of the libcrux provider (its `Cargo.toml`); gate G-M2 checks that no GHOST code names it.
- **Forbidden in every shipped graph (G-M2):** `crypto-debug`, `content-debug` (they log secrets and content if any logger is installed), `test-utils`, `targeted-messages-draft` (leaks sender→recipient to members), `virtual-clients-draft`, `sqlite-provider`, `js`, `backtrace`, `0-8-1-storage-format`, `migration-import`, `extensions-draft`, `fork-resolution`, `draft-ietf-mls-pq-ciphersuites` (D3), `all-ciphersuites`, `generate-kats`, `unchecked-conversions`. The test graph may enable `test-utils` for OpenMLS's own suite (`mls-vendor-tests`, §13.2) only.
- `openmls_basic_credential` is not used: the Signer is GHOST's (§3.3).
- **Lock resolution (V10-1, S0-1; normative).** `openmls` 0.9.0 declares the optional dependency `openmls_sqlite_storage` 0.3.0 → `rusqlite ^0.37` → `libsqlite3-sys ^0.35` (`links = "sqlite3"`), which conflicts with `ghost-client-net`'s `libsqlite3-sys 0.37.0` (Arti's directory cache, SQLite 3.51.3); Cargo resolves optional dependencies into the lock and `links` values must be unique there, so the workspace does not resolve at all with today's pins. No newer `openmls_sqlite_storage` exists (0.3.0 of 2026-08-25; upstream `main` still requires `rusqlite = "0.37"`). **Default (Q40):** `ghost/Cargo.toml` carries `[patch.crates-io] openmls_sqlite_storage = { path = "third_party/openmls-sqlite-storage-stub" }`: an empty crate of the same name and version (licence `LicenseRef-GHOST-Proprietary`, `publish = false`, an empty `lib.rs`, the four feature names `openmls` forwards to it — `draft-ietf-mls-pq-ciphersuites`, `extensions-draft`, `virtual-clients-draft`, `test-utils` — declared empty, and a README naming this rule). It is a manifest-level stand-in for a forbidden feature, not a modification of OpenMLS's code, and is removed when upstream relaxes the requirement (F-5). With it the lock gains 40 entries (22 names linked, below; 18 optional members nothing enables), measured. **Alternative (Q40):** Arti 0.46's `tor-dirmgr` accepts `rusqlite >=0.36, <0.40`, so pinning `ghost-client-net`'s `libsqlite3-sys` to 0.35.0 resolves without a stub (rusqlite 0.39.0 → 0.37.0, hashlink 0.11.1 → 0.10.0, the real `openmls_sqlite_storage` in the lock, never compiled), at the cost of an older SQLite in Arti's cache (3.50.2 instead of 3.51.3; it carries CVE-2025-7709, an FTS5 defect that needs control of the database file, fixed in 3.50.3).

### 3.3 Provider composition (normative)

| OpenMLS trait | GHOST implementation |
|---|---|
| `OpenMlsCrypto`, `OpenMlsRand` | `openmls_libcrux_crypto::CryptoProvider` (HMAC-DRBG seeded from `rand::rngs::SysRng`, reseeded from it, `crypto.rs:14-58`; `SysRng` reaches `getrandom`). **The deterministic host `getrandom` backend F9 designed (§22.8.10 point 7) was never built** (F9 §22 item 13 and l.3862: "no `getrandom_backend = "custom"` anywhere in the tree"), and OpenMLS encrypts path secrets inside `rayon` `par_iter` (`om:src/treesync/treekem.rs:75`) with an HPKE PRNG seeded from `SysRng` on the worker thread (`hpke-rs-libcrux/src/lib.rs:355-369`), so per-actor-thread attribution cannot work: S2 builds a **process-global** seeded stream with native calls serialised per JVM, or adopts record/replay (§19.10). **V10-6 confirmed the approach in a first probe** (two processes byte-identical, with the preconditions of §12.1, S0-7) |
| `StorageProvider` (**53 methods** in GHOST's feature set, 41 group-scoped and 12 global, plus `version()`; the trait's other 18 exist only under the forbidden `extensions-draft` and `virtual-clients-draft` features, `openmls_traits-0.6.0/src/storage.rs`, V10-5, S0-6) | `GhostKv`: an in-memory map loaded from the rows Kotlin passes, recording every write and delete into a change-set (§3.4) |
| `Signer` | `PseudonymSigner`: `ed25519-dalek` 2.2.0 (the workspace version) over the 32-byte pseudonym seed held in native memory for an open channel context |
| `OpenMlsProvider` | `GhostProvider { crypto: CryptoProvider, storage: GhostKv }` |

The pseudonym **public key** computed by `:identity` (BouncyCastle, ADR-17) and by `PseudonymSigner` must be equal: RFC 8032 key generation from a seed is deterministic, and a shared vector (`protocol/test-vectors/ch_derive.txt`) is replayed by both. MLS credentials are signed and verified only in Rust (the "one verifier per artifact type" rule of ADR-35's X-21 correction).

### 3.4 The storage adapter (normative; answers S1-3)

1. **Scope.** OpenMLS storage keys are either **group-scoped** (they include the `GroupId`: tree, context, confirmation tag, message secrets, epoch secrets, own leaf, proposal queue, join config, …) or **global** (KeyPackages by `KeyPackageRef`, signature key pairs, encryption key pairs by public key, PSKs). `GhostKv` maps them to two tables: `mls_kv(channel_id, k, v_env)` and `mls_global_kv(k, v_env)` (§9). A key that matches neither pattern at runtime is a `STATE_CORRUPT` result, never a guess (`GhostKvScopeTest` enumerates the 53 methods of GHOST's feature set, 41 group-scoped and 12 global, and a compile-time check fails if a feature adds methods; S0-6). An active member holds 11 group-scoped rows per channel and no global row (a KeyPackage's rows exist only from `keyPackage` until its Welcome is processed; V10-5).
2. **Load.** Before a call, Kotlin reads every `mls_kv` row of the channel and every `mls_global_kv` row **tagged with the channel** (every call is channel-scoped, so the adapter tags each global write — a KeyPackage and its private keys, a leaf encryption key pair — with the channel it was made for; GHOST's Signer stores no signature key in OpenMLS), and passes them as one TLV array. The rows' values are `nonce(12) ‖ ChaCha20-Poly1305(mls_state_key, AD = "ghost/v1/mls-kv" ‖ scope ‖ channel_id ‖ SHA-256(k), v)`; Rust unwraps them. A row that does not open is `STATE_CORRUPT`.
3. **Run.** OpenMLS runs over the map; every `write_*`, `queue_*`, `append_*`, `delete_*`, `remove_*` and `clear_*` updates the map and appends `(put | delete, scope, k, v)` to the change-set.
4. **Return.** On success the call returns its outputs and the change-set with wrapped values; on any error, `INTERNAL` (a caught panic) or a native crash, nothing is returned and Kotlin writes nothing. Kotlin applies the change-set **in the same SQLCipher transaction** as the channel rows the call affects (the inbox claim, the post row, the outbox op). There is no native cache across calls in P0 (Phase 9's value rule). **Its cost, measured (V10-5, medians of 9 on a loaded host):** every call parses the channel's whole state — 47 KB at 32 members and 134 KB at 100 with no past epoch, plus ≈ 18.5 KB / ≈ 53 KB per retained past epoch — so a received post costs 2.6 ms at 100 members with 2 retained epochs and 92 ms with 64, and a trial settle 13 ms and 300 ms. Two consequences are normative (S0-17): the calls that come in numbers are **batched** — `receive` takes up to 32 application messages of one epoch in peek order, and CO-3's successor count is one `trialCount` call per candidate (§10.2 fns 6 and 20) — so a pass costs one load per batch, not one per blob; and `GhostKv` loads its rows behind one interface (a **cache seam**), so Q18's version-checked native cache can be added later without changing a caller.
5. **Day rewrite (the "clock" half of F-10; corrected §19.4).** OpenMLS stamps `MessageSecrets` with `SystemTime::now()` when an epoch becomes **current** (`with_timestamp(SystemTime::now())` in `new_with_secret` and `replace_current_message_secrets`, `om:src/group/mls_group/past_secrets.rs:80,105`), not when it becomes past, and serialises it (`added_at`). `GhostKv::write_message_secrets` serialises the entity to `serde_json::Value`, replaces every `added_at` it has not seen before by the start of **the Kotlin-supplied `day` of the call** (the trusted clock, never the device's `now`) with zero nanoseconds, and stores the result. GHOST never uses these stamps for deletion (`before_timestamp` deletes **all** past epochs when the current epoch's stamp precedes the cutoff, `past_secrets.rs:271-279`, so a rule "keep e until final_day(e + 1) + R" cannot be expressed with day stamps); retention is by count (§8.3). This reads and writes OpenMLS's serde layout, outside semver (**recorded deviation**, like F9's S0-15): gate G-M7 pins a golden `MessageSecrets` per OpenMLS pin and fails when the field moves; a missing or renamed field is `STATE_CORRUPT`, never a silent skip.
6. **Reload oracle (tests; corrected §19.23).** In every world, after every native call, the harness reloads the group from the stored rows (`MlsGroup::load`, `om:src/group/mls_group/mod.rs:500`) and compares what the public API exposes — epoch, `epoch_authenticator` (`exporting.rs:218`), `tree_hash` (`mod.rs:1132`), own leaf index, and an exporter output under a test-only label — with the call's in-memory result. The secret tree has no public accessor, so its persistence is checked **behaviourally**: a reloaded group decrypts the next message of each sender that the in-memory group would decrypt, and refuses a replay of one it already consumed. This is the test that would have caught GHSA-qr9h-x63w-vqfm (a secret tree not persisted after processing). **S0 status (V10-12, S0-13):** the fingerprint half ran over the WG passive-client vectors (1 861 checks, all equal); the behavioural half cannot run on those vectors, which carry no application messages, and runs in GHOST's own worlds (in S0: late, out-of-order and replayed posts under value semantics, where every call is a reload, V10-5).
8. **Past epochs, one row each (V10-5, S0-6; a second recorded deviation like point 5).** `write_message_secrets` stores the `MessageSecretsStore` as a head row (the store with `past_epoch_trees` replaced by its epoch numbers) and one row `(channel_id, "MessageSecretsPast" ‖ epoch)` per retained past epoch; a row whose bytes did not change is not rewritten, and `message_secrets` reassembles them. A received post then writes 3.3–3.5 KB at 32 members and 5.5–5.7 KB at 100 **whatever the retention**, instead of the whole store (40 KB … 3.4 MB); the reassembled store is byte-identical to the unsplit one (V10-12's replay with 16 retained epochs: 295 of 295 states) and every late-post path behaves alike (V10-5). It reads OpenMLS's serde layout (`past_epoch_trees[].epoch`): G-M7's golden `MessageSecrets` covers it, and a missing field is `STATE_CORRUPT`. **What it does not save:** CPU. OpenMLS hands the adapter the whole store at every write and reads it whole at every load, so every call still (de)serialises every retained epoch; the spike's unoptimised split (it re-serialises each retained epoch to compare it with its stored row) took 0.4–2.8 times the unsplit call's median time (1.5–2.8 times at 100 members). The CPU lever is the retention cap (§3.5: 16), the I/O lever the split; S2 measures its own implementation of both.
9. **Batched calls (S0-17).** A batched call (`receive` with several messages, `trialCount`) checkpoints `GhostKv`'s map before each item and restores it when that item fails, so a failed item leaves no trace (point 4's rule, per item); the change-set is the net of the successful items, applied in one transaction with the claims of all of them. A batch that ends in `INTERNAL` or a native crash is retried item by item, so a poison blob is isolated (F9 §7.8's rule).
7. **Storage format stability.** OpenMLS 0.9.0 changed its serde encoding relative to 0.8.1 and offers `0-8-1-storage-format` and `migration-import`; GHOST starts on 0.9.0 and never enables them. Every pin bump runs golden-state upgrade tests (§3.8, G-M7).

### 3.5 Configuration (normative)

| Setting | Value | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Ciphersuite | 0x0003 | D3 |
| Wire format policy | `PURE_CIPHERTEXT_WIRE_FORMAT_POLICY` (the default, `config.rs:642-645`) | handshake and application messages encrypted |
| `padding_size` | 0 | the outer seal pads to the bucket |
| `use_ratchet_tree_extension` | true (the Welcome carries the tree) | the joiner has no other source; measured sizes in §5.7 |
| Sender ratchet | `SenderRatchetConfiguration::new(16, 1000)` | out-of-order tolerance 16 generations; peek ordering by the author-set `(sender_leaf, seq)` of the MLS AAD (§6.3.3, §19.18) makes larger tolerance unnecessary for honest reordering; a relay that withholds part of a burst of more than 16 posts from one sender past the 12-minute hold still pushes the withheld ones out of range (a declared loss, §6.3.6) |
| Past epochs | `max_past_epochs(16)` as a hard cap (S0-6; round 10c had 64, at which a received post rewrote up to 3.4 MB at 100 members, V10-5); count-based deletion by GHOST at SWEEP: `delete_past_epoch_secrets(PastEpochDeletion::before_timestamp(UNIX_EPOCH).max_past_epochs(n))`, n computed by Kotlin from `ch_epoch.final_day` (`om:src/group/mls_group/config.rs:153,180`; `past_secrets.rs:304-324`) (§8.3, §19.4); past epochs stored one row each (§3.4 point 8) | late posts decrypt; bounded FS loss; bounded state and per-call parsing |
| KeyPackage lifetime | `Lifetime::init(day_start(day) − 4 · 86 400, day_start(day) + 80 · 86 400)` with the Kotlin-supplied `day` (`om:src/key_packages/lifetime.rs:79`; 84 days) (§19.4). OpenMLS never checks a lifetime's span (`Lifetime::has_acceptable_range` is called nowhere; a 100-year KeyPackage is accepted, V10-11), so GHOST enforces the exact form: the canonical KeyPackage of §6.2.4 (B), checked by the admitting committer and by every member in CO-2 (A) (S0-12) | no second-level clock in state; a member whose system clock is up to 4 days behind the creator's trusted day, or up to 66 days ahead of it at an admission within 14 days, accepts it (Phase 7: every honest clock within σ = 3 d of true time, F7:58, so two honest clocks differ by up to 6 days and a member at that extreme can be `clock_blocked` for up to 2 days, L8-C20) |
| Leaf lifetime validation | **received Adds:** OpenMLS always checks the KeyPackage lifetime against the device's `SystemTime::now()` (`key_package_in.rs:195-197`, `public_group/validation.rs:405,882-887`; only a crate-internal test flag switches it off, `skip_validation.rs`), before the update path, the proposal set and every GHOST check, so its refusal masks every other defect of the commit (V10-11). GHOST applies **one predicate on the member's trusted day on both branches** (CO-2, S0-12): a KeyPackage the trusted day does not accept makes the commit invalid whatever OpenMLS answered; a device-clock refusal of a KeyPackage the trusted day accepts makes the member `clock_blocked`, never a different choice. **Joins:** `JoinBuilder::skip_lifetime_validation()` (`creation.rs:1313`), and GHOST checks every leaf lifetime for day alignment and span 84 d; an expired never-updated leaf is eviction work for committers, not a join failure (V10-11: the default `JoinBuilder` refuses such a tree) | a never-updated idle leaf past its lifetime would otherwise make every later Welcome fail for the whole channel (`public_group/mod.rs:302-309`) |
| External commits | refused: GHOST never publishes a GroupInfo, and a commit whose sender is external is dropped before `process_message` (§6.4 CO-1) | RFC 9750 §5.3; the sponsor model |
| Credentials | `BasicCredential` whose identity is the 32-byte pseudonym public key, equal to the leaf's signature key; **continuity** (RFC 9420 §5.3.1, left to the application by OpenMLS, `public_group/validation.rs:890-895`): every leaf a commit installs keeps the pre-commit signature key and credential of its member, checked by CO-2; GHOST never calls `CommitBuilder::build_with_new_signer` (`commit_builder.rs:621`) (§19.14) | D12 |
| Capabilities | exactly the canonical capabilities of §6.2.4 (B) in every leaf — versions [mls10], suites [0x0003], extensions [0xF0A1 (context), 0xF0A2 (leaf: box key and refresh epoch), 0xF0A4 (KeyPackage binding)], proposals [], credentials [Basic], no GREASE (S0-4, S0-5); `RequiredCapabilities` of the group names 0xF0A1 and 0xF0A2. OpenMLS's `KeyPackageBuilder` builds a KeyPackage whose leaf omits 0xF0A4 without complaint and its `KeyPackageIn::validate` refuses it (`UnsupportedExtension`, V10-13), so `keyPackage` must list it | §5.3, §5.4, §19.3, §19.5 |
| Exported GroupInfo | never built: every commit calls `CommitBuilder::create_group_info(false)` after `create_group_info_with_extensions` (with `use_ratchet_tree_extension(true)` OpenMLS otherwise builds one, with an `ExternalPub` key, for **every** commit, V10-8) (S0-9) | no published GroupInfo (D1, Q17) |

### 3.6 Clocks, logs and threads in the OpenMLS graph (normative)

- **Clock sites** (verified by grep of the 0.9.0 sources): `past_secrets.rs:80,105` (stamps when an epoch becomes current, rewritten to the call's Kotlin day, §3.4), `past_secrets.rs:234,254` (time-based deletion with `older_than_duration`, never called by GHOST), `past_secrets.rs:271-279` (`before_timestamp`, called only with `UNIX_EPOCH` so that only the count applies, §8.3), `key_packages/lifetime.rs:66` (`Lifetime::new`, never called: GHOST uses `Lifetime::init`), `lifetime.rs:88` (validation against the device's system clock: reached for every received Add and, unless skipped, for every tree leaf at a join; the JVM worlds cannot move it). **Measured (V10-11):** KeyPackages made on host − 79 … host + 4 are accepted at the host clock; a received Add whose KeyPackage has expired is refused with `ValidationError(KeyPackageVerifyError(LifetimeError(Expired …)))`, and that refusal comes after the framed-content signature but **before** the update path, the proposal set and everything GHOST checks, so it masks every other defect of the commit. GHOST therefore never lets this site decide validity: validity follows the trusted-day predicate of CO-2 (S0-12), and this site only makes a member `clock_blocked` while its trusted day still accepts the KeyPackage. Gate **G-M8** (the G-R8 method of F9 §16.1) scans the compiled library code of the `ghost-client-mls` graph and compares the sites with a committed, justified baseline.
- **Logging.** `log` 0.4 is a hard dependency of OpenMLS; my pattern count over the non-test sources on 2026-09-25 is 200 matching lines, different from earlier hand counts (135, 190, 229; F9 RX-12), which is why the gate counts mechanically at the pin. No crate of the graph installs a logger (`log::set_logger` is never called), so every call is a no-op; `crypto-debug` and `content-debug` are forbidden (§3.2). Gate **G-M4** extends F9's G-R4 rule to the MLS graph.
- **Threads.** `rayon` is a normal dependency (`treesync/treekem.rs`, `treesync/node/parent_node.rs`). `ghost-client-mls` builds the global pool once at library load with **one worker** (`ThreadPoolBuilder::new().num_threads(1).build_global()`); a unit test asserts `rayon::current_num_threads() == 1` inside a native call (the F-10 "single-worker check"). One worker is necessary for deterministic test runs (V10-6: with rayon's default pool two runs of one seed give identical posts but different commits, Welcomes and state; with one worker, 98 of 270 draws still happen on the worker thread) but not sufficient: the worker thread draws HPKE randomness from `SysRng`, so determinism needs the process-global seeded stream of §19.10 (S0-7).

### 3.7 Maturity table and the audit scope (normative evidence table)

| Component | Audits and advisories | Deployment | Formal analysis |
|---|---|---|---|
| OpenMLS 0.9.0 | SRLabs v1.2 (S1-3 acknowledged → answered by §3.4; S0-1 risk-accepted → **closed for channels by canonical KeyPackages and leaves**, §6.2.4, CO-2, S0-5: V10-4 showed that the 64 KiB blob limit alone does not bound it — four members inflating their own leaves within the blob limit made every later Welcome of a 100-member channel too large to send); four advisories, all fixed at or before 0.9.0, none in RustSec (§3.1) | Wire and XMTP forks, Air | RFC 9420's analyses are of the protocol (TreeKEM's DY* proof covers the authors' executable specification, not OpenMLS; eprint 2025/410) |
| libcrux provider 0.4.0, `hpke-rs` 0.7.0 | outside SRLabs's scope. **Every advisory on a crate of the stack, all fixed at the locked versions (V10-2, S0-2; RustSec `593df8c1` of 2026-09-24):** `hpke-rs` RUSTSEC-2026-0069/0070/0071 (= GHSA-g433-pq76-6cmf, < 0.6.0; pin 0.7.0); `libcrux-ed25519` RUSTSEC-2026-0026 (= GHSA-435g-fcv3-8j26, < 0.0.6) and RUSTSEC-2026-0075 (< 0.0.7; pin 0.0.9); `libcrux-ecdh` RUSTSEC-2026-0023 (= GHSA-435g: X25519, the key exchange of suite 0x0003; < 0.0.6; locked 0.0.8); `libcrux-chacha20poly1305` RUSTSEC-2026-0124 (< 0.0.8; pin 0.0.9); `libcrux-poly1305` RUSTSEC-2026-0073 (< 0.0.5; pin 0.0.6); `libcrux-sha3` RUSTSEC-2026-0074, -0207, -0208 (< 0.0.10; locked 0.0.10); `libcrux-secrets` RUSTSEC-2026-0212 (< 0.0.6; locked 0.0.6); `libcrux-intrinsics` RUSTSEC-2025-0133 (< 0.0.4; locked 0.0.8); RUSTSEC-2026-0209 and -0211 (AES-GCM AAD limits, non-constant-time tag check), filed under `libcrux-aesgcm`, the **old name** of `libcrux-aes`, fixed in `libcrux-aes` 0.0.9 (the pin) — a RustSec entry under a renamed crate is invisible to `cargo deny` and `cargo audit`, the same blind spot as OpenMLS's; `crossbeam-epoch` RUSTSEC-2026-0204 (< 0.9.20; pin 0.9.21). The upstream repositories moved to `celabshq/libcrux` and `celabshq/hpke-rs` | OpenMLS users | libcrux primitives partly verified (hax/F*); HPKE glue not |
| GHOST seal, generation derivations, commit arbitration, box-key HPKE, storage adapter, JNI | none yet | none | EM-3 (state model, §12.3); no ProVerif model in Phase 10 (Q23) |

The external audit of plan row 15 names "OpenMLS"; its scope for GHOST is: the pinned OpenMLS and provider revisions **as GHOST configures them**, `ghost-client-mls` in full, the Kotlin channel engine and schema, the generation and credential design, and the commit arbitration. It is a release gate, not a Phase 10 gate.

### 3.8 Size, build and bump policy (normative)

- **Size (measured in S0, V10-3, S0-3; normative).** With `scripts/build-native.sh`'s recipe (cargo-ndk 4.1.2, NDK 30, release, `strip = "symbols"`, path remapping, portable libcrux) on the committed tree of S0 (`b05fd85`), OpenMLS 0.9.0 with the libcrux provider, `GhostKv` and every OpenMLS call `ghost-client-mls` makes grow the stripped `libghost_client_net.so` by **+2 488 200 B on arm64-v8a** (17 931 600 → 20 419 800) and **+2 916 384 B on x86_64** (19 519 400 → 22 435 784); the PQ feature would add 16 792 / 37 040 B more. About 0.42 / 0.53 MB of it is KEM code suite 0x0003 never runs (P-256 and ML-KEM-512/1024, linked because `hpke-rs-libcrux` dispatches over every KEM at run time; Q16); `openmls_memory_storage` is dropped by the linker. **Budget: ≤ +3 MiB (3 145 728 B) per ABI, enforced per ABI.** It holds, but x86_64 has only **229 344 B (7.3 %)** of headroom, and the measured probe lacks most of `ghost-client-mls`'s own code (the 20 JNI functions, the seal, the codecs, the reveal): **S2's exit re-measures with the real crate by the V10-3 method and fails above the budget on either ABI**, and every OpenMLS bump re-measures. `apk-size:` reports the library per ABI.
