- **Rotation rule (revised §19.7, §19.16).** A merged commit opens generation g + 1 iff it removes a pseudonym that it does not re-add (Remove, leave, eviction), carries a **restore** swap (its `SwapEvidence` kind 2: the replaced leaf belonged to a lost, possibly stolen device, which holds `G_g`, the READ and DEPOSIT secrets and `JK_g` — LIMITE:39 treats it as revoked), changes `size_class`, or carries the **rotation flag**; a **rejoin** swap (kind 1, fork repair: the old leaf's state is on the same device) does not rotate. The flag is a bit of the commit's `GhostCommitAad` (committers only, CO-2). A committer sets the flag at a pass when an invite issued in g has expired unconsumed (its holders no longer need the generation's secrets) or g is 28 days old, and no generation of the channel is draining (Q28). Every member computes the rule from the commit alone; joiners learn `(g, G_g, E_g)` from the bootstrap.
- **At the merge (`CH-SETTLE`):** derive `G_{g+1}` from the new epoch and store `ch_generation(g + 1, pending_switch, switch_hour)`, with `switch_hour = ceil_hour(commit_minute + 30 min)` from the commit's `GhostCommitAad` (clamped to at most the settle + 2 h). **The switch is `CH-SWITCH` (§19.8), never the settle**: at the first listing step at or after `switch_hour`, or at the next session start if the member settled after `switch_hour`, it registers `ns_{g+1}` with its relay set, installs its READ and DEPOSIT credentials for every slot, sets `ch_generation(g + 1, current)` and `ch_generation(g, draining, drain_until_day = day(switch_hour) + 14)` — the day of the commit's switch hour, so a member that switches late (or a removed member, which computes it from the commit that removed it) ends the drain on the same day as everyone. A member's first listing of `ns_{g+1}` therefore follows the commit's hour, not the minute it fetched the commit. Posts composed between the settle and the switch wait as plaintext (§6.3.1); ops already enqueued for `ns_g` complete there.
- **At most two generations listened per channel.** A switch while a generation is already draining ends the older drain at once (its unfetched posts are a declared loss, §6.3.6), so a channel costs at most 6 read pairs (§7.7).
- **Funding at a rotation:** the member whose commit rotated funds `ns_{g+1}` for the current week at its switch (it acted; the time is its own commit's hour), and the listed funders from their next EXPIRING renewal only — a non-rotating funder creates registration rows for `ns_{g+1}` in its renewal step, never at the settle or the switch (§19.8; otherwise F9 Q31's "redeem as soon as eligible" would make it a reflex, mutant PC-6). Until a registration lands, stores to `ns_{g+1}` wait (`wait_capability`) and complete when it does; if the rotating committer has no eligible token, the new generation waits up to a week for a funder's renewal (declared, L8-C6). Who funds a new generation is Q31.
- **Drain.** Members list `ns_g` until `drain_until_day` (14 days: the 7-day store window plus 2σ of Phase 7's 3-day clock tolerance, rounded up), then `Namespaces.remove(ns_g)` and delete `G_g`, `JK_g` and the generation's credentials. `Namespaces.remove` returns false while the namespace has operations or fetched, unconsumed rows (`ghost/android/sync/.../api/Namespaces.kt:55-61`), so `CH-SWEEP` first marks consumed every fetched row of `ns_g` still deferred (by then unopenable: its epoch keys are past retention) and retries the removal at the next sweep while an op for `ns_g` is still open (§19.23). Funders stop renewing `ns_g`; its registrations lapse at `start(w + 2) + 1 h` (F9 §4.4).
- **What a removed member keeps.** The old generation's namespace and secrets: it **keeps listing `ns_g` on its schedule until `drain_until_day`** like every member (fetching and dropping; §19.8 — stopping at its own merge would be a fetch-time reflex that names it), and sees the volume of stale posts (of epochs it belonged to; the removal hold keeps posts after the removing commit out of its epochs, CO-13) and of join traffic addressed to `ns_g`. It holds nothing of `ns_{g+1}` (P-C4(b)). That it never lists `ns_{g+1}` still singles it out under AD-1 (LC-8).

### 6.6 Leave, remove, idle eviction

- **Leave** (`CH-LEAVE`, user): enqueue a `LEAVE` post (handshake TTL, §5.8), set `state = leaving`, stop posting; when the op resolves (any outcome), delete the MLS state, keep listening until the generation's drain ends (as a removed member, §6.5), keep local posts until their timers. A committer removes the leaver at its next pass (a leave request is pending work).
- **A committer leaves in two steps (§19.13).** A commit cannot remove its own committer (RFC 9420 §12.2; OpenMLS `CannotRemoveSelf`, `om:src/group/mls_group/commit_builder.rs:808-810`) and CO-2 lets a rank remove only higher ranks, so round 10b's owner could never leave. Now: a committer of rank r ≥ 1 first commits its **resignation** (a context change dropping its own key from `committers` and `funders`, CO-2), then leaves as a member; rank 0 first **transfers** rank 0 (a context change reordering `committers` so that another committer is at rank 0 and itself at a higher rank or out), then leaves the same way. The UI refuses a leave by the only committer of a channel with other members until it names a successor (Phase 13); the only member left in a channel leaves by deleting its state (no traffic). Both steps are user actions, not reflexes.
- **Remove** (committer, user action): an immediate pass with the Remove proposals (and the `committers`/`funders` update when needed); the removed member, on merging the commit, sees itself removed (OpenMLS marks the group inactive), sets `state = removed` — or `replaced` when the same commit adds its own pseudonym with a KeyPackage it did not make (§6.7) — deletes the MLS state and keeps listening, fetching and dropping, until the generation's drain ends (§6.5).
- **Idle eviction.** A committer keeps, per member, the day of the member's last leaf refresh (join, rejoin, own self-update or own commit), `ch_member_refresh(channel, pseudonym, day)`; a member idle longer than `idle_evict_days` (default 63 = 2 × 28 + 7, at most 70, §19.4) is removed at the next pass (RFC 9420 §16.6 "Applications SHOULD have mechanisms for evicting group members that are offline for too long"; RFC 9750 §8.2.2). The evicted member may return through a new invite. A committer is evicted only by a lower rank (CO-2), so **an idle owner is never evicted**: if its device state leaked, its stale leaf keeps receiving every later path secret. Whether an absent owner can be succeeded is Q37 (§19.13; default no, declared L8-C16). The protocol review suggested replacing `ch_member_refresh` by the tree's `GhostLeaf.refresh_epoch`; it is **not** adopted: a KeyPackage leaf carries `refresh_epoch = 0`, so the tree does not show when a member that never updated joined, and a committer would still need its own epoch-to-day log (§19.13).

### 6.7 Resync and fork recovery (FR-3.2; TM S3)

| Situation | Detection | Recovery |
|---|---|---|
| Restart | none needed | state is persisted atomically (CS-C1) |
| Missed commits still on relays | blobs that do not open are deferred; the next epoch's commit arrives | in epoch order, within the session: posts, then the winner settled by CO-4 right after the listing pass (the anchors lie in the past), then `Inbox.reofferDeferred` opens the next epoch's blobs (§19.1) |
| Missed commits past their TTL | CO-11: foreign blobs stay unopenable, no progress | `RESYNC_NEEDED` (shown to the user) |
| Fork, this member's branch dead | CO-11 | `RESYNC_NEEDED` |
| Fork, both branches alive | CO-12 `split_suspected` | the member stays; the UI shows the fingerprint for out-of-band comparison and offers `resync` |
| Relay partition that keeps the branches apart (e.g. a removing commit withheld from a subset) | CO-12 `partition_suspected` after 9 days without a committer commit; on the other side, `split_suspected` once the subset's posts outlive the retention of their epoch (≤ 2 d + 72 h) while the old namespace drains | out of band; `resync` (declared, L8-C17) |
| Silent orphan: the member missed a rotating commit that has since expired, and its old namespace drained and went quiet (nothing unopenable arrives, nothing opens) | CO-12 `stale_suspected` after 9 days without a committer commit or any other member's message (§19.15) | out of band; `resync` while the committers still drain its namespace, otherwise `REINVITE_NEEDED` |
| Rejoin not answered within 14 days, or the generation drained | no Welcome | `REINVITE_NEEDED`: any member's new invite admits the pseudonym as a **restore** swap (§6.2.4, §6.12) |
| Own leaf swapped by a KeyPackage this client did not make | a merged commit removes its leaf and adds its pseudonym with a `kp_ref` not in `ch_join` | `REPLACED` (shown: "another device with your pseudonym took your place"); state deleted, posts kept; return by a new invite (restore) (§19.3) |

**Rejoin (`CH-REJOIN`, a user action or the user's next action in the channel; never a reflex; revised §19.3).** Create a fresh KeyPackage for the same pseudonym (purpose rejoin, `GhostKpBind`), sign `rejoin_sig` over (channel_id, the target generation, its trusted day, its last final epoch and that epoch's winner hash, SHA-256 of the KeyPackage) and enqueue a `JoinRequest(type = rejoin)` sealed in mode 0x12, one copy in each generation namespace the member listens to (current and draining; a fork at a rotating commit leaves the two sides with different new generations, and only the older one is shared); keep the old state and the KeyPackage's private keys until a Welcome arrives or 7 days pass, then send a fresh request; `REINVITE_NEEDED` after 14 days. **A committer admits a rejoin** (policy) only if: the pseudonym is a current member of its branch; `rejoin_sig` verifies under that leaf's signature key; `day ∈ [its day − 7, its day + 3]`; the `kp_ref` and the init key were never seen in this channel (`ch_kp_seen`); `GhostKpBind` names this channel and purpose rejoin; the claimed `(last_epoch, last_hash)` is not a point the requester can still catch up from — `last_epoch` < its current epoch and either its epoch log holds a different winner for `last_epoch` (a fork) or the commit that closed `last_epoch` was finalized more than one handshake TTL ago or is older than the log (the requester cannot catch up in-band); and no authenticated message from that pseudonym's current leaf opened on its branch in an epoch after `last_epoch` (a live leaf is never replaced by a rejoin). It swaps with the rejoin evidence in the commit's `GhostCommitAad`, where CO-2 verifies the signature (the old leaf removed and the new KeyPackage added in one commit; no rotation). **Any committer may swap a member of any rank** (§19.13): a committer or the owner on a dead branch rejoins like any member and keeps its rank, since its key is unchanged; round 10b's CO-2 let only a lower rank remove a committer's leaf, so rank 0 could never be readmitted after losing a fork. These rules are GHOST's: OpenMLS's `swap_members` "does not enforce that the removed `members` and new members … correspond" (`om:src/group/mls_group/membership.rs:61-71`), and its key-uniqueness check leaves out the removed leaves (`om:src/group/public_group/validation.rs:206-225`). On the Welcome — accepted only if its GroupInfo is signed by one of the committers of the member's last final epoch (the rejoin's anchor, §6.2.5, S0-15; a rejoiner holds no invite digest, and a committer set that changed on the live branch since the fork can leave a legitimate Welcome unanchored, in which case the member stays in `resync_needed` and returns by a restore invite, whose digest is current) — the member replaces its MLS state (the old group-scoped rows are not passed to `welcomeJoin` and are deleted in the same transaction, §6.2.5), keeps its displayed posts, adopts the bootstrap's generation (switching at its next session start, §19.8) and resumes. OpenMLS's `fork-resolution` helpers are not needed.

**What a replay can still do.** A copied rejoin request re-presents the requester's own KeyPackage, whose private keys the requester keeps for the 7-day window: if admitted, it completes the requester's own rejoin. A join-purpose KeyPackage cannot be re-wrapped as a rejoin (no pseudonym signature) or as a restore (`GhostKpBind.purpose`, checked by CO-2). A holder of the pseudonym seed (a thief, L8-C9) can still rejoin as the member once the member's leaf is quiet; the member then sees `REPLACED`.

**What stays open (declared).** A member on a dead branch loses its posts of that branch for the others; a committer on a dead branch (a backup that committed while partitioned) rejoins like any member; members removed at the end of generation g, who hold `JK_g`, see that a rejoin happened in `ns_g` and the rejoiner's KeyPackage (its pseudonym) during the drain (L8-C5); two colluding members can, in a channel quiet since an epoch's winner, attract a returning member to a fabricated branch that they keep alive (CO-3's successor count excludes a candidate's own committer, not a second colluder), which that member sees as `split_suspected` once the real branch's traffic reaches it (L8-C21).

### 6.8 PCS and self-updates

Committers update their leaf with every commit: an Add-only commit needs no path in RFC 9420 §12.4 (OpenMLS includes one only when a proposal requires it, `om:src/group/mls_group/membership.rs` doc of `add_members`), so GHOST builds every commit with `CommitBuilder::force_self_update(true)` (`commit_builder.rs:287`); its size cost is measured and fits (V10-4, §5.7). Committers therefore refresh at least every 7 days (every day in channels with timers ≤ 1 d, Q36, §19.19), members every 28 days (CO-9). A committer's PCS commit does **not** shut out a seized member device: that device's leaf is still in the tree and receives the new path secrets until the member is removed or replaced; what the cadence bounds is how far back a seized device's current epoch reaches (§19.19). **PCS is scoped (§19.3):** a member whose device state was exposed and then recovered heals at its next self-update or rejoin **only against an attacker who did not obtain the pseudonym seed**; the pseudonym signing key is derived from the root seed and never rotates, so whoever read the seed keeps it, signs rejoins and swaps itself in as the member (L8-C9) — only a new identity heals that. A removed member's state heals nothing for it (it is out). The 28-day cadence is Q9.

### 6.9 Reveal (`CH-REVEAL`, a user action; ADR-04, LIMITE:135)

1. The user chooses a member M. The client builds `RevealPayload = version(1) = 1 ‖ channel_id(32) ‖ P_author(32) ‖ P_M(32) ‖ minute(u32) ‖ card_v2(131) ‖ sig_id(64) ‖ sig_ps(64)` (360 bytes), where `sig_id` is the `ghost1` identity key's Ed25519 signature over `"ghost/v1/ch/reveal-id" ‖ channel_id ‖ P_author ‖ P_M ‖ minute` (made in Rust with the DM context's identity key, the ADR-35 X-21 rule), and `sig_ps` the pseudonym key's signature over `"ghost/v1/ch/reveal-ps" ‖ channel_id ‖ SHA-256(card_v2) ‖ P_M ‖ minute`.
2. HPKE (RFC 9180) base mode, KEM 0x0020 (DHKEM X25519, HKDF-SHA256), KDF 0x0001, AEAD 0x0003 (ChaCha20-Poly1305), through the provider's `hpke_seal` (the HPKE code MLS already uses), `info = "ghost/v1/ch/reveal" ‖ channel_id`, `aad = op_id`, to `box_pk(M)` as M's current leaf publishes it; the result goes in a `REVEAL` post.
3. Every member tries its box key of the post's epoch on each `REVEAL` (one HPKE open per reveal post; `ch_box_key` keeps each box key until the retention of the last epoch whose tree held that leaf ends, §19.17); M verifies both signatures, `P_M` = its own key and `P_author` = the post's MLS sender, and offers "add contact" through `Messaging.addContact(card, CardSource.LINK, …)` — trust `unverified`, the existing source for a card received through another channel (`ghost/android/messaging/.../api/Messaging.kt:350-361`; corrected §19.17: round 10b's `CardSource.CHANNEL_REVEAL` does not exist, `dm_contact.source` has a frozen CHECK, `Schema.kt:928`, and a new value would leave a lasting "came from a channel" mark on the contact).
4. **What members learn:** the author revealed to someone. **What M learns and keeps forever:** the author's DM card and a **transferable** proof that the pseudonym belongs to that identity: `sig_id` and `sig_ps` verify for anyone M shows them to (an operator, a court, other members), and the REVEAL post's MLS signature already proves which pseudonym sent it (L8-C8, the L7-D25 residue in a new form; the deniable alternative is Q35). Nothing crosses the channel otherwise (TB-7).
5. **Forward secrecy of reveals (§19.17).** `box_pk` is fresh in every leaf the member creates (its KeyPackage and each of its update paths) and its private key is deleted when the last epoch holding that leaf leaves retention. A later compromise of M's seed or device therefore opens no REVEAL addressed to an earlier leaf; round 10b derived `b_sk` from the pseudonym seed, so a member who archived REVEAL posts could open every past reveal to M after stealing M's seed. **Retention:** `ch_reveal` rows (sender and recipient side) follow the REVEAL post's timer and carry no time column; the card itself lives on in the DM contact list only if M adds it.

### 6.10 Governance primitives delivered in Phase 10 (D17)

| Primitive | Phase 10 | Later |
|---|---|---|
| Roles (owner, moderators = committers), policy object | yes (§5.3, CO-1, CO-2) | UX in Phase 13 |
| Remove, leave, idle eviction | yes (§6.6) | — |
| Sponsor attribution | yes: `ch_sponsor(member, sponsor, invite_id, day)` from the admitting commit's `GhostCommitAad` (sponsor-signed, so every member, joiners included, records the same attribution; §19.2) | strikes (P1) |
| Threshold function and the S5 simulation | yes: `threshold(n_active, policy)` as a pure function (ADR-13: `max(N, fraction)`, age weighting, sponsor-subtree diversity) and a simulation of Sybil brigades over it (TM S5) | wiring to real flags (Phase 13) |
| Flags, quarantine, strikes, exclusion lists, verified channels | reserved formats only (kind 0x06) | Phase 13 with ADR-13's UX (Q15) |

### 6.11 State machines (each has a JVM test of every legal and illegal transition)

| Machine | States | Driven by |
|---|---|---|
| Channel (member view) | joining → active → {leaving → left, removed, replaced, resync_needed → active (rejoin, or progress resumed, CO-11) or reinvite_needed → active}; joining → join_expired. Flags on `active`: `split_suspected`, `partition_suspected`, `stale_suspected`, `clock_blocked`, `removal_hold` (CO-12, CO-13, §19.4, §19.15) | CH-ACCEPT, CH-JOIN, CH-SETTLE, CH-RECV, CH-LEAVE, CH-REJOIN, SWEEP |
| Role (own) | member ↔ committer (rank r); committer → resigned (a member, CO-2); rank 0 → rank r ≥ 1 by its own transfer; a committer's `leave` is refused until it resigned or transferred (§6.6, §19.13) | CH-PASS (policy), CH-SETTLE |
| Epoch | open → candidates → final (sticky; later commits for it are fork signals, CO-6) | CH-RECV, CH-PASS, CH-SETTLE |
| Own commit | pending → merged (won with a verified copy, or successors seen; Welcomes enqueued) / cleared (lost at a settle point, FAILED, or no copy can remain after deadline + handshake TTL; Welcomes deleted); never cleared merely because the op closed INDETERMINATE (CO-4, §19.13) | CH-PASS, CH-SETTLE, OUTCOME |
| Generation | pending_switch → current → draining → removed | CH-SETTLE (rotation), CH-SWITCH, SWEEP |
| Post (outgoing) | pending_plaintext → queued → sent / degraded / failed / indeterminate; failed → queued (user retry) | CH-POST, OUTCOME, SWEEP |
| Post (incoming) | unread → read → deleted | CH-RECV, DISPLAY, SWEEP |
| Join request (committer) | pending → proposed (own commit pending) → admitted (own commit won; invite consumed, Welcome enqueued) or back to pending (lost) / ignored (expired, consumed, invalid, sponsor removed) | CH-RECV, CH-PASS, CH-SETTLE |
| Invite (sponsor) | issued → expired | CH-INVITE, SWEEP |
| Registration | the F9 §13.5 machine, for funded namespaces | work lane |

### 6.12 Device revocation, restore, wipe (C-6; LIMITE:39)

- **Restore from seed.** Channel state is not backed up (T4), so a restored client has no channel. A member's new invite readmits it as a **restore** (§19.3; the restored device accepts the Welcome only under the invite's committers anchor, S0-15): its KeyPackage has purpose restore, its pseudonym (derived from the `channel_id` in the invite) is a current member, so the committer swaps its leaf with the invite's sponsor-signed evidence in the commit's `GhostCommitAad` (no freshness rule applies: the invite authorizes it; any committer may swap any rank, the owner included, §19.13); the lost device's leaf is gone and **the lost device decrypts no later epoch** (the LIMITE:39 test, in SCN-M20). **The restore rotates the generation** (§6.5, corrected §19.16): round 10b did not, so the lost — possibly stolen — stack kept the current generation's `G_g`, READ and DEPOSIT secrets and `JK_g` for the rest of the generation (listing volume and timing, filling the quota, opening every later joiner's KeyPackage under `JK_g`), and the T38 oracle's "no later generation" clause passed vacuously for the device-swap case. If the lost device is still running, it sees `REPLACED` and, like a removed member, keeps listening to the old namespace until its drain ends.
- **Stolen device.** The thief holds the seed-derived pseudonym key and can rejoin as the member; a committer cannot tell the thief from the owner. Real exclusion is: the owner asks a committer out of band to remove the pseudonym (a rotation) and returns with a new identity (ADR-14's "identitate nouă" after compromise). Declared, L8-C9.
- **Wipe.** Deletes every channel row, `mls_kv` and `mls_global_kv`; no traffic; the pseudonym is evicted after 63 days.

---

## 7. Relay, sync and entitlement integration

### 7.1 Namespaces (normative)

| Namespace | Registered by | `Namespaces.register` | Read | Write |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| current `ns_g` | every member at accept (`CH-ACCEPT`) and at the switch (`CH-SWITCH`, §19.8) | `(ns_g, CHANNEL, set(ns_g, w), listen = true, LOCAL)`; the rotating committer `Funding.set(ns_g, true)` at its switch, other funders in their renewal step | READ credential of the relay's slot | DEPOSIT credential of the relay's slot |
| channel media namespace | reserved for the channel-media slice (§5.10, Q38); not registered in P0 | — | — | — |
| draining `ns_{g−1}` | every member, removed and leaving members included | listening until `drain_until_day`, then `remove`; at most one draining generation per channel (§6.5) | READ | DEPOSIT, only for ops already enqueued |
| invite `ns_g` (invitee) | the invitee at accept | `(ns_g, CHANNEL, set(ns_g, w), listen = true, LOCAL)` | READ | DEPOSIT (its join request) |

### 7.2 Relay sets

`set(ns, w)` is F9 §5.2's public keyed permutation of the week's ES slots (≥ 2 operators, 3 relays, the 6-week union after a slot-table change), used unchanged for channel namespaces (its HMAC label names DM, but the function takes any namespace; the same vectors apply). **Slot moves (declared, L8-C23; §19.22):** a credential names the ES slot, not the relay (F9's L7-D26); after a slot moves to another operator, the operator that left can present the channel's READ and DEPOSIT secrets it was shown at the slot's new relay until the generation rotates — at most 28 days by the rotation flag (Q28), sooner at any removal.

### 7.3 Credentials: Phase 9's relay features, unchanged

READ (`list`, `get`, `check`) and DEPOSIT (`store`, `check`) bearer credentials v3, their verifiers, `RegisterKey` with the `{cap_week, cap_week + 1}` rule, accumulation, `keys.redb` and the deposit ledger (F9 §4.2–§4.6) serve channels without change. `get` with a READ credential returns `uploaded_at` floored to the ISO week and the exact expiry (`relay/crates/node/src/lib.rs:463-469`), which the unread deadline needs. **No new RPC, kind, field or observable** (§7.8).

### 7.4 Funding and token accounting (normative)

- **Who funds.** The policy's `funders` (default: the owner; up to 3). A funder, for every relay r of `set(ns_g, w)`, keeps READ and DEPOSIT registration rows for `week(now)` and `{c, c + 1}` of its usable **registration capability** of week c (F9 §4.8's `REGROWS` rule, generalised to funded namespaces). Registration capabilities are redeemed by the Phase 8 planner at the EXPIRING renewal time (a PRF time in the last 23 h of the week) and, when a registration is pending without one, as soon as an eligible token exists (F9 Q31). They are stored apart from store credentials (`relay_register_capability`, §9) and used **only** by the registration step (mutant PC-10).
- **Tokens.** One ACCESS token per (relay of the set, funded namespace, week) per funder: **3 tokens per week per channel per funder**, plus 3 for the rotating committer at each rotation it performs — removals, restores (§19.16), and the periodic and invite-expiry rotations of §6.5, at most one per 14 days when no removal happens (Q28). A pack gives 16 per slot per week and the client's own DM namespaces take ≤ 2 (F9 §4.8; 3 once Phase 11 adds the media namespace `M_0`, faza11 §5.2). **Worked budget (corrected §19.23):** with a 3-slot ES every namespace's set covers the same three slots, so a funder of the maximum 9 channels spends 9 tokens per slot per week on them, leaving 16 − 9 − 2 = **5** per slot per week (4 with `M_0`) for the rotations it commits; beyond that a rotation waits for next week's tokens (L8-C6). After a slot-table change, each extra union relay costs one more token per funded namespace per week for up to 6 weeks (F9 §21.1). Members that are not funders spend **0** on channels.
- **Offline tolerance.** A funder online in week w registers `{w, w + 1}`; registrations live until `start(w + 2) + 1 h`, so deposits keep working for about two to three weeks after a funder's last renewal (F9 §21.2). Beyond that, stores wait in `wait_capability` and end at their deadlines (L8-C6).
- **Quota.** Every registration credits its capability's quota (256 MiB per week); any member (or invite holder) can exhaust it; the remedy is Remove and rotation (L8-C7).

### 7.5 `:sync` additions (normative; X-8)

```kotlin
/** A namespace this client funds: the planner redeems registration capabilities for it; stores and reads keep using bearer credentials. */
interface Funding {
    fun set(tx: SyncTransaction, namespace: NamespaceId, funded: Boolean)
    fun isFunded(tx: SyncTransaction, namespace: NamespaceId): Boolean
}
```

1. **`Funding`** and table `sync_namespace_fund`; `Capabilities.needed()` reports a `REGISTER` need (a new `CapabilityKind.REGISTER`) for a funded namespace with a pending registration row and no usable registration capability; the planner treats it like WRITE MISSING (redeem as soon as eligible).
2. **`relay_register_capability`** holds redeemed capabilities of funded namespaces; the registration step uses it (else, for Phase 9's owner namespaces, the write capability as today); store, list, get and check never read it.
3. **`SendDelay.OFF` refused for `Consumer.CHANNEL`** (a v6 trigger, the `sync_namespace_dm_delay_*` pattern).
4. No change to the listener and consumer-tail slots: the one messaging actor serves both consumers.
5. `Inbox.defer` is used for unopenable channel blobs (F7's "an MLS message for a future epoch"); deferred rows do not count against `FETCHED_CAP` (`InboxStore.kt:156-165`).
6. **`Inbox.reofferDeferred(tx, namespace)`** (new; §19.1): sets `offer_after_minute = 0` on the namespace's deferred rows so the next `peek` returns them. Today `defer` only moves `offer_after_minute` and `peek` returns only due rows (`InboxStore.kt:332-360`), so a blob of epoch e + 1 deferred with a 6-hour backoff would wait out the backoff after e settles. Called from `CH-SETTLE`, `CH-JOIN` and `CH-SWITCH`; it causes no traffic.
7. **Registration rows of non-rotating funders** are created only by the funder renewal step at the EXPIRING renewal time (§6.5, §19.8); `Funding.set` for a new generation is called by the rotating committer's `CH-SWITCH` and by the renewal step, never by `CH-SETTLE` on a received commit.

### 7.6 `:entitlement` changes

The planner handles `REGISTER` needs as WRITE needs of a REDEEM namespace (the Q29 redeem hold arms for them); NI-K stays identical (its worlds have no channels). The T2 world (Phase 8) gains channel funders (§7.9).

### 7.7 When channel traffic happens (normative; T19, T40)

| Relay-visible event | Trigger | Timing |
|---|---|---|
| list, get | Phase 7 schedule per (relay, namespace) | T19, independent of activity |
| store of a post, reaction, reveal, `INVITE_ISSUED`, `LEAVE` | user action | at the first session where the channel is current, then STANDARD immediately or HIGH after U[0, 10 min] |
| store of a join or rejoin request | user action | as a post |
| store of a commit | committer pass or committer user action | CO-8 |
| store of its Welcomes | the committer's settle of its own winning commit (pass + 20 min + one listing pass) | CO-5, §19.2 |
| store of a self-update | PRF slot every 28 days; a retry at the next session after 24 h if it lost (LC-6) | CO-9 |
| store of a resignation, transfer, rejoin or restore request | user action | §6.6, §6.7, §6.12 |
| check | Phase 7 verification of own copies | Phase 7 |
| redeem, register | funder renewal (PRF, last 23 h of the week); rotating committer at the switch hour of its own commit | F8 §12.4, §6.5 |
| first list of a new generation's namespace | the commit's switch hour, or the next session start for a member that settled later or joined | §6.5, LC-4, LC-5 |
| **anything else, because a blob arrived** | **never** | T40 |

Declared: LC-6 (a pass commits only when work is pending; its Welcomes and a rotating commit's funding follow only if it won; a member's self-update is retried after 24 h only if it lost, CO-9), LC-4 and LC-5 (listening changes after a received rotation or Welcome, at commit-fixed or session-start times), the self-update retry after a lost tie-break (CO-9), and the catch-up-dependent store minute of posts composed offline (LC-7). **NFR-2** (p95 < 8 s): not met by design with the 30-second listing interval (the same reason as NFR-1, LIMITE L2.1 #5); measured in Phase 12 (Q21). **Read pairs (revised §19.8):** each channel costs 3 pairs, and at most 6 while a generation drains (at most one draining generation per channel, §6.5); with `maxReadPairs = 64` (`TrafficPolicy.kt:50`), the budget must hold `6 × channels + DM pairs + drop pairs ≤ 64` even when every channel drains at once — a hostile co-member who leaves every shared channel makes them all drain, and past the cap every pair, the DM inbox included, acts at 1/m of its events (`PairSchedule.kt:17-20,47`), a remotely triggered marker. **9 channels** per client keep that bound (54 pairs, leaving 10 for the DM inbox — 3, up to 6 after a slot-table change — and drops) (the P0 limit, Q4) **outside the six weeks after a slot-table change** (corrected §19.23): during that window readers list the union of the old and new sets (F9 §5.2), up to 6 relays per namespace after one change (more after two changes within six weeks), so a channel costs up to 6 pairs and up to 12 while it drains; 9 channels then take up to 54 pairs with no drain, and a single draining channel adds 6 more, which with the DM inbox's 3 … 6 pairs reaches 63 … 66 — at or past the cap — and a hostile co-member who leaves several shared channels inside that window pushes the client past the cap, where every pair, the DM inbox included, acts at 1/m of its events. The window follows an owner-controlled schedule change and affects every client alike; the residue is declared (L8-C24), and Phase 11's `M_0` (3 more pairs, faza11 §5.2) tightens it further.

### 7.8 Observables (T1)

No change to `allowed-observables.json` v2. Channel traffic uses `store` (hash, bucket, `ttl_bucket_seconds`, minute, the DEPOSIT `capability_scope`), `list`/`get`/`check` (the READ scope), and the funders' `redeem` and `register` (`key_kind` read or deposit). A new observable would be a privacy decision (INV:66); none is needed.

### 7.9 T2 world and gate (as F9 §4.9)

`t2-exit-gate` gains funders: world clients that own a channel fund it at the renewal time and at rotations they commit; the population line gains `channel_funded_namespaces`; the export is unchanged (no relay table is added). NI-1, NI-2, NI-3 and NI-K are unchanged in rule: no issuer input reaches channel events. **Ownership (corrected §19.23):** this is Rust work in `ghost/issuer/crates/service/tests/t2/world.rs` and belongs to **slice S6** (funding), sized there. **Dependency:** the nightly sweep of the T2 gate has never been green (NI-1d pairs 2 and 3, NI-1 across cells on pair 9; F9 §22.24, §22.28); Phase 10 adds no new failure mode but cannot make T2 green. A green T2 sweep is **not** a Phase 10 exit item; the channel funders' T2 run is reported with the sweep's existing state (Q39).

---

## 8. Ephemeral posts and the history policy

### 8.1 Timer, TTL, deadlines

The channel timer is `GhostChannelContext.timer_code` (set by the owner; default 7 d, ADR-27 point 7); a change is a GroupContextExtensions commit from rank 0 that every member processes and the UI shows ("the timer is itself a message shown to everyone", ADR-27 point 8). TTLs and deadlines: §5.8.

### 8.2 Countdown and deletion (ADR-32 points 4–7, applied to posts)

- The author's countdown starts at the commit of the `CH-POST` transaction that encrypted the post; a reader's at first display (`markDisplayed`).
- An unread post is deleted at `min(relay expiry E, consume + bucket + 1 h)`, E from `InboundBlob.relayExpiryHour`; deadlines only move earlier; the expiry clock never runs backwards.
- The cascade deletes, in one transaction: the post row, its reactions, its `ch_pending_post` row, the notification content, and every quote rendering (replies keep their own text; a `reply_to` that no longer resolves renders as expired); then `wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` with `busy = 0` checked (the F9 `Checkpoint.truncate()`).
- Sweeps run where DM sweeps run (open, foreground sessions, the consumer tail, the in-process timer, the one inexact alarm of ADR-32); every channel read query filters expired rows (the F9 `DmExpiredRowLint` extended to `ch_*`).

### 8.3 Epoch-secret retention (the second meaning of "history policy"; spec §7.3, TM:83)

- Past epoch secrets (OpenMLS) and epoch keys `K_ep(e)` (GHOST) are kept until `final_day(e) + R` — `final_day(e)` being the day epoch e became past at this member (`ch_epoch.final_day`) — with `R = min(2 d, 2 × post_bucket + 1 h)` rounded up to whole days (1 d for 5 min, 1 h and 1 d channels; 2 d otherwise), and never more than 16 past epochs (`max_past_epochs(16)`, S0-6; typically 2–8 epochs pass in 2 days). **Deletion is by count (§19.4):** SWEEP computes n = the number of past epochs whose `final_day + R ≥ today` (Kotlin's trusted day) and calls `delete_past_epoch_secrets(PastEpochDeletion::before_timestamp(UNIX_EPOCH).max_past_epochs(n))`, which drains the oldest past epochs beyond n (`past_secrets.rs:304-324`) and never consults OpenMLS's stamps; `K_ep` rows follow the same n. (Round 10a's `before_timestamp(day)` would have deleted every past epoch whenever the current epoch started before the cutoff, `past_secrets.rs:271-279`.)
- Why short: posts are encrypted at session (§6.3.1), so a post of epoch e reaches the relays within the send delay unless a relay outage delays its store; a longer retention only widens what a seized device decrypts (AD-8: relay-retained or recorded ciphertext of retained epochs, including posts the device never fetched; L8-C10).
- **Retention bounds past epochs only (corrected §19.19).** A seized device also holds the **current** epoch, whose sender ratchets derive forward up to 1 000 generations per sender (`sender_ratchet.rs:274-315`): every message of the current epoch the device has not consumed opens, from the epoch's start, whether fetched from an honest relay within its TTL or from a relay operator's recording (AD-1). The current epoch lasts until the next commit: at most about 7 days while a committer is active (the weekly PCS commit), about 1 day in channels with timers ≤ 1 d under Q36, and without limit while no committer commits (members' self-updates every 28 days shorten it in busy channels). Round 10b's "≤ 2 days" in L8-C10 was therefore wrong; in short-timer channels it also exceeded ADR-27's promise that a seized device holds "cel mult ultima fereastră de timp" of a conversation for anyone who recorded the relays.
- Generation secrets live while the generation is current or draining (§6.5).

### 8.4 History policy "none" (FR-3.8; D11)

A joiner holds no key of an epoch before its join epoch, so it opens no earlier post or commit (P-C5); it can list the namespace and see earlier blobs' timing and volume (metadata only, L8-C3). **It is not "no history" in every sense (corrected §19.22):** the joiner holds `JK_g` from its invite and opens the generation's earlier **join traffic** (other joiners' join requests: their KeyPackages, pseudonyms and invite ids, and Welcome blobs' outer layer), and it learns the generation number (how many rotations — removals among them — the channel has had), the epoch number and the tree's blank leaves. The UI therefore says "no content from before you joined", not "no history" (Q11's Romanian text is corrected). The policy byte is 0; values 1 and 2 are reserved and refused until an ADR defines a history transfer.

### 8.5 T25 for channels (cases; the EP suite of F9 §15.5 over channels)

Every timer × {displayed, unread with honest E, unread with short E, pending plaintext, post of a removed member, post on a losing fork branch, post arriving after its deadline}; a timer change mid-conversation (older posts keep their own timer, clamped by §5.5); a clock step back; a busy reader during checkpoint; the physical scan of the database and the WAL for a plaintext canary after expiry; T1 on the relay capture: the TTL is the bucket of §5.8 and nothing finer.

**Rows derived from posts (added §19.21).** Every row a post creates is either deleted with the post's timer or declared as membership metadata that outlives it:

| Row | Source | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `ch_reaction` | REACTION | cascades with its target post (ADR-32) |
| `ch_reveal` (both sides) | REVEAL | deleted with the REVEAL post's timer; no time column |
| `ch_invite_issued` | `INVITE_ISSUED` (an administrative post, handshake TTL, §5.8) | **declared** membership metadata: kept until `expiry_day + 1` (≤ 8 days), because committers admit up to the invite's expiry; it holds ids, keys and a signature, no user text (L8-C12) |
| `ch_request(leave)` | `LEAVE` (administrative) | **declared**: kept until the leave is served or `until_day` |
| `ch_sponsor` | the admitting commit's `GhostCommitAad` (not a post) | **declared** membership metadata for ADR-13 attribution: kept while the member is in the channel, deleted at its removal; its `admitted_day` feeds ADR-13's age weighting and is declared with it (T33, L8-C12) |

The EP-C suite runs these rows under every timer (a 5-minute channel's REVEAL row is gone after 5 minutes; its `ch_invite_issued` row after the invite's expiry); mutant ME-C5 (a derived row outliving its rule) is killed by EP-C and the physical scan.

---

## 9. Storage schema: migrations 6 and 7 (normative)

`CURRENT_VERSION` is 5 (`Schema.kt:120`). Phase 10 adds **migration 6** (the `:sync` half, slice S1) and **migration 7** (the channel tables, slice S3), each one transaction, append-only and fail-closed, following the v4/v5 split of F9 §13.6: explicit column lists, `typeof` CHECKs on integer columns, write-once and state-machine triggers, REPLACE guards (`replaceGuard`, `Schema.kt:1255-1258`), every time column `*_minute`, `*_hour` or `*_day` with its CHECK (T20, T33), no FTS (G-DM6 extended), no counters per channel except the bounded liveness counters of CO-11, no activity records except those declared (corrected §19.21; L8-C12): the committer's refresh days and last-activity epochs per member (§6.6, §6.7), its own last commit day (`ch_schedule.last_own_commit_day`, CO-8's PCS cadence), the liveness window's start minute and the last committer-commit day (CO-11, CO-12), the sponsor records' admission day (ADR-13 age weighting), and invite records until their expiry. `ch_epoch.anchor_minute` is cleared when the epoch becomes final, and `ch_reveal` has no time column but its deletion deadline.

**Migration 6 (`:sync`).**

| Object | Definition |
|---|---|
| `sync_namespace_fund` | `namespace_id BLOB PRIMARY KEY REFERENCES sync_namespace(namespace_id) ON DELETE CASCADE`, `WITHOUT ROWID`: presence = this client funds the namespace (§7.5). A side table because `sync_namespace.capability_source` has a frozen CHECK (`'redeem', 'local'`, `Schema.kt:852-853`) that SQLite cannot alter without rebuilding a referenced table |
| `relay_register_capability` | `(relay_id, namespace_id)` primary key, `token` (1..512 bytes), `expires_hour` (`% 3600 = 0`), `state IN ('usable','rejected','exhausted')`, `generation ≥ 1`; cascades from `relay_directory` and `sync_namespace`; `WITHOUT ROWID`; the `relay_capability` columns of v2 verbatim |
| triggers `sync_namespace_channel_delay_{insert,update}` | refuse `send_delay = 'off'` for `consumer = 'channel'` (the v4 DM triggers' text) |

**Migration 7 (channels).** Step 0: a guard table with `CHECK (row_count = 0)` fed by `count(*)` of each v1 table `channels`, `channel_pseudonyms`, `mls_state`, `posts` (no production code writes them: `ChannelPseudonymRepository` (`Repositories.kt:43-62`) has no caller outside tests, and its rows need a `channels` row that nothing writes; the guard fails closed if any row exists anyway; S3 deletes the repository and its tests), then `DROP INDEX idx_posts_channel` and the four `DROP TABLE`s. `media` and `media_blobs` stay for Phase 11. Step 1: the tables below. Secret-bearing columns are `*_env` (wrapped under the channel state key, §10.4); plaintext content columns are wrapped under the content key (F9 §13.8).

| Table | Key columns (all others NOT NULL unless marked) | Time columns | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ch_channel` | `channel_id` (32) PK; `state` ∈ {joining, active, leaving, left, removed, replaced, resync_needed, reinvite_needed, join_expired}; flags `split_suspected`, `partition_suspected`, `stale_suspected`, `clock_blocked` (0/1 each); `suite` = 3; `generation`; `epoch`; `joined_epoch`; `size_class` ∈ {0,1}; `timer_code` 0..5; `my_pseudonym` (32); `name_env` NULL | `last_committer_commit_day` NULL | the MLS state is in `mls_kv`, not here |
| `ch_liveness` | `channel_id` PK; `unopenable_count` 0..255; `unopenable_operators` (bitmask of operator slots); `sessions` 0..255; `progress_env` (the distinct progress senders, wrapped) | `unopenable_first_minute` NULL | CO-11, CO-12; reset at progress |
| `ch_generation` | (`channel_id`, `generation`) PK; `namespace_id` (32) UNIQUE; `state` ∈ {pending_switch, current, draining}; `g_env`, `jk_env`; `r_join` (32, a recognition key) | `switch_hour` NULL, `drain_until_day` NULL | at most one `current` and one `draining` per channel (triggers) |
| `ch_epoch` | (`channel_id`, `epoch`) PK; `state` ∈ {open, final}; `k_env`; `r_ep` (32); `winner_hash` (32) NULL; `own_seq` ≥ 0 (the `seq` of §5.6 for own posts) | `anchor_minute` NULL (a trigger requires it NULL once `state = final`, §19.21), `final_day` NULL, `keep_until_day` NULL | ≤ 17 rows per channel (trigger; the current epoch and 16 past, S0-6) |
| `ch_epoch_log` | (`channel_id`, `epoch`) PK; `winner_hash` (32) | `final_day` | ≤ 1 024 per channel; the rejoin fork check (§6.7) |
| `ch_candidate` | (`channel_id`, `epoch`, `hash`) PK; `sender` (32); `rank` 0..255; `own` 0/1; `verified` 0/1 (own commit: a copy verified, CO-4); `lifetime_pending` 0/1 (OpenMLS refused a KeyPackage lifetime the trusted day accepts, CO-2, S0-12); `body_env`; `welcome_env` NULL (own commit only: its sealed WELCOME blobs, enqueued at settle if it wins); `box_env` NULL (own commit only: the new leaf's box key, moved to `ch_box_key` if it wins) | `first_seen_minute`, `store_hour` NULL | ≤ 8 per epoch plus the own commit; one per sender; deleted when their epoch settles (the own commit also when CO-4 clears it) |
| `ch_fork_signal` | (`channel_id`, `epoch`, `hash`) PK | `first_seen_day` | ≤ 16 per channel; diagnostics only (CO-6) |
| `mls_kv` | (`channel_id`, `k`) PK; `v_env` | — | an ordinary rowid table, **not** `WITHOUT ROWID` (S0-6: its values are 0.1–125 KB — the tree alone 41–125 KB, a past epoch ≈ 18.5–53 KB, V10-5 — and SQLite's rule of thumb keeps WITHOUT ROWID rows under about 1/20 of a page, ≈ 200 bytes at 4 KiB, sqlite.org/withoutrowid.html §4); written only from change-sets; ≤ 11 + 16 rows per channel (§3.4) |
| `mls_global_kv` | `k` PK; `v_env`; `channel_id` (32), the channel the row was written for (§3.4) | `delete_after_day` NULL | KeyPackage and key-pair rows; deleted with the channel or by day |
| `ch_post` | `op_id` (16) PK; `channel_id`; `direction` ∈ {out, in}; `author` (32); `kind` ∈ {1, 2}; `reply_to` (16) NULL; `timer_code`; `epoch`; `body_env`; `state` ∈ {queued, sent, degraded, failed, indeterminate, unread, read} | `author_minute` (`% 60 = 0`), `displayed_minute` NULL, `delete_at_minute` NULL, `relay_expiry_hour` NULL | the expiry filter in every read query |
| `ch_reaction` | (`target`, `author`) PK; `channel_id`; `grapheme_env` | — | cascades from `ch_post` |
| `ch_pending_post` | `op_id` PK; `channel_id`; `kind`; `content_env` | `fail_after_hour` | encrypt-at-session queue (§6.3.1) |
| `ch_outbound_op` | `operation_id` PK; `channel_id`; `purpose` ∈ {post, commit, welcome, join, rejoin, request}; `ref` NULL | — | maps Phase 7 ops to channel objects |
| `ch_join` | (`channel_id`, `kp_ref`) PK; `type` ∈ {join, rejoin, restore}; `invite_id` (16) NULL; `generation` | `expiry_day` | invitee, rejoiner or restorer side; the KeyPackage's private keys live in `mls_global_kv` until `expiry_day` (7 days for a rejoin) |
| `ch_join_request` | (`channel_id`, `hash`) PK; `generation`; `type` ∈ {join, rejoin, restore}; `invite_id` NULL; `body_env` | `until_day` | committer side; ≤ 64 per channel |
| `ch_invite_issued` | (`channel_id`, `invite_id`) PK; `invite_pk` (32); `sponsor` (32); `sponsor_sig` (64); `committers_digest` (32, S0-15); `consumed` 0/1; `void` 0/1 (sponsor removed, §19.7, or `committers` changed, S0-15) | `expiry_day` | from `INVITE_ISSUED` posts; consumed and void set at settle |
| `ch_kp_seen` | (`channel_id`, `tag`) PK, `tag` = `HMAC(local key, kp_ref ‖ init_key)[0..16]` | `until_day` | committers only; ≤ 1 024 per channel; `WITHOUT ROWID` (§19.3) |
| `ch_member_seen` | (`channel_id`, `member`) PK; `last_epoch` | — | committers only: the last epoch with an authenticated message from the member's current leaf (§6.7; L8-C12) |
| `ch_invite_own` | `invite_id` PK; `channel_id`; `invite_env` | `expiry_day` | the sponsor's link, for the UI |
| `ch_sponsor` | (`channel_id`, `member`) PK; `sponsor`; `invite_id` | `admitted_day` | ADR-13 attribution |
| `ch_member_refresh` | (`channel_id`, `member`) PK | `refresh_day` | committers only (§6.6) |
| `ch_request` | (`channel_id`, `kind`, `subject`) PK; `kind` ∈ {leave, remove, policy, class} ; `payload_env` NULL | `until_day` | a committer's pending work |
| `ch_schedule` | `channel_id` PK | `next_selfupdate_day`, `last_own_commit_day` NULL, `next_pass_minute` NULL | CO-8, CO-9 |
| `ch_seen` | (`channel_id`, `tag`) PK, `tag` = `HMAC(local key, op_id)[0..16]` | `until_day` | `WITHOUT ROWID`; the `dm_seen` pattern |
| `ch_reveal` | (`channel_id`, `direction`, `peer`) PK | `delete_at_minute` (the REVEAL post's timer deadline; no other time, §19.17) | the card goes to `dm_contact` through `addContact` with source `link` |
| `ch_box_key` | (`channel_id`, `box_pk`) PK; `sk_env`; `last_epoch` NULL (NULL while the key is in the member's current leaf; set when a new leaf replaces it) | — | the member's own box keys (§5.1, §19.17); deleted by SWEEP when `last_epoch` leaves retention (§8.3) |

`expectedTables` and `expectedTriggers` gain the new names and lose the four v1 tables; `DmSchemaIntrospectionTest`'s rule (every time column checked, no virtual table) extends to `ch_*` and `mls_*` (`ChannelSchemaIntrospectionTest`).

## 10. Modules, the closed JNI surface, the facade (normative)

### 10.1 Module layout

| Module / package | Contents | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| `client-core/mls` (Rust, `ghost-client-mls`; new) | `GhostKv` (§3.4), `PseudonymSigner`, the channel operations over `MlsGroup`, the seal (§5.6), derivations (§5.2), `ChannelContent`, invite and join codecs, the reveal HPKE and the per-leaf box keys, result codes, the JNI export behind `jni-bridge`; no network, async runtime, logger or clock; `rayon` pinned to one worker | `openmls`, `openmls_traits`, `openmls_libcrux_crypto`, `ed25519-dalek` 2.2.0, `hkdf`, `hmac`, `sha2`, `chacha20poly1305`, `serde_json`, `zeroize`; `ghost-client-dm` (identity signing for reveals, the card); Phase 11's `ghost-client-media` only with the channel-media slice (§5.10) |
| `client-core/net` | `ghost_client_mls::export_jni!()` beside the DM export (`net/src/jni_bridge.rs:63` pattern) | `ghost-client-mls` |
| `client-core/dm-host` (`ghost-dm-host`) | also exports `MlsNative` (with a `test-vectors` entry), so JVM tests load one host library; the host build's process-global seeded randomness (§12.1, §19.10) | `ghost-client-mls` |
| `client-core/mls-kat` (`ghost-client-mls-kat`; new, test-only, outside every shipped and host library) | the WG passive-client replay through `GhostKv` (§12.2, V10-12). **What it proves (S0-13):** that the adapter (value semantics, scopes, the day rewrite, the split of past epochs) carries OpenMLS's own processing of independently produced commits without loss; it joins through OpenMLS's `JoinBuilder` with the vectors' configuration, **not** through `welcomeJoin`, whose structural checks and CO-2 (A) the vectors' KeyPackages fail by design (lifetimes `[0, u64::MAX]`, no `GhostLeaf` or `GhostKpBind`) — it must not be wired to GHOST's join path | `ghost-client-mls`; `openmls` with `test-utils` as a dev-dependency. A workspace member (the `dm-host` precedent) adds 13 test-only packages to the lock (measured: `addr2line`, `backtrace`, `cast`, `gimli`, `minicov`, `miniz_oxide`, `object`, `oorandom`, `rustc-demangle`, `wasm-bindgen-futures`, `wasm-bindgen-test`, `-macro`, `-shared`), none in a shipped or host graph; `cargo deny` stays green (S0-1) |
| `:crypto-bridge` `org.ghost.crypto.bridge` | `MlsNative` (§10.2), `MlsResult`, `ChannelCrypto` (typed facade), `ChannelRecognition` (hint matching with recognition keys), `ChannelInvite` codec wrapper; its types are byte arrays and its own value types | `libghost_client_net.so` only — **no project dependency** (corrected §19.23): the module has none today and its build file forbids `:storage` and `:messaging` (`ghost/android/crypto-bridge/build.gradle.kts`), and `:sync` api-exports `:storage` and `:network` (`ghost/android/sync/build.gradle.kts:31-32`), which would put network classes on the bridge's classpath against G-DM1 |
| `:messaging` `…messaging.channel.engine` | the transactions of §10.4 run by the **messaging actor** (the DM actor, renamed in code comments only), `ChannelPeekOrder`, `CommitArbiter` (CO rules), `Generations`, `Funders`, `ChannelSweeper`, the channel consumer steps; the mapping between the bridge's values and `:sync` types (namespace ids, credentials) | `:crypto-bridge`, `:sync`, `:identity` |
| `…messaging.channel.store` | DAOs over `ch_*`, `mls_*` (every read applies the expiry filter) | `:storage`, `:sync` API |
| `…messaging.api` | `Channels` facade (§10.3), value types with redacted `toString` | — |
| `:storage` | migrations 6 and 7 | — |
| `:sync` | §7.5 | — |
| `:entitlement` | §7.6 | `:sync` |
| `:app` | wiring: the actor already holds the listener and the tail; the invite deep link `ghost://channel/` is Phase 13 UI | all |

Rules: `:messaging` and `:crypto-bridge` reference no network class and no coroutine (G-DM1 extended); only `:crypto-bridge` references `MlsNative` (G-M6); typed catches only (`sync-no-catch-all.sh` scope); no protocol logic in Kotlin beyond orchestration (ADR-19): parsing, sealing, derivations and every MLS operation are native.

### 10.2 `MlsNative`: the closed list (gate G-M6)

All functions are `static native`, take byte arrays and ints, return `code(1) ‖ TLV fields`, perform no I/O, read no clock (a `day` or `minute` input is supplied by Kotlin), log nothing, and run under `catch_unwind` with the silent panic hook (`INTERNAL`). `rows` is the TLV array of wrapped `mls_kv`/`mls_global_kv` rows of §3.4; every state-changing function returns a change-set.

| # | Function | Main outputs | Codes besides `OK` |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `ctxOpen(stateKey32)`, `ctxClose(h)` | handle | `CRYPTO_UNAVAILABLE` |
| 2 | `channelOpen(h, channelId, pseudonymSeed32)`, `channelClose(h, channelId)` | P (Kotlin compares P with `:identity`'s value) | `INVALID_KEY` |
| 3 | `groupCreate(h, channelId, policy, day)` | change-set, `K_ep(0)` env, `R_ep(0)`, `G_0` env, generation values (fn 13) | `INVALID_POLICY` |
| 4 | `keyPackage(h, channelId, purpose, day)` | the canonical KeyPackage (§6.2.4 (B): lifetime `[day − 4, day + 80]`, `GhostKpBind`, a fresh `box_pk`, the canonical capabilities with 0xF0A4 listed; 395 bytes), `kp_ref`, global change-set, the new box key wrapped (`box_env`, §19.17) | — |
| 5 | `welcomeJoin(h, channelId, rows, welcomeBlobBody, day, expectedNs, anchor)` (`rows`: for a rejoin or restore, only the KeyPackage's global rows, §6.2.5; `anchor`: the invite's `committers_digest` for a join or restore, the committers of the last final epoch for a rejoin, S0-15) | change-set, context fields, roster, epoch, `K_ep` env, bootstrap (g, `G_g` env) from the GroupInfo, checked against `expectedNs` when its generation matches (§5.4) — through `JoinBuilder::skip_lifetime_validation()`, GHOST's structural leaf-lifetime check (§19.4) and the committers anchor (the GroupInfo signer's key among the anchored committers, §6.2.5); on any failure nothing is returned, so the KeyPackage stays | `NOT_FOR_ME`, `INVALID_WELCOME` (including an unanchored Welcome), `INVALID_BOOTSTRAP`, `INVALID_POLICY`, `STATE_CORRUPT` |
| 6 | `receive(h, channelId, rows, epoch, mlsBytes[], day)` — **batched (S0-17):** up to 32 application messages of one epoch in peek order, or one commit or proposal alone | per message: `APP`: content, sender P, the `ChannelOrder` checked against the MLS sender; or its error code (a failed message is rolled back inside the batch, §3.4 point 9); one change-set for the batch. `COMMIT`: a validation summary (sender and rank inputs, adds, removes, swaps, the parsed and verified `GhostCommitAad` evidence, context change, rotation flag, the update-path leaf's `refresh_epoch`, the canonical-leaf, signature key and credential checks, the KeyPackage lifetime predicate, size, hash, the staged `epoch_authenticator`), **no change-set**; `PROPOSAL`: nothing (dropped unprocessed, §6.3.2); `OWN`: nothing (an `OwnPrivateMessage` result, evidence of nothing, §6.3.2) | `INVALID_COMMIT(reason)`, `LIFETIME_PENDING`, `UNDECRYPTABLE`, `WRONG_EPOCH`, `ORDER_MISMATCH`, `NON_CANONICAL`, `STATE_CORRUPT` |
| 7 | `settle(h, channelId, rows, winnerBytes or OWN, day)` | change-set, new epoch, `K_ep` env, `R_ep`, rotation flag and, if set, `G_{g+1}` env and generation values, the commit's `commit_minute`, roster diff, `epoch_authenticator` (the trial mode of round 10c is replaced by fn 20, S0-17) | `INVALID_COMMIT`, `LIFETIME_PENDING`, `STATE_CORRUPT` |
| 8 | `clearOwn(h, channelId, rows)` | change-set | `STATE_CORRUPT` |
| 9 | `commit(h, channelId, rows, adds[(kp, evidence)], removes[P], swaps[(P, kp, evidence)], contextUpdate?, rotate, selfUpdateOnly, floor, commitMinute, day)` | commit bytes (with `GhostCommitAad` set through `MlsGroup::set_aad`, `om:src/group/mls_group/mod.rs:329`, and the update-path `GhostLeaf.refresh_epoch` and fresh `box_pk` through `leaf_node_parameters`; never `build_with_new_signer`, §19.14), Welcome bytes (kept by Kotlin until settle; the bootstrap through `create_group_info_with_extensions` followed by `create_group_info(false)`, so no exported GroupInfo is built, S0-9), joiners' `kp_ref`s, the items kept and deferred (§5.7 rule, S0-16), the new box key wrapped (`box_env`, kept in the own candidate row until the commit wins), change-set (pending commit) | `NO_FIT`, `INVALID_KEYPACKAGE(index)`, `INVALID_EVIDENCE(index)`, `INVALID_POLICY` |
| 10 | `encrypt(h, channelId, rows, content, seq, day)` (`seq` = `ch_epoch.own_seq`, incremented in the same transaction; the call sets `ChannelOrder` as the message's MLS AAD, §5.5) | MLS bytes, `sender_leaf`, change-set | `PENDING_COMMIT` (only when the own pending commit removes a pseudonym, §6.3.1, §19.13), `STATE_CORRUPT`. The channel-media slice adds `dmHandle` and `mediaKeyEnv` (§5.10); not in P0's list |
| 11 | `seal(h, keyEnv, keyClass, ns, bucket, mode, order, body)` | blob | `NO_FIT` |
| 12 | `open(h, keyEnv, keyClass, ns, mode, blob)` | order, body, and for mode 0x11 the PrivateMessage header's epoch, content type (to classify late candidates, CO-6, and to drop proposals) and, for an application message, its `ChannelOrder` | `NOT_OPEN`, `NON_CANONICAL` (including an `order` that differs from the header epoch or the `ChannelOrder`, CS-S7) |
| 13 | `generation(h, gEnv, slots[])` | `ns`, per-slot READ and DEPOSIT credentials v3 and verifiers, `JK` env, `R_join` | — |
| 14 | `inviteBuild(h, channelId, gEnv, invite fields)`, `inviteParse(bytes)` | invite bytes (with the `committers_digest` of the context's `committers`, S0-15) and `INVITE_ISSUED` fields with `sponsor_sig` / parsed fields | `NON_CANONICAL`, `UNSUPPORTED_SUITE` |
| 15 | `joinRequest(h, invite, kp)`, `rejoinRequest(h, channelId, generation, day, lastEpoch, lastHash, kp)`, `joinVerify(h, channelId, body, invitePk?, memberP?)` | body / KeyPackage, `kp_ref`, type and verified evidence | `BAD_SIGNATURE`, `INVALID_KEYPACKAGE`, `WRONG_PURPOSE` |
| 16 | `revealSeal(h, dmHandle, channelId, recipientP, recipientBoxPk, minute, opId)`, `revealOpen(h, channelId, boxEnv, enc, ct, opId, senderP)` (`boxEnv`: the member's box key of the post's epoch, `ch_box_key`) | TLV values / verified card and bindings | `NOT_OPEN`, `BAD_SIGNATURE` |
| 17 | `contentEncode`, `contentDecode` | canonical `ChannelContent` | `NON_CANONICAL` |
| 18 | `roster(h, channelId, rows)` | members (P, leaf, `box_pk`, `refresh_epoch`), epoch, `epoch_authenticator`, tree hash | `STATE_CORRUPT` |
| 19 | `sweepPast(h, channelId, rows, keepCount)` | change-set (`before_timestamp(UNIX_EPOCH).max_past_epochs(keepCount)`, §8.3) | `STATE_CORRUPT` |
| 20 | `trialCount(h, channelId, rows, candidateBytes or OWN, heldBlobs[≤ 32], day)` (S0-17) | for CO-3: the candidate settled in memory, every held blob tried under the trial state (each from the post-merge checkpoint), the distinct successor senders (other than the candidate's committer and the member) and whether the candidate's own committer has a message among them (abandonment); **nothing written, no change-set** | `INVALID_COMMIT`, `LIFETIME_PENDING`, `STATE_CORRUPT` |

`MlsResultCodesTest` enumerates the codes; G-M7 includes a compile-time exhaustive `match` over OpenMLS's error enums used, so a variant added at a bump fails the build (F9 G-R7(d)).

### 10.3 `Channels` facade for Phase 13 (normative signatures)

```kotlin
interface Channels {
    fun create(name: String, timer: Timer, sizeClass: SizeClass): CreateResult              // CREATED | LIMIT_REACHED | NO_REPLICAS | CRYPTO_UNAVAILABLE
    fun createInvite(channel: ChannelId, expiryDays: Int): InviteResult                    // link + QR bytes | NOT_ALLOWED | LIMIT_REACHED
    fun acceptInvite(link: ByteArray): AcceptResult                                        // PENDING | INVALID | EXPIRED | UNSUPPORTED_SUITE | ALREADY_MEMBER | LIMIT_REACHED
    fun channels(): List<ChannelView>                                                      // id, name, state, role, member count; no times
    fun posts(channel: ChannelId, limit: Int, before: PostCursor?): List<PostView>
    fun post(channel: ChannelId, text: String, replyTo: PostId?): PostResult               // QUEUED | TOO_LARGE | NOT_MEMBER | STORAGE_FULL | CRYPTO_UNAVAILABLE
    fun react(target: PostId, grapheme: String?): PostResult
    fun markDisplayed(ids: List<PostId>)
    fun members(channel: ChannelId): List<MemberView>                                     // pseudonym fingerprint, role, sponsor; no times
    fun remove(channel: ChannelId, member: PseudonymId): Result                            // committers only
    fun leave(channel: ChannelId): Result                                                  // NOT_ALLOWED for a committer until it resigned or transferred (§6.6)
    fun setPolicy(channel: ChannelId, change: PolicyChange): Result                        // owner: timer, name, moderators, funders, class, invites, transfer of rank 0; any committer: resign
    fun reveal(channel: ChannelId, member: PseudonymId): Result                            // after the Phase 13 warning
    fun resync(channel: ChannelId): Result                                                 // rejoin request
    fun fingerprint(channel: ChannelId): Fingerprint                                       // (epoch, 8 bytes of epoch_authenticator), for out-of-band comparison (CO-12)
    fun hide(post: PostId); fun mute(channel: ChannelId, muted: Boolean)                   // FR-3.6: local only
    fun status(): ChannelStatus                                                            // counts only
    fun setListener(listener: ChannelListener?)                                            // content-free change hints
}
```

Every method posts to the messaging actor and returns after its transaction commits; none performs network I/O.

### 10.4 Transactions (normative inventory; each is one SQLCipher transaction, change-sets applied only on OK)

| Name | Trigger | Writes |
|---|---|---|
| `CH-CREATE` | user | channel, generation 0, epoch 0, `mls_kv`, `ch_box_key`, namespace, credentials, funding, registrations |
| `CH-INVITE` | user | `ch_invite_own`, `INVITE_ISSUED` post and op |
| `CH-ACCEPT` | user | `ch_channel(joining)`, `ch_join`, `mls_global_kv`, namespace, credentials, join op |
| `CH-JOIN` | a WELCOME blob | group rows, channel, generation (a newer bootstrap generation as `pending_switch`), epoch; deletes join state; `reofferDeferred`; **no** namespace, outbox, registration, funding or capability call (G-M5) |
| `CH-POST` | user or the channel becoming current | post row, op; or `ch_pending_post` |
| `CH-RECV` | a claimed batch: up to 32 application messages of one epoch in peek order, or one commit, join or Welcome blob alone (S0-17; a batch that fails with `INTERNAL` is retried blob by blob) | post or reaction rows, `mls_kv` change-set for application messages, candidates, join requests, invite records, reveal records, fork signals, liveness counters and flags (CO-11, CO-12); `markConsumed` or `defer` |
| `CH-PASS` | CO-8, CO-9, a committer's user action | pending commit (`mls_kv`), the commit op, `ch_candidate(own)` with the sealed Welcomes wrapped in `welcome_env` (not enqueued) |
| `CH-SETTLE` | time (the CO-4 settle point), outcome of own commit | merge (after one `trialCount` call per candidate when CO-3 needs them; they write nothing, S0-17), invites voided by a change of `committers` (S0-15), epoch rows and log (`anchor_minute` cleared), the own winning commit's Welcome ops and its new box key into `ch_box_key` (the replaced key's `last_epoch` set), consumed and void invites, sponsor, refresh and member-seen rows, the next generation as `pending_switch`, `reofferDeferred` |
| `CH-SWITCH` | the first listing step at or after `switch_hour`, or the next session start (§19.8) | namespaces (register the new, mark the old draining, end an older drain), credentials, the rotating committer's funding; `reofferDeferred` |
| `CH-REJOIN` | user | KeyPackage rows, `ch_join(rejoin)`, the signed rejoin op(s) |
| `CH-REVEAL` | user | reveal post and op, `ch_reveal` |
| `CH-LEAVE` | user | `LEAVE` post and op, state |
| `CH-OUTCOME` | Phase 7 outcomes | post states, own-commit bookkeeping |
| `CH-SWEEP` | the ADR-32 sweep points | expired posts and their derived rows (§8.5), epochs past retention (`sweepPast` with the count of §8.3) and the box keys whose `last_epoch` left it, drained generations (their still-deferred fetched rows marked consumed first, §6.5), expired joins, invites, requests, seen tags, KeyPackage tags, fork signals; an own INDETERMINATE commit past its deadline plus the handshake TTL (CO-4 (c)); checkpoint |

**State key.** `mls_state_key`: 32 random bytes created at the first channel use, wrapped by a non-exportable Keystore key with the database key's authentication policy (the F9 §2.5 `session_state_key` pattern; one key for all channels), held in native memory while unlocked (L7-D19 extended). A restore creates a new one (channel state is lost anyway).

---

## 11. Invariants (new T36–T41; changes to existing ones)

### 11.1 New invariants (Romanian rows for INVARIANTS.md in Appendix B.1)

| ID | Invariant | Verification | Executable from |
|---|---|---|---|
| **T36** | Channel blindness at relays: no relay-visible value is computable from `channel_id`, pseudonym or box keys, KeyPackages, invite ids or MLS headers without a generation, epoch or invite secret; every channel blob is a §5.6 seal; no MLS header byte outside it | derivation vectors; G-M3 (static); JC-1 (every relay-visible value of the world recomputed from public material: 0 matches), JC-2 (header scan of every stored blob for group id, epoch, KeyPackageRef, pseudonym keys: 0); PC-1, PC-7, PC-13 | Phase 10 (S2, S10) |
| **T37** | One scope per kind and uniform shape: every store into a generation namespace at a relay presents that (relay, namespace)'s DEPOSIT credential, every list/get its READ credential, registration capabilities appear only in `register`; in channels with timer 7 d, 30 d or off, every blob has the channel TTL, and every commit has the floor bucket | JC-3, JC-4; NI-CH2; PC-3, PC-4, PC-8, PC-10 | Phase 10 (S10) |
| **T38** | Removal: nothing a removed member held (retained epoch and generation secrets, credentials, KeyPackage keys) opens a blob of an epoch after the removing commit, it holds no secret of any later generation, and no post composed by an honest member after that member first held the removing candidate opens under the removed member's keys (the removal hold, CO-13); the same for the stack a **restore** replaced (§19.16); all for members to which the relays delivered the removing commit (§19.15) | the removed-member seizure oracle over every later blob, its hold variant over every post composed after its author's first sighting of the removing commit, and its restore variant over the replaced stack (every blob of every later generation, not only later epochs); PC-2, PC-14, MM-7, MM-8, MM-29 | Phase 10 (S6, S10) |
| **T39** | Joiner history "none": nothing a joiner holds opens a blob of an epoch before its join epoch; the generation's earlier join traffic (mode 0x12 and the outer layer of 0x13), which it opens with the invite's `JK_g`, is the declared exception (L8-C3, §19.22). **A joiner joins only the channel it was invited to** (S0-15): it accepts only a Welcome whose context carries the committers its invite names and whose GroupInfo one of them signed (a rejoiner: one of the committers of its last final epoch); a refused Welcome writes nothing | the joiner oracle (with that exception counted and reported); PC-2; JN FAKE_WELCOME (a holder of `JK_g` builds a group under the channel's id and Welcomes the joiner: refused, the KeyPackage intact, the real Welcome joins), PC-19 | Phase 10 (S4, S10) |
| **T40** | No reflex in channels: two worlds that differ only in what a client received give identical relay views of that client (stores, redeems, registrations, list schedule, session close times), except the declared LC-6 (pass outcome; Welcomes and a rotating commit's funding after a won commit; a self-update retry after a lost one), LC-4 (a received rotation changes the listened namespaces at the commit's switch hour or the next session start) and LC-5 (a joiner's listening after its Welcome) | NI-CH1 (valid, forged, replayed, join request, commit, rejoin request), NI-CH4 (rotating vs non-rotating commit); G-M5 (static: the enqueue, register, redeem and namespace call sites); PC-5, PC-6, PC-15. Session close times are compared only once the world models circuit timing (§19.10); until then the report names the parts of the view compared | Phase 10 (S5, S11) |
| **T41** | Convergence: members whose system clocks are within the KeyPackage lifetime margin and that hold the same valid candidates and the same held blobs for epoch e when they settle it merge the same commit, and equal `epoch_authenticator`s follow at every epoch; commit validity depends only on the commit, the group state and its authenticated data, **and for KeyPackage lifetimes on the member's trusted day through one predicate on both branches (S0-12), which judges every honest commit alike; members whose trusted days straddle a lifetime bound a malicious committer chose can split (L8-C20)**; a settled epoch is never left because of a later commit; a member on a dead branch reaches `resync_needed` or `reinvite_needed`; branches kept apart by the relays, and a member cut off in silence, are flagged (`split_suspected`, `partition_suspected`, `stale_suspected`) and are not claimed to be detected in-band; every leaf a commit installs keeps its member's signature key and credential and is canonical (S0-5); all of this while at least one relay serving each member delivers every commit within its TTL | EM-3 (with the §19.1 and §19.13 adversaries and the lifetime adversaries of S0-12); SCN-M20 and its FORK and owner-restore variants; SCN-CL, SCN-CM; `oracle_epoch`; MM-2, MM-3, MM-4, MM-16, MM-19 … MM-28, MM-31 … MM-34, MM-36 … MM-40 | Phase 10 (S5, S7) |

### 11.2 Changes to existing invariants

| ID | Change |
|---|---|
| T1 | no schema change; channel traffic fixtures added to `privacy-capture.sh` (stores with deposit scopes of channel shape, registrations by funders) |
| T3 | canaries extended: pseudonym seeds, box keys, `G_g`, `K_ep`, `JK_g`, invite seeds, KeyPackage private keys, post text, channel names; G-M4 over the MLS graph |
| T9 | channels: bucket = §5.7 (floor by class, never by type), bytes uniform (CS-S1) |
| T13 | channels: `author_minute` in `ChannelContent`; KeyPackage lifetimes day-aligned |
| T14 | unchanged (vectors); the "no rotation on re-join" reading of ADR-04 is recorded (X-4) |
| T20 | the new time columns of migrations 6 and 7 join the introspection sets |
| T21 | unchanged: channel namespaces use their circuit; registration capabilities pass through the existing matrix |
| T25 | extended to channels: the §8.5 cases |
| T27 | amended (§19.22): "a namespace that is not its own" becomes "a namespace that is neither its own nor a channel namespace it funds"; "redemptions and registrations do not depend on DM activity" gains "nor on channel activity, except the rotating committer's funding of the generation its own won commit opened (LC-3, LC-6)" |
| T28, T31 | T28's wording covers DMs; T40 is its channel twin. T31 already allows reading "canalele"; an invitee reads the invite's namespace until expiry; T31's "own writes at activity-independent times" gains the same exception as T27 (a funder's renewal is activity-independent; a rotating committer's funding follows its own commit) (§19.22) |
| T33 | extended: channel tables; OpenMLS state holds no sub-day time (day rewrite, day-aligned lifetimes), verified by a state scan over every stored `mls_kv` value (ME-C-series mutants); the activity-time columns it allows are exactly those §9 declares (§19.21), and the introspection test lists them (a new time column fails it) |
