(Round 10b's rule counted 48 h without an owner *commit*, which in a quiet channel made the first moderator active about five days in seven and raced the owner routinely.) | **yes** | Moderatorii preiau cererile lăsate de proprietar 48 h; pot scoate oricând; doar proprietarul schimbă regulile și își poate preda rolul |

Or RFC 9750 §5.2.2's other option: merge at once and keep a copy of the previous group state for a few days so late commits can be judged — at the cost that a device seized in that window re-derives every later epoch, read posts included? | **sticky finality** | O epocă odată acceptată nu se mai schimbă; bifurcațiile se semnalează cu o amprentă de comparat în afara aplicației |

Without a server there is no trusted time: an epoch-count rule can be pumped by a moderator's empty commits, and a commit minute is the committer's claim, so any rule lets a moderator take over an active but quiet owner. | **accept, declared** (L8-C16); the UI urges naming a moderator and transferring before leaving | Dacă proprietarul dispare fără să-și predea rolul, regulile canalului rămân înghețate; fără succesiune automată (ar permite unui moderator să preia canalul) |

The slice also decides the media namespace (a separate one per generation costs about 4 of the 9 channels, §5.10). | **close Phase 10 without it**; the channel-media slice runs after Phase 11's engine, FR-3.4 stays partly open until then, and Phase 11's Q17 text is aligned at merge | Media în canale: formatul e rezervat acum; implementarea după motorul de media al Fazei 11; Faza 10 se închide fără ea |

May S0 start now (evidence only), S1 onwards only once those ADRs are decided or explicitly accepted as the basis, and may Phase 10 close before Phase 9 with every claim marked as inheriting Phase 9's open gate? A green T2 sweep is not a Phase 10 exit item (§7.9). | **yes** (S0 now; S1 after the ADR decision; closure before Phase 9 allowed, marked) | Faza 10 poate începe cu S0 acum; restul după decizia pe ADR-urile Fazei 9; se poate închide înaintea Fazei 9, cu mențiune |
| Q40 | **New (V10-1, S0-1).** OpenMLS 0.9.0 does not resolve into the ghost lock: its optional SQLite provider (which GHOST never enables) needs an older `libsqlite3-sys` than Arti's, and `links = "sqlite3"` must be unique. Accept **(a)** a manifest-only `[patch.crates-io]` stub of that crate (never compiled; removed when upstream relaxes its requirement, F-5); or **(b)** pin `ghost-client-net`'s `libsqlite3-sys` back to 0.35 so Arti uses `rusqlite` 0.37 (Arti 0.46 allows it; measured to resolve), shipping SQLite 3.50.2 instead of 3.51.3 in Arti's directory cache (CVE-2025-7709, an FTS5 defect fixed in 3.50.3, needs control of the database file); or **(c)** wait for upstream? | **(a) the stub** | Un înlocuitor gol, niciodată compilat, pentru stocarea SQLite opțională a OpenMLS, ca să se rezolve dependențele lângă SQLite-ul lui Arti |
| Q41 | **New (V10-8, S0-15).** A joiner accepts only a Welcome whose GroupInfo is signed by one of the committers named in its invite (a 32-byte digest in the invite and in `INVITE_ISSUED`): without it, any holder of the generation's join key (an invite holder, a member removed at the end of the generation) can move a joiner into a group of its own under the channel's id, and the real Welcome then fails (measured). Accept the anchor with its costs — a change of committers voids every outstanding invite, a committer named at the invite's issue (even one removed since) can still capture the joiner until the invite expires (L8-C25, sharpened by §19.27 TR-4: once a committer is **removed**, capture is guaranteed for anyone acting on the voided invite, with recovery `reinvite_needed`), a rejoiner accepts only committers it last knew — or require the sponsor-confirmed admission of Q30 instead (a second online step)? The anchor's digest is now over the committer **set** (TR-6) and is checked on the unverified GroupInfo before the tree is validated (TR-7). | **the anchor** (Q30 stays reserved) | Cel invitat acceptă doar un Welcome semnat de unul dintre moderatorii numiți în invitație; o schimbare a moderatorilor anulează invitațiile în curs |
| Q42 | **New (§19.27 TR-1 … TR-3).** Give the canonical KeyPackage a **constant lifetime `[0, 2⁶⁴ − 1]`** (measured: still 395 bytes, accepted by the committer, every receiver and the default `JoinBuilder`), so OpenMLS's mandatory **device-wall-clock** lifetime check never fires — removing the `clock_blocked` state, the malicious-committer split (L8-C20) and R-14, and the undecidable refusal branch (on which GHOST cannot read a commit's KeyPackage bounds, sender or proposals, TR-1) — with freshness left to `GhostKpBind.day` on the **trusted** clock (unchanged)? Or keep finite lifetimes with the corrected refusal-branch rule (a lifetime-refused candidate ranks lowest and blocks the settle only as the sole candidate) and the declared L8-C20/R-14 stall? | **the constant lifetime** | Pachetul de chei primește o durată de viață constantă, ca verificarea pe ceasul de sistem al OpenMLS să nu mai oprească niciodată un membru cu ceasul greșit; prospețimea rămâne pe ziua de încredere |
| Q43 | **New (§19.28).** Protect joining with a **password-sealed invite**: the invite's secrets encrypted under a key derived with Argon2id (RFC 9106) from the password and a salt in the link (mechanism (a)), rather than a committer-checked stored verifier (b) or an asynchronous PAKE (c)? Only (a) uses standard primitives already in the graph, stores no verifier anywhere (no seized-device guessing surface), adds no interactive protocol or relay reflex, and composes unchanged with the invite, the committers anchor and admission. | **(a) the password-sealed invite** | Parola de canal protejează intrarea printr-o invitație sigilată cu Argon2id; fără verificator stocat, fără PAKE |
| Q44 | **New (§19.28.5).** Argon2id profile for the sealed invite: **m = 64 MiB, t = 1, p = 4** (measured ≈ 126 ms on the dev host, ~2–3 s estimated on a low-end phone), re-measured at S2's arm64 device step and dropped via `kdf_code` if > ~3–4 s? Floors `t ≥ 1`, `p ≥ 1`, `m ≥ 19 MiB`. | **64 MiB / t=1 / p=4**, device-gated | Argon2id la 64 MiB, o trecere, 4 benzi (≈ 2–3 s pe un telefon slab); se remăsoară pe dispozitiv |
| Q45 | **New (§19.28.4).** A password change bumps `pw_version` (a policy commit) and a committer **refuses** an invite whose `pw_version` is older than the channel's, so old links stop at admission; already-decrypted invites keep working only until that check. Accept that the change takes effect at the committer's admission, not at the invitee's link-opening (a link decrypted before the change still yields a valid join request, refused at admission by `pw_version`)? | **yes** | Schimbarea parolei anulează linkurile vechi la admitere (prin `pw_version`), nu la deschidere |
| Q46 | **New (§19.28.4).** In a password channel a **sponsor must hold the current password** to issue a sealed invite (so a member without it cannot mint invites; `members_may_invite` still applies), rather than restricting issuing to the owner? | **the sponsor must hold the password** | Cine invită într-un canal cu parolă trebuie să știe parola |
| Q47 | **New (§19.28.5).** Run Argon2id in **Kotlin (BouncyCastle `Argon2BytesGenerator`, already a dependency)** with a shared RFC 9106 vector, keeping the Rust shipped graph and allowlist unchanged; or in Rust (the `argon2` crate, +4 crates to G-M1)? | **Kotlin / BouncyCastle** | Argon2id în Kotlin (BouncyCastle, deja prezent), nu în graful Rust livrat |
| Q48 | **New (§19.28.4).** A **rejoin (Q33) and a restore (Q19) do not require the password** (they re-admit a known pseudonym, authorized by the pseudonym signature or a fresh invite), so a member who forgot the password is not locked out of its own channel? | **yes** (no password on rejoin/restore) | Reintrarea și restaurarea nu cer parola (readmit un pseudonim cunoscut) |

---

## 18. Findings outside Phase 10 (proposed as separate tasks)

- **F-1.** STATUS:14 and `relay/README.md:20` assign "Noise gossip" to Phase 10; that is spec v2.0 numbering (its Phase 10 was multi-relay metadata protection). The v2.1 plan places relay-to-relay work nowhere explicitly; the owner should assign it (Phase 12 or operations). Erratum in Appendix B.4.
- **F-2.** The guarantee matrix (plan:241), TM:50 (AD-2 "nu vede … apartenență") and TM:38 (AS-7 "doar în capetele MLS") say relays do not see channel membership; under AD-1 session co-occurrence rebuilds it (L7-D2). Text corrected in Appendix B; decoupling is Phase 12.
- **F-3.** Spec FR-5.5 (90-day default TTL) is superseded for channel and DM blobs by ADR-27/ADR-32; the spec erratum should say so.
- **F-4.** Phase 9's G-R3, G-R4 (graph half), G-R7 and G-R8 are not built; **decided by S0: each is built once, generic over the DM and MLS graphs** (§13.1, S0-18); Phase 9 work in progress builds them, and S2 extends that version.
- **F-5.** Upstream OpenMLS requests (no fork): a storage transaction hook (begin/commit around an operation), which would let GHOST drop the change-set copy; a sender-data peek for ordering; a documented time source for past-epoch stamps; **an injectable time source for leaf and KeyPackage lifetime validation** (today `SystemTime::now()`, switchable only by a crate-internal test flag, §19.4), which would let GHOST judge lifetimes on its trusted clock and let the JVM worlds run on virtual time; **(added by S0)** relaxing `openmls_sqlite_storage`'s `rusqlite` requirement or dropping that optional dependency from `openmls` (V10-1: it blocks the ghost lock; Q40's stub goes when it lands); storing past epochs' secret trees apart from the `MessageSecretsStore` (V10-5: every post rewrites every retained epoch); calling `Lifetime::has_acceptable_range` (V10-11: never called); un-ignoring the test of `create_group_info_with_extensions` (V10-8); and publishing advisories to RustSec (V10-2).
- **F-11 (added by S0; F-10 is left unused here because the text cites Phase 9's F-10 carry-over).** RustSec files RUSTSEC-2026-0209 and -0211 under `libcrux-aesgcm`, the former name of `libcrux-aes`, with no patched version, so no tool maps them to the crate GHOST links; a note to the RustSec maintainers (or a `libcrux-aes` entry) closes the gap for every user (V10-2).
- **F-6.** The v1 `media` table has `owner_operation_id` without a foreign key (`Schema.kt:211-219`); Phase 11 should replace it with the migration-7 style. (The Phase 11 design's migration M drops `media` and `media_blobs` behind a fail-closed guard, `faza11-media.md` D21; migration 7 of this design leaves them to it, so the two agree.)
- **F-9.** The plan's FR-3.4 row reads "Timestamp rotunjit la minut" (plan:262), while spec FR-3.4 reads "Posts MUST support text, media up to 50 MiB and an authenticated client timestamp", accepted by "Boundary-size, invalid-media and timestamp-signature tests pass" (spec p.8). The plan row should carry the media clause; which phase closes it is Q38 (C-17).
- **F-7.** Phase 9's harness debts that Phase 10 must build (§19.10): the seeded native randomness (never built; F9 §22 item 13), the redeem lane inside the world, HIGH mode with send delays, circuit timing, and the fuzz and vendor-test arrangements. Built once, generic over the DM and MLS worlds, they also close parts of Phase 9's own open gate; STATUS should say so when they land.
- **F-8.** THREAT_MODEL S3 ("MLS transcript detects gaps") overclaims against a delivery service that partitions the group: RFC 9750 §5.2 says such a partition "can be detected only by out-of-band comparison". The wording is corrected in Appendix B.2.

---

## 19. Normative corrections after review (round 10b)

**The review.** Round 10a (commit `a79b7ce`) was reviewed adversarially under three lenses: protocol and cryptographic soundness (findings `P10-Rn`), privacy, metadata and threat coverage (`PRV-Hn`), and feasibility, testability, scope and consistency (`FEA-Hn`, `FEA-Mn`). Every finding received was checked against the OpenMLS 0.9.0 sources in the local cargo registry (`om:`; the crate's `.cargo_vcs_info.json` names commit `3a3e35de3feeca8f6605143c464d5452ae584d43`, which is the tag `openmls-v0.9.0`), against RFC 9750 (text fetched 2026-09-25 and quoted below) and against the repository at `f244b80` (`InboxStore.kt`, `Inbox.kt`, `Schema.kt`, `PairSchedule.kt`, `TrafficPolicy.kt`, `.github/workflows/*.yml`, `docs/ci-self-hosted.md`, F7, F9). **No finding received was rejected as wrong** (round 10c rejects no finding in full; it rejects parts of two — P10-R8, P10-R13 — narrows two — P10-R9, PRV-M2 — and declines two optional suggestions — P10-R5, PRV-M5 — each with its reason in §19.0a). Eight proposed fixes are narrowed, replaced or left to the owner, each with its reason in its subsection: P10-R1's "late commit loses at the source" and its optional pre-merge copy (§19.1, Q27); P10-R3's ±1-day window and "no fork evidence" test (§19.3); FEA-H3's "authenticated commit minute" anchor (§19.1); PRV-H3's sponsor-confirmed admission (§19.7, Q30) and its rotation after every expired invite (spaced to one per 14 days, §19.7); PRV-H5's switch at "the first session after the commit's day plus 1" (replaced by the commit's switch hour, which is commit-determined without a day-long pause in posting, §19.8); PRV-H6's reconsidered funder default (kept, with the leak declared and the alternatives offered, §19.9, Q7, Q31).

**What each round read (revised in round 10c).** A tooling defect cut each review at 10 000 characters before round 10b's author saw it: the protocol lens inside P10-R4's correction (P10-R5's title and everything after it unread; P10-R3 referred to a P10-R15 whose text was not delivered), the privacy lens inside PRV-H7's fix (PRV-M1 … PRV-M6 and PRV-L1 … PRV-L4 unread), the feasibility lens inside FEA-M1 (its second half, FEA-M2 … FEA-M8, six low findings, a table of eight proposed owner questions and a table of claims "verified but not true" unread). Round 10b corrected P10-R5 from a reconstruction (§19.5), P10-R15 from P10-R3's reference (§19.3) and FEA-M1 from its first half (§19.6). **Round 10c read the three reviews in full** — `p10-review-protocol.md` (22 935 characters), `p10-review-privacy.md` (15 808), `p10-review-feasibility.md` (16 947), kept under `.claude/scratch/reviews/` outside version control — and checked **every** finding, before and after the cut, against the round-10b text, the repository at `d0fddf8`, the OpenMLS 0.9.0 sources and, where a finding touched it, the Phase 11 design on branch `p11-design` at `116cfcd`. The result is §19.0a: 55 findings (17 protocol, 17 privacy, 21 feasibility) and the feasibility lens's 8 proposed questions; **none remains unread or unanswered**. Round 10b's reconstruction of P10-R5 matched the reviewer's text except for one optional suggestion, which §19.13 declines with its reason. The corrections of round 10c are §19.13 … §19.24; §19.25 is their bookkeeping.

**This section is normative: where it and an earlier section disagree, this section wins.** Earlier sections were corrected in place where the change is local; those places cite "(§19.n)".

| FEA-L4 reload oracle: no secret-tree accessor | low | 10c (behavioural checks) | §19.23 |

| FEA-L6 arm64 never run | low | 10c (named owner action) | §19.23 |
| FEA-Qa … FEA-Qh proposed owner questions | — | a, b, h: adopted as design in 10b (§19.4, §19.1); c, d, e: adopted as design in 10c (§19.13, §19.17), no question needed; f, g: **Q39** | §17 |

### 19.1 Sticky finality, successor-based choice, branch death (P10-R1, FEA-H3, PRV-H4 in part)

**Defects.** (a) *Unbuildable.* Round 10a's CO-6 recorded "a valid commit for a final epoch" with a flag `better`. OpenMLS rejects every handshake message whose epoch is not the group's current epoch in `validate_framing`, which `decrypt_message` calls before decrypting (`om:src/group/public_group/validation.rs:53-75`, valn1307; `om:src/group/mls_group/processing.rs:1142`). After a member merges epoch e, a competing commit for e cannot be decrypted, so its sender, rank and validity are unknowable — unless GHOST keeps the whole pre-merge group state, which is RFC 9750 §5.2.2's second option ("keep a copy of the previous group state … any copies of previous or forked group states must be deleted within a reasonable amount of time to ensure that the protocol provides forward secrecy"). With such a copy and the recorded commits, a seized device re-derives every later epoch, read posts included; without the validity check, anyone holding `K_ep(e)` (a member removed at e + 1 included) can forge "fork evidence". (b) *The majority gives way.* `better = true` sent every member that had settled a rank-255 winner to `RESYNC_NEEDED` when a lower-rank commit for that epoch arrived late (HIGH send delays of up to 10 minutes, Phase 7 retries, a relay outage, or deliberate delay under AD-1), and a malicious member could keep its epoch-e state and grind a self-update below any settled rank-255 winner (a uniform hash beats another in two attempts on average). (c) *Catch-up.* A returning member applied CO-3 to everything it found, so it could merge the late "better" commit the online majority had rejected; and because `first_seen` was its own sighting and it can open epoch e + 1's commit only after settling e, it waited 20 minutes per missed epoch (64 epochs ≈ 21 hours), with the next epoch's blobs deferred under a backoff of up to 6 hours that nothing re-offers (`Inbox` has no re-offer call: `defer` only moves `offer_after_minute` and `peek` returns only due rows, `InboxStore.kt:332-360`).

**Rules (normative; the text is in §6.4 and §6.7).**
1. **Finality is sticky (CO-6).** A settled epoch is never left because of a later commit. A later commit for it is a *late candidate*: recorded as a fork signal (hash and day), never validated, ranked or merged; signals feed diagnostics only, so a forged one costs nothing. No copy of the previous group state is kept (§0.4); Q27 offers the owner the RFC's other option with its forward-secrecy cost stated.
2. **Choice with successors (CO-3).** When more than one valid candidate is held at settle and some held blob does not open, the member trial-settles each candidate (value semantics make this a native call whose change-set Kotlin does not write) and counts the distinct authenticated senders — other than the candidate's own committer and itself — of held blobs that open under the trial state's `R_ep(e + 1)`. The candidate with more successor senders wins; `(rank, hash)` breaks ties and decides alone in the normal case (no successors anywhere). A candidate whose own committer's message opens under another candidate's trial state is *abandoned* (the committer merged something else) and excluded. This makes a returning member follow the branch that others actually continued, and makes a late or ground commit lose unless other members continued it.
3. **Settle point (CO-4).** `anchor(e)` is the earlier of the member's own first sighting and the relay's store hour of the candidate's copy plus 60 minutes (store hour = `relayExpiryHour − handshake TTL`; the relay rounds expiry up to the hour, F7:485); the member settles 20 minutes after the anchor **and after one complete listing pass that started after that moment**, so every candidate stored before the pass is held. A returning member's anchors are in the past: it settles each epoch right after its session's listing pass, then `Inbox.reofferDeferred` (§7.5, X-8) opens the next epoch's blobs at once. The catch-up cost is one settle per missed epoch inside one session.
4. **Branch death (CO-11)** replaces round 10a's stale rule and "fork, this member lost": at least 3 foreign unopenable blobs from at least 2 operators over at least 72 h and 3 sessions, with no progress (no merged commit by another member; fewer than min(2, branch members − 1) distinct other authenticated senders), moves a member to `resync_needed`; progress returns it to `active`. A member pushed there by spam (a member or invite holder storing random blobs) loses only a rejoin request that committers refuse (L8-C11).
5. **Split and partition flags (CO-12).** Foreign unopenable traffic with continuing progress raises `split_suspected`; nine days without a committer's commit while others post raises `partition_suspected`. The UI shows the fingerprint `(epoch, 8 bytes of epoch_authenticator)` for comparison out of band — RFC 9750 §5.2: "A DS can cause a partition in the group by partitioning key exchange messages; this can be detected only by out-of-band comparison (e.g., confirming that all clients have the same epoch_authenticator value)" — and the `resync` action.

**Narrowed or replaced.** *"A late commit loses at the source"* (the committer clears its own commit when its copy was confirmed more than W/2 after another candidate's first sighting): a committer cannot stop a late store — Phase 7 ops close at an hour-granular deadline and are not cancelled — and a late copy is harmless under rules 1 and 2 (nobody who settled moves; a returning member prefers the branch with successors; a committer that merged another winner abandons its own candidate by posting). *An authenticated commit minute as the anchor*: a malicious committer could backdate it to make members settle before a competing candidate arrives; the relay's store hour can be shifted only by a relay, which can partition the group anyway (declared in L8-C17). *"Majority"* is not measured directly: members cannot count the other branch's senders (their blobs do not open); successor senders and the no-progress rule are the observable proxies.

**What stays open (declared).** A relay partition that keeps the branches' traffic apart is detected only by the liveness flag or out of band (L8-C17); two colluding members can attract a returning member in a quiet channel to a fabricated branch until the real branch's traffic reaches it (L8-C21).

**Tests.** EM-3 gains the adversaries of §12.3 (late better commit, grinding, catch-up with and without successors, flooding, spam, permanent withholding from a subset, replay, a wrong system clock) and the catch-up latency property; SCN-CE's latency assertion; SCN-CL; SCN-CM; mutants MM-16 (finality not sticky), MM-24 (no successor preference), MM-25 (no store-hour anchor).

### 19.2 Validity from the commit alone; Welcomes only after a won commit (P10-R4 validity half, P10-R2)

**Defects.** (a) Round 10a's CO-2 required every member to repeat the admission checks of §6.2.4, which read the member's own records: an Add carries only a KeyPackage, not `invite_id` or `invite_sig`; members discard join requests; and a member admitted after an `INVITE_ISSUED` post never holds it (history "none"). Such members rejected commits everyone else merged and forked off silently, `consumed` flags differed between committers, and §6.10's sponsor attribution had no `invite_id` to read. RFC 9750 §5.3 requires that "all honest and correctly implemented clients will arrive at the same 'first valid Commit'". (b) Welcomes were enqueued at the pass, before the commit settled. RFC 9750 §5.2.3: "applications need to ensure that only Welcome messages corresponding to the commit that 'succeeded' are processed by new members"; OpenMLS deletes a non-last-resort KeyPackage once its Welcome is processed (`om:src/group/mls_group/creation.rs:706-709`). A joiner of a losing commit was left alone in a dead epoch with its KeyPackage consumed, and the committer re-admitted a KeyPackage whose private keys were gone.

**Rules (normative).**
1. **`GhostCommitAad`**, carried in every commit's MLS `authenticated_data` (set through `MlsGroup::set_aad`, `om:src/group/mls_group/mod.rs:329`, which the commit builder uses for commits, `commit_builder.rs:939-944`; read by receivers through the processed message, S0 check V10-13). It is authenticated by MLS, readable by every member of the epoch and hidden from relays by the outer seal (T36's header scan covers it):

```
struct {
    uint8          version;        // 1
    uint32         commit_minute;  // the committer's trusted clock, whole minutes (T13); fixes the switch hour (§6.5)
    uint8          flags;          // bit 0: rotate (committers only, CO-2); bits 1-7 zero
    AdmitEvidence  admits<V>;      // one per Add of a new pseudonym, in proposal order
    SwapEvidence   swaps<V>;       // one per (Remove of leaf L, Add of L's pseudonym), in proposal order
} GhostCommitAad;
struct {
    opaque  kp_ref<V>;             // the Add's KeyPackageRef
    opaque  invite_id[16];  opaque invite_pk[32];  uint32 expiry_day;
    opaque  sponsor[32];           // the sponsor's pseudonym key
    opaque  sponsor_sig[64];       // from INVITE_ISSUED (§5.5): over "ghost/v1/ch/invite" ‖ channel_id ‖ invite_id ‖ invite_pk ‖ u32(expiry_day)
    opaque  invite_sig[64];        // from the join request (§5.6)
} AdmitEvidence;                   // about 245 bytes
struct {
    opaque  kp_ref<V>;
    uint8   kind;                  // 1 rejoin, 2 restore
    select (kind) {
        case 1: uint32 generation; uint32 day; uint64 last_epoch; opaque last_hash[32]; opaque rejoin_sig[64];
        case 2: AdmitEvidence restore;
    };
} SwapEvidence;
```

2. **CO-2 checks only what follows from (commit, pre-commit group state, `GhostCommitAad`)**: the evidence of §6.2.4 (A). Expiry, single use, freshness and "the committer holds the `INVITE_ISSUED`" are the admitting committer's policy (§6.2.4 (B), §6.7). The sponsor must be a current member not removed by the same commit, which also voids a removed sponsor's invites deterministically (§19.7).
3. **Single use** is enforced branch-wide by requiring the admitting committer to hold the `INVITE_ISSUED`: it was a member when the invite was issued, so it processed every later Add of its branch and knows every consumption (recorded at settle from the merged `GhostCommitAad`). Across branches of a fork it is not guaranteed (declared in L8-C4).
4. **Welcomes (P10-R2).** `CH-PASS` builds the commit and the Welcome and seals the WELCOME blobs but keeps them wrapped in its own `ch_candidate.welcome_env`; `CH-SETTLE` enqueues them only after `merge_pending_commit` of the committer's own winning commit, and marks the invites consumed there; a lost commit's Welcomes are deleted and its requests stay pending with intact KeyPackages. LC-5 and LC-6 are restated (the Welcome store follows the commit by about 20 minutes and only if it won).

**Tests.** JN (a joiner admitted after an `INVITE_ISSUED` accepts a later Add of that invite; a committer that joined later does not admit it; single use across two committers present since the invite; a removed sponsor's invite), GV (a swap without evidence refused), SCN-CD with an Add on the losing commit, mutants MM-19 (Welcome at the pass), MM-21 (swap without evidence), MM-23 (CO-2 reading local history).

### 19.3 Rejoin and restore: authorization, freshness, binding (P10-R3, P10-R15 by reference)

**Defects.** A rejoin request carried no freshness and no signature: any holder of `JK_g` (current members, invite holders, members removed at the end of g) could re-wrap a member's earlier join or rejoin KeyPackage as a type-0x02 rejoin, and a committer would swap it in with `swap_members`, which "does not enforce that the removed `members` and new members in the `key_packages` correspond" (`om:src/group/mls_group/membership.rs:61-71`), past a key-uniqueness check that leaves out the removed leaves (`om:src/group/public_group/validation.rs:206-225`); OpenMLS keeps no history of used init keys. The victim, processing a commit that removes its own leaf (`processing.rs:470`), set `removed` and deleted its state: a silent eviction, leaving a leaf nobody controls. The restore path of §6.12 (a join with an invite for a current pseudonym) contradicted §6.2.4 (joins required a non-member pseudonym). §6.8's PCS claim ignored that the pseudonym signing key is derived from the seed and never rotates.

**Rules (normative; text in §5.4, §5.6, §6.2.4, §6.7, §6.12).**
1. `JoinRequest` types join, **rejoin** and **restore**. A rejoin carries `rejoin_sig` by the pseudonym key over (label, channel_id, the target generation, the requester's trusted day, its last final epoch and that epoch's winner hash, SHA-256 of the KeyPackage). A restore carries invite evidence and is an invite-authorized swap.
2. **`GhostKpBind`** (KeyPackage extension 0xF0A4; the binding P10-R3 attributes to P10-R15): channel_id, purpose (join, rejoin, restore) and day, signed with the KeyPackage. CO-2 requires the purpose to match the evidence kind, so a join KeyPackage cannot be re-wrapped as a rejoin or restore.
3. **Committer policy for a rejoin** (§6.7): signature under the current leaf's key; day within `[−7, +3]` days of the committer's (Phase 7's σ = 3 d, F7:58, makes the reviewer's ±1 day refuse honest clients; freshness rests mainly on the next three rules); KeyPackage and init key never seen (`ch_kp_seen`, kept for the KeyPackage's lifetime); the claimed point is a fork on the committer's branch (`ch_epoch_log`) or can no longer be caught up in-band (older than a handshake TTL or than the log); no authenticated message from the member's current leaf after the claimed epoch (`ch_member_seen`). CO-2 verifies the signature from the `SwapEvidence`.
4. **What a replay can do**: re-present the requester's own KeyPackage, whose private keys the requester keeps for the request's 7-day window, so an admitted replay completes the requester's own rejoin.
5. **`replaced`**: a member whose leaf is removed by a commit that adds its own pseudonym with a KeyPackage it did not make enters `replaced` (shown), distinct from `removed`.
6. **PCS scoped (§6.8, B.3 L1):** healing holds only against an attacker who did not obtain the pseudonym seed; a seed holder signs rejoins (L8-C9). The reviewer's alternative — every swap authorized by an invite — is Q33's option.

**Tests.** JN (each freshness refusal; old join KeyPackage as rejoin or restore; `GhostKpBind` of another channel or purpose; restore by invite for a current pseudonym), SCN-CM (REPLAY_JOIN: the victim is never evicted), SCN-M20 (the device swap as a restore), mutants MM-20, MM-21.

### 19.4 Clocks: OpenMLS lifetimes, joins, retention, stamps, virtual time (P10-R4 clock half, FEA-H1)

**Defects (all verified in `om:`).** (a) OpenMLS validates the KeyPackage lifetime of every received Add against `SystemTime::now()` (`key_packages/key_package_in.rs:195-197`; `public_group/validation.rs:405` → `882-887`; `lifetime.rs:88-110`); the only switch is a flag settable from OpenMLS's own `cfg(test)` code (`skip_validation.rs`). Commit validity therefore depended on each member's device clock, while round 10a's lifetime started only one day back and Phase 7 tolerates σ = 3 d (F7:58): honest members could disagree. (b) Joins validated every never-updated tree leaf the same way by default (`LeafNodeLifetimePolicy::Verify`, `creation.rs:1293`, `public_group/mod.rs:302-309`), so one idle leaf past its lifetime (possible with round 10a's `idle_evict_days` up to 365) would make every later Welcome of the channel fail. (c) The JVM worlds run on virtual days that OpenMLS never sees: a KeyPackage made on virtual day 60 is "not yet valid" on the host clock. (d) §3.4 point 5 said `added_at` is stamped when an epoch becomes past; it is stamped when an epoch becomes **current** (`past_secrets.rs:80,105`), and `before_timestamp` deletes **every** past epoch when the current epoch's stamp precedes the cutoff (`past_secrets.rs:271-279`), so round 10a's day-based retention could not be expressed.

**Rules (normative).**
1. KeyPackage lifetime `[day − 4 d, day + 80 d]` on the Kotlin-supplied trusted day (84 days, within OpenMLS's 84 d + 1 h maximum).
2. **(Replaced by S0-12, V10-11.)** One predicate on the member's trusted day judges every KeyPackage an Add or swap carries, whatever OpenMLS answered: not accepted by the trusted day ⇒ the commit is invalid; accepted by the trusted day but refused by OpenMLS on the device clock ⇒ *lifetime-pending*, ranked by `(rank, hash)` only, never merged or counted as valid, the member `clock_blocked` (shown: "check the device clock") while it would win (CO-2, CO-4). Round 10b's "ranked as valid" was unsound (OpenMLS's lifetime refusal masks every other defect of the commit, measured) and S0's first correction still let a committer split correct clocks from slow ones. Honest commits are judged alike by every honest member; a malicious committer's near-expiry KeyPackage can split members whose trusted days straddle its bound (T41 and CS-C3 qualified; L8-C20).
3. Joins call `JoinBuilder::skip_lifetime_validation()` (`creation.rs:1313`); GHOST checks every leaf lifetime for day alignment and span ≤ 84 d. `idle_evict_days` is capped at 70 (§5.3), checked by CO-2.
4. `GhostKv::write_message_secrets` stamps new `added_at` values with the call's Kotlin day, never the device's `now`; retention is by count: `delete_past_epoch_secrets(PastEpochDeletion::before_timestamp(UNIX_EPOCH).max_past_epochs(n))`, n from `ch_epoch.final_day` (`config.rs:153,180`; `past_secrets.rs:304-324`) (§8.3).
5. Worlds are anchored to the host clock (§12.1); OpenMLS's lifetime site is exercised in Rust with KeyPackages relative to the host clock (V10-11).

**Tests.** SCN-CM (a member 10 days slow: `clock_blocked`, no divergence), JN expiry cases through GHOST's day checks, mutants MM-27 (lifetime failure treated as invalid), MM-28 (time-based deletion), ME-C4 (stamp).

### 19.5 Self-update flooding (P10-R5, reconstructed)

**What was received.** Only the title: "A member flooding self-updates c…". The fix author reconstructed the finding from round 10a's text; the owner should compare it with the reviewer's full text when it is re-sent.

**Defects (reconstruction).** (a) CO-7 let a committer pass only when it held **no** candidate for its current epoch: a member storing a fresh self-update right after every settle kept every committer from passing — including the pass that would remove it. (b) Nothing bounded how often one member may self-update: it could make every epoch last 20 minutes and churn every member's state and the relays. (c) The candidate cap (the best 8 by `(rank, hash)`) let one member fill it with hash-ground variants and push honest self-updates out.

**Rules (normative).** CO-7: only candidates of rank ≤ the committer's own block its pass (its commit outranks a rank-255 candidate). CO-14: a rank-255 commit for epoch e is valid only if the sender's leaf has `GhostLeaf.refresh_epoch ≤ e − min(8, n)`; the update-path leaf of every commit carries `refresh_epoch = e` (set with `CommitBuilder::leaf_node_parameters`, `commit_builder.rs:359`; read from `StagedCommit::update_path_leaf_node`, `staged_commit.rs:931`; V10-13), so the rule reads only the tree and every member computes it alike. `k = min(8, n)` keeps honest members inside the rule (at 20 members about 20–32 epochs pass in 28 days; at 3 members about 7) while bounding one member to a 1/k share of epochs. Candidates: at most one per sender (its lowest hash). Self-updates never trigger the removal hold, so they never pause posting (§19.6).

**Tests.** SCN-CM (FLOOD_SELFUPDATE: the excess refused, the committer passes and removes the flooder), GV (a rank-255 commit above the rate refused), EM-3 liveness, mutant MM-26.

### 19.6 Removal hold; E-5 corrected (PRV-H1, FEA-M1)

**Defects.** (a) A member kept posting in epoch e until its own settle, 20 minutes after first seeing a commit, and a member returning late also posted in e for 20 minutes after catching up — into a namespace the removed member still lists for 14 days. Everything posted in that window was readable by the removed member, while T38 tested only later epochs. (b) E-5 claimed OpenMLS refuses messages while a commit is pending; `create_message` checks only `is_active()` and the proposal store (`om:src/group/mls_group/application.rs:89-94`) and never calls `is_operational` (`mod.rs:1115-1121`).

**Rules (normative).** CO-13: while a member holds a valid candidate for its current epoch that removes a pseudonym, the channel is not current for posting; posts wait as plaintext and are encrypted in e + 1. The own-pending-commit hold is GHOST's (`MlsNative.encrypt` refuses with `PENDING_COMMIT`; narrowed by §19.13 to an own commit that removes a pseudonym, FEA-M1's second half). With §19.1's anchors a returning member settles at once, so its hold is short. RFC 9750 §5.2.2's first option pauses sending for **every** candidate; GHOST pauses only for removals, where a departing member could read, because self-updates and Adds expose nothing and a pause per self-update would stall posting about ten times a week at 20 members.

**Residue (declared, L8-C18).** Posts encrypted before their author first held the removing commit — bounded by the commit's propagation: the committer's send delay (up to 10 minutes in HIGH) plus the author's listing interval.

**Tests.** T38's hold variant (§12.8), SCN-M20's assertion, mutant PC-14.

### 19.7 Invites: sponsor validity, leaked invites, bounded invite secrets, the name (PRV-H2, PRV-H3)

**Defects.** (a) Admission never required the sponsor to be a member: a removed sponsor's unexpired invites let it back in with a new seed within 7 days, through a request in the draining namespace that committers admit automatically. (b) L8-C4 said a leaked invite "nu dă intrarea (unică folosință)"; single use only means the first presenter wins, and admission is automatic, so a leaked unused invite **is** an entry. Q5 mentioned only traffic volume. (c) `RB_g`, `DB_g` and `JK_g` live as long as the generation, which rotated only on removal or class change: in a channel that never removes anyone, every invite ever issued kept metadata read access, deposits (quota exhaustion) and join-traffic opening for the channel's life. (d) The link carried the channel name in clear.

**Rules (normative).** (a) CO-2 requires the sponsor to be a current member not removed by the same commit (from the `GhostCommitAad`); committers void a removed member's unconsumed invites at settle. (b) L8-C4 and Q5 corrected; QR in person remains the recommended path. (c) The **rotation flag** (§6.5): a committer rotates when an invite of the generation expired unconsumed or the generation is 28 days old, at most once per 14 days and never during a drain (Q28); routine rotations also blur removal timing (LC-4). (d) No name in the link by default (Q29). The reviewer's sponsor-confirmed admission step (the sponsor compares the joiner's key fingerprint before a committer admits) would make a leaked invite useless without the sponsor but adds a second online round trip; it is Q30, with a policy bit reserved.

**Tests.** JN (a removed sponsor's invite), FD (rotations by flag, never during a drain), mutant MM-22.

### 19.8 No-reflex corrections (PRV-H5)

**Defects.** T40 forbade changes caused by a received blob except LC-6, yet the design had four: (a) every member registered and started listing `ns_{g+1}` at its own settle, first sighting + 20 minutes, which revealed each member's fetch minute and let relays spot the rotating commit by the wave of new listings that followed each member's `get` (breaking P-C3 for rotating commits and pointing at the committer); (b) the removed member stopped listening at its merge (and §6.6 contradicted §6.5); (c) `CH-JOIN` re-registered namespaces while G-M5 forbade `Namespaces.register` from `CH-JOIN`; (d) a non-rotating funder's settle created registration rows, which F9's Q31 rule redeems "as soon as an eligible token exists", with the Q29 redeem hold lengthening the session — exactly mutant PC-6 — and the text never said when those rows appear. And the read-pair arithmetic was wrong: 12 channels × 6 pairs while draining = 72 > `maxReadPairs = 64` (`TrafficPolicy.kt:50`); past the cap every pair, the DM inbox included, acts at 1/m of its events (`PairSchedule.kt:17-20,47`), so a co-member who leaves several shared channels could slow a victim's DM listing — a remotely triggered marker.

**Rules (normative).** (a) **`CH-SWITCH`**: the new generation is registered and listened to from the first listing step at or after `switch_hour = ceil_hour(commit_minute + 30 min)` (from the commit's `GhostCommitAad`, clamped to the settle + 2 h), or from the next session start for a member that settled later or joined; posts wait until the switch. (b) Removed and leaving members keep listening on their schedule until the drain ends (`day(switch_hour) + 14`, computed from the commit that removed them), fetching and dropping. (c) `CH-JOIN` makes no namespace call; `CH-SWITCH` does it at the joiner's next session start; G-M5 lists `CH-SWITCH`. (d) Non-rotating funders create registration rows only in their EXPIRING renewal step; the rotating committer funds at its switch hour (Q31). (e) At most two generations listened per channel (a second rotation ends the older drain), and **9 channels** per client (Q4): `6 × 9 = 54` pairs plus the DM inbox (3, up to 6 after a slot-table change) and drops fit 64 even if every channel drains at once. T40's exceptions now list LC-4, LC-5 and LC-6; NI-CH4 is the rotating-commit twin; PC-15 is its mutant.

### 19.9 Roles visible to relays (PRV-H6)

**Defect.** The design disclaimed only membership (§0.4), while under AD-1 the relays single out roles: the default funder (the owner) by weekly redemptions and registrations for the namespace; the rotating committer by its redemptions for a brand-new namespace and its Welcome-sized stores; co-funders by several capability serials crediting one verifier in `keys.redb` (`relay/crates/node/src/register.rs:8-11,139-146`); the removed client as the one listener that never moves to `ns_{g+1}`. Phase 7's co-occurrence attaches each to the client's DM cluster, and one relay serving both `ns_g` and `ns_{g+1}` links the generations without AD-1. Channel-admin identity is the most sensitive graph fact under AD-12 (legal demands).

**Correction.** Declared as LC-8 and L8-C19, in §0.4 and in Appendix B.2; Q7 states what the owner-as-funder default reveals and its alternative; Q31 (who funds a new generation) and Q32 (Welcome fragments) give the owner the two mechanisms that would reduce the leak. No mechanism is changed by default: every reduction costs either availability (stores wait for a funder's renewal) or a new format (fragmentation), and Phase 12 owns the decoupling of co-occurrence.

### 19.10 Harness debts, determinism, interoperability vectors, fuzzing, scripts (PRV-H7, FEA-H2, FEA-H4, FEA-H5)

**Defects.** The test plan leaned on Phase 9 pieces that were designed and never built: the seeded `getrandom` backend (F9 §22 item 13 and l.3862: "no `getrandom_backend = "custom"` anywhere in the tree"; confirmed by grep), the redeem lane inside the world (JD8, PM-1 unbuilt), send delays and HIGH mode (`ModelRelay` ignores `not_before_minute`; F9 worlds fix STANDARD), circuit open and close times (INVARIANTS T28 lists session-close comparison as not built), refusals and hostile modes, the `dm-fuzz` arrangement and the vendor tests (STATUS, Phase 9 row), and a `libcrux-portable.sh` script that does not exist (the rule is inline in `dm-emulator.yml:30-31,176-189`). OpenMLS's `rayon` path draws HPKE randomness on the worker thread (`treekem.rs:75`; `hpke-rs-libcrux/src/lib.rs:355-369`), defeating per-actor-thread attribution. The RFC 9420 passive-client replay "through `GhostKv`" needs `KeyPackageBundle::new` (`test-utils` only, `key_packages/mod.rs:752-756`) and meets KeyPackages "expired by now" that OpenMLS's own KAT tolerates only through its crate-internal flag (`passive_client.rs:110,195`); and the crates.io package ships no `test_vectors/` (its directories are `benches`, `examples`, `src`, `tests`; the KATs read `../test_vectors/*.json`, present in the git repository at the tag commit).

**Rules (normative).** The debts are sized work in this phase (§14): S1 builds the redeem lane, send delays with HIGH, circuit timing and refusals in the world; S2 builds the process-global seeded stream with serialised native calls (or record/replay if V10-6 fails) and the test-only crate `ghost-client-mls-kat`; S11 builds the fuzz arrangement generic over DM and MLS; `libcrux-portable.sh` is extracted (decided by S0, S0-8). `mls-vendor-tests` is a git checkout at `3a3e35de3feeca8f6605143c464d5452ae584d43`, verified by hash, run with `all-ciphersuites` (S0-13). GHOST's passive-client replay covers the 43 vectors (252 epochs) of the provider's suites, which V10-12 passed through `GhostKv` (S0-13; round 10c expected a host-clock-dependent subset: the received Adds carry unbounded lifetimes). Until each harness piece exists, every report line names what it compared (§15 item 10a); SCN-M20 and the privacy world run STANDARD only and say so.

### 19.11 Effort and CI capacity (FEA-H6, FEA-H7)

**Defects.** §14's "12 weeks, team of 5–6" measured the wrong constraint and was not calibrated: F9 estimated 10–12 weeks and landed its code in about seven active days, while its gate stayed open for reasons that are not coding (the artifact quota, KVM, owner decisions, 30 of 97 mutants run). The claim that the plan's 6–9 weeks holds with Q3, Q15 and Q20 was unsupported (Q3 saves about a day; Q15's default was already assumed; Q20 changes CI time). S0 was blocked on CI restoration although its checks run locally, and the runner budget measured only the PR `android` job while eight nightly workflows already share four runners on the owner's laptop, which run only while he is logged in.

**Rules (normative).** §14 states implementation effort per slice at F9's observed pace (about 32 working days) and, separately, the closure blockers with their owners and calendar; CI restoration is required at the S10 and S11 exits, not S0; S0 measures total nightly occupancy (V10-14) and sets the gate rotation; the laptop dependency is declared (R-7, Q20).

### 19.12 Bookkeeping

- **New identifiers.** CO-11 … CO-14; C-15, C-16; LC-8; NI-CH4; SCN-CL, SCN-CM; MM-19 … MM-28, PC-14, PC-15; V10-11 … V10-14; L8-C17 … L8-C21; R-14 … R-17; Q27 … Q34; F-7, F-8; E-26 … E-36; the transaction `CH-SWITCH`; the tables `ch_liveness`, `ch_epoch_log`, `ch_fork_signal`, `ch_kp_seen`, `ch_member_seen`; the formats `GhostCommitAad`, `GhostKpBind`, `GhostLeaf.refresh_epoch`, `JoinRequest` types rejoin and restore; the states `replaced` and the flags `split_suspected`, `partition_suspected`, `clock_blocked`; `Inbox.reofferDeferred`; the crate `ghost-client-mls-kat`.
- **Changed values.** KeyPackage lifetime `[day − 4, day + 80]`; `idle_evict_days` 35 … 70; 9 channels per client; past-epoch deletion by count; rotation triggers; the funding time of the rotating committer (its switch hour).
- **Withdrawn.** Round 10a's CO-6 "fork evidence, better", its stale rule of §6.7, the Welcome enqueue at the pass, the removed member's stop at its merge, `CH-JOIN`'s namespace registration, "OpenMLS refuses messages while a commit is pending" (E-5), the claim that the seeded `getrandom` backend exists, the crates.io source of the vendor vectors, and the 9–12-week "team" estimate.
- **Appendices.** The ADR drafts (A), the text changes (B) and the evidence (C, D) are updated to this section.

### 19.13 Committers: own-commit custody, swaps of any rank, resignation and transfer, the moderators' work-age rule (P10-R7, P10-R12, P10-R17, FEA-M1, FEA-M2, FEA-M7, P10-R5 remainder)

**Defects (verified).** (a) *Stranded committer (P10-R7).* CO-4 cleared an own commit when its op closed INDETERMINATE without a verified copy. If a copy had reached a relay and others merged it, the committer could never adopt it: OpenMLS surfaces the author's own PrivateMessages as `OwnPrivateMessage` without decrypting them (`om:src/framing/validation.rs:158-172`; `processing.rs:251-277`), so the committer stayed in epoch e, merged nothing, and reached only a rejoin — which (b) forbade for rank 0. The same code shows that the "own sender" claim rests on sender data, which every member of the epoch can encrypt. (b) *Rank-0 swaps (P10-R7, FEA-M2).* A swap contains a Remove of the member's leaf, and CO-2 let rank r remove only ranks above r: the owner could be neither restored onto a new device nor readmitted after a fork, and a moderator only by rank 0. (c) *The owner could not leave (P10-R12).* RFC 9420 §12.2 makes a commit that removes its own committer invalid; OpenMLS refuses it (`CreateCommitError::CannotRemoveSelf`, `commit_builder.rs:808-810`); nobody ranks below 0. (d) *Moderator passes (FEA-M7, P10-R17).* Rank r ≥ 1 passed only while no lower rank had committed in `r × 48 h`; rank 0 commits weekly in a quiet channel, so rank 1 was active about five days in seven and raced the owner on fresh requests, and Q25's "48 h of absence" misdescribed it; whether a moderator's user-action Remove was a pass was ambiguous. (e) *Posting freeze (FEA-M1, second half).* Every own pending commit froze the committer's posts for at least 20 minutes, though only a removal needs it. (f) *Rank basis (P10-R17).* CO-2 and CO-3 did not say which context supplies the rank.

**Rules (normative; text in §6.3.1, §6.4, §6.6, §6.7, §6.12).**
1. **Own-commit custody (CO-4).** An own commit that may have reached a relay is kept pending until it wins at a settle point with a verified copy, shows successor senders in a trial settle, loses to another candidate, or can no longer exist anywhere (deadline plus handshake TTL). Own blobs are recognised by hash, never through `process_message`; a blob OpenMLS reports as `OwnPrivateMessage` that is not an own op is a forgery (FORGE_OWN). An unverified own candidate ranks below a held foreign one with as many successors (CO-3).
2. **Swaps of any rank (CO-2).** A swap with rejoin or restore evidence may be committed by any committer for any member; the pseudonym keeps its place in `committers` and `funders`. No hijack follows: rejoin evidence is signed by the member's pseudonym key, and a restore KeyPackage's credential is that key.
3. **Resignation and transfer (CO-2, §6.6).** Any committer may drop its own key from `committers` and `funders` (not the last committer); rank 0 may reorder `committers`. A committer leaves by resigning (rank 0: transferring) and then leaving as a member.
4. **Work-age rule (CO-8).** Every committer's user actions are passes at once; rank r ≥ 1 includes in scheduled passes only work it recorded at least `r × 48 h` earlier, plus its own weekly PCS commit (rank 0's PCS commit is daily in short-timer channels, Q36).
5. **Posting (§6.3.1).** A committer's own pending commit holds its posts only if it removes a pseudonym (CO-13 covers it; `PENDING_COMMIT` is its native backstop).
6. **Rank basis (CO-1).** Ranks are read from the pre-commit context.

**Declined (P10-R5's optional suggestion).** Replacing the committer-local `ch_member_refresh` days by the tree's `GhostLeaf.refresh_epoch` for eviction: a KeyPackage leaf carries `refresh_epoch = 0`, so the tree does not show when a never-updated member joined, and turning epochs into days still needs the committer's own log; the local rows stay (L8-C12).

**Owner question.** An owner who vanishes without transferring (Q37): no safe succession rule exists without trusted time (an epoch count can be pumped by a moderator's empty commits; a commit minute is the committer's claim), so the default declares it (L8-C16).

**Tests.** EM-3 (stranded committer, owner on a dead branch, resignation and transfer, aged-work races), SCN-CD INDETERMINATE variant, SCN-CK, SCN-M20 owner-restore variant, GV, RO-C (posting during a non-removing commit), RP-C (FORGE_OWN); mutants MM-32, MM-33, MM-34.

### 19.14 Leaf continuity (P10-R8)

**Defect (verified).** RFC 9420 §5.3.1: when a member's credential is replaced, "the AS MUST also verify that the set of presented identifiers in the new credential is valid as a successor". OpenMLS leaves this to the caller (`om:src/group/public_group/validation.rs:890-895`, valn0108–0110; no signature-key comparison exists outside tests) and supports rotating the signature key inside a commit (`CommitBuilder::build_with_new_signer`, `commit_builder.rs:621`). Round 10b checked "identity equals signature key" only for Adds, so a member could change its pseudonym mid-channel through an update path — breaking D12, sponsor records, eviction and rejoin mapping — or present a credential whose identity is another member's key.

**Rule (CO-2).** Every leaf a commit installs keeps its member's pre-commit signature key and carries a `BasicCredential` whose identity equals it; a swap's new leaf has the removed leaf's key. GHOST never calls `build_with_new_signer` (G-M2's source scan refuses the symbol, with `store_pending_proposal`, §13.1). **Rejected part:** the reviewer also asked that `GhostLeaf.box_pk` stay the same; §19.17 makes the box key fresh per leaf for forward secrecy, and a member changing its own `box_pk` affects only reveals addressed to itself.

**Tests.** GV (NEW_SIGNER: a changed signer, a mismatched credential), SCN-CM; mutant MM-31.

### 19.15 Silent orphans, the delivery qualifier, spam outside the current generation (P10-R9, PRV-M5)

**Defects (verified against round 10b's CO-11 and CO-12).** (a) A member that missed a rotating commit which has since expired keeps listening to the old namespace; members post in the new one after the switch (posts wait until the switch, §6.5), so the old namespace drains and goes quiet: nothing unopenable arrives (CO-11 never fires) and no message from others arrives (CO-12's `partition_suspected` requires them). The member is cut off in silence. (b) CS-C4, T41 and P-C4 claimed their properties unconditionally, though every one of them assumes the relays deliver the commits. (c) CO-11 counted unopenable blobs in every listened namespace, so members removed at the end of the old generation — who can still deposit into the draining namespace — could push a quiet channel's members into `resync_needed` and make them re-expose KeyPackages under `JK_g` (L8-C5).

**Rules.** (a) `stale_suspected` (CO-12): no committer commit and no other member's message for 9 days, while someone else is a committer; a flag with the fingerprint and `resync`, never a state change (the reviewer's "go to `RESYNC_NEEDED`" is narrowed: in an idle channel it would send every member into a rejoin). (b) CS-C3, CS-C4, P-C4 and T41 hold "while at least one relay serving the member delivers every commit within its TTL" (§0.2, §11.1); what remains is the flags and L8-C17. (c) CO-11 counts only the current generation's namespace; invite holders of the current generation can still trigger it (L8-C11). The reviewer's alternative — "require a missed-commit signal" — has no observable signal to build on (a missed commit is exactly an unopenable blob) and is not adopted.

**Tests.** SCN-CL silent-orphan variant (MM-36), SCN-CM spam from a removed member (MM-37), EM-3 detection.

### 19.16 Joins, restores and proposals (P10-R10, P10-R11, PRV-M1, P10-R14, P10-R15 remainder, P10-R17 rejoin Welcome)

**Defects (verified).** (a) *Bootstrap fallback (P10-R10).* The WELCOME blob is sealed under `JK_g`, which invite holders and members removed at the end of g also hold, and the fallback bootstrap was HPKE base mode to the joiner's `box_pk`, which authenticates no sender (RFC 9180 §5.1.1): any `JK_g` holder could re-seal the same MLS Welcome with a bootstrap of its choosing and move the joiner to a namespace it controls; the joiner never checked `G`. (b) *Restores did not rotate (P10-R11, PRV-M1).* The replaced — possibly stolen — stack kept `G_g`, READ, DEPOSIT and `JK_g`: listing, quota exhaustion and every later joiner's KeyPackage; T38's "no later generation" passed vacuously because no later generation existed. (c) *Received proposals (P10-R14).* OpenMLS refuses `create_message` while its proposal store is non-empty (`application.rs:92-94`) and stores a received proposal only through `store_pending_proposal` (`processing.rs:398`); the receive table had no row for proposals, so an implementer following OpenMLS's examples would let one member's proposal block everyone's posts until the next commit. (d) *Rejoin Welcomes (P10-R17).* `welcomeJoin` over rows that still hold the old group fails with `GroupAlreadyExists` (`creation.rs:305-312`) unless `replace_old_group()` is used. (e) *Cloned channel ids (P10-R15).* A creator who knows a `channel_id` (a removed member) can create a new group with it; a former member who accepts re-derives its old pseudonym.

**Rules.** (a) **(Replaced by S0-9 and S0-15.)** The bootstrap travels only in the Welcome's GroupInfo (V10-8 confirmed it reaches it; the `K_boot(e)` fallback and `boot_flag` are withdrawn); since the GroupInfo signature authenticates only a signer the Welcome's own tree supplies, the joiner also checks the committers anchor (§5.4, §6.2.5); and it checks `ns(G)` against the namespace the Welcome arrived in when the generation matches (`welcomeJoin`'s `expectedNs`). (b) A restore swap rotates the generation (§6.5); a rejoin swap does not (MM-18 stands). (c) Proposals are dropped before `process_message`, counted, never stored (§6.3.2). (d) Rejoin and restore Welcomes are joined over the KeyPackage's global rows only; the old rows are deleted in the same transaction (§6.2.5). (e) Declared (L8-C22): the client keeps no list of channels it left (T33), so a clone cannot be recognised; the accept screen states the pseudonym rule. A salt per join would break D12 (Q13).

**Tests.** JN (REPLACE_BOOTSTRAP, wrong namespace, rejoin Welcome with old rows), T38 restore variant, FD, RP-C (PROPOSE), SCN-CK; mutants PC-17, MM-29, MM-30.

### 19.17 Reveal: transferable proof, box keys per leaf, card source, retention (P10-R13, PRV-M3, FEA-M4)

**Defects (verified).** (a) `sig_id` and `sig_ps` are Ed25519 signatures anyone can verify, so the recipient can prove pseudonym ↔ `ghost1` to an operator, a court or other members; L8-C8 said only that M "keeps the proof". (b) `b_sk` was derived from the pseudonym seed and never changed, so reveals had no forward secrecy: a member who archived REVEAL posts could open every past reveal to M after a later theft of M's seed. (c) `CardSource.CHANNEL_REVEAL` does not exist: the enum has `QR_IN_PERSON` and `LINK` (`ghost/android/messaging/.../api/Messaging.kt:350-361`), `dm_contact.source` has a frozen CHECK (`'qr_in_person', 'link', 'request'`, `Schema.kt:928`), migration 7 did not rebuild it, and a new value would mark the contact for good as "came from a channel". (d) `ch_reveal` had a minute column and no retention rule.

**Rules.** (a) Declared in L8-C8 and §0.4; the Phase 13 warning names it; the deniable alternative is **Q35**. The reviewers' deniable mechanisms (HPKE Auth mode with an identity X25519 key; a MAC under a Diffie-Hellman value M could also compute) are **not buildable as specified**: card v2 carries only the Ed25519 identity key, and the Olm identity key lives inside vodozemac, which exposes no raw Diffie-Hellman (F9 D4, §2); Q35's alternative confirms the binding inside the recipient's first Olm DM instead, whose messages carry no signatures. Either way the REVEAL post's MLS signature proves which pseudonym sent that ciphertext. (b) A fresh box key per leaf (§5.1, §5.4), kept in `ch_box_key` until the last epoch holding that leaf leaves retention. (c) The card enters as `CardSource.LINK`, trust `unverified`; no schema rebuild. (d) `ch_reveal` follows the REVEAL post's timer and keeps only its deletion deadline.

**Tests.** RV, SCN-CJ (forward secrecy after a leaf refresh); mutant PC-18.

### 19.18 Ordering keys, unlisted losses, worst-case commits (P10-R16 parts 2 and 3, P10-R17)

**Defects (verified).** (a) CS-S7 promised that "MLS authenticates the real sender and generation, and a mismatch drops the message"; `ProcessedMessage` exposes group id, epoch, sender, AAD, content and credential, not the ratchet generation (`om:src/framing/validation.rs:500-530`). (b) §6.3.3 said a lying `order` "only misorders the liar's own messages"; but `order` lives in the seal, which every holder of `K_ep(e)` can produce, so a member could re-seal a victim's post with a false `order` — moving a late post to the front advances the victim's ratchet and pushes its earlier posts past the tolerance of 16 (`sender_ratchet.rs:274-315`): a member-caused loss of another member's posts. (c) §6.3.6 omitted the loss a relay causes by withholding part of a burst past the 12-minute hold. (d) The floor rule split only Adds, and V10-4 measured single operations, while a composite commit (context change, Remove, swap by value, forced path) in a blank tree is estimated at 3.8–4.3 KB against `C(4096) = 4 028`, and `GhostCommitAad` adds about 245 bytes per admission.

**Rules.** (a, b) Application messages carry `ChannelOrder = (sender_leaf, seq)` in their MLS AAD (§5.5), readable before decryption (`PrivateMessageIn::aad`, `private_message_in.rs:59`), authenticated by MLS and settable only by the author; the seal's `order` must repeat it (`open` drops a mismatch), and the decrypted sender must equal `sender_leaf`; `seq` stays the author's claim. (c) The ratchet-range loss is declared (§6.3.6, CS-C5). (d) **(Revised by S0-4 and S0-16.)** §5.7 defers Adds, then swaps, then the context change except the drops of keys whose Removes the commit keeps, then extra Removes with their drops; V10-4 measured the worst cases: with canonical KeyPackages every single-proposal commit fits the small floor at 32 members in every width (tightest 172 B), so the cap stays 32 and Q3 is re-asked with both options (R-19).

**Tests.** RO-C (RESEAL_ORDER, withheld burst), V10-4, V10-13; mutant MM-35.

### 19.19 The seized-device window (PRV-M2)

**Defect (verified).** L8-C10 bounded a seized device's reach to retained epochs "(≤ 2 zile)". The device also holds the current epoch, whose sender ratchets derive forward up to 1 000 generations per sender and keep 16 skipped keys (`sender_ratchet.rs:274-315`): every message of the current epoch it has not consumed opens, from the epoch's start — up to about 7 days with the weekly PCS commit and without limit while no committer commits. **Narrowed:** the reviewer's "exceeds ADR-27's promise" holds against whoever recorded the relays; from honest relays the device can fetch only posts within their TTL, which ADR-27 already bounds.

**Rules.** L8-C10 corrected; §2.1, §8.3 and §12.8 state the current-epoch exposure; the seizure oracle measures it against every recorded blob and prints the oldest message opened. **Q36:** a daily PCS commit in channels with timers ≤ 1 d (default yes), which keeps the window near the timer when a committer is active; a PCS commit does not shut out the seized device itself, whose leaf stays until the member is removed or replaced (§6.8).

### 19.20 The relay tries the secrets it is shown (PRV-M4)

**Defect.** JC-1 recomputes relay-visible values from public material; nothing checked that a seal, recognition or join key is not derivable from what relays are shown (READ and DEPOSIT secrets inside bearer credentials, verifiers, registration capabilities) — the F9 PM-20 analogue.

**Rule.** JC-7 (§12.7): the model relay tries every value it was shown as K and as R of §5.6 on every stored blob: 0 hints match, 0 blobs open; mutant PC-16. Derivation rule 1 of §5.2 (every key input a secret never presented) is what makes it pass.

### 19.21 Rows derived from posts; the administrative posts' TTL (PRV-M6)

**Defects (verified against §8.5 and §9).** T25's cases covered POST and REACTION only: `INVITE_ISSUED` became a `ch_invite_issued` row kept up to 7 days even in 5-minute channels, REVEAL became a `ch_reveal` row kept with no limit, `ch_sponsor` lived for the membership. T33's "no activity times except refresh days" was contradicted by `ch_reveal.minute`, `ch_epoch.anchor_minute` (kept after the epoch was final), `ch_schedule.last_own_commit_day` and `ch_sponsor.admitted_day`, among others. Checking this found a functional defect too: `INVITE_ISSUED` and `LEAVE` travelled with the post TTL, so in 5-minute and 1-hour channels they left the relays after one hour and a committer that did not list within that hour never admitted the invitee or removed the leaver.

**Rules.** §8.5's table: each derived row follows its post's timer or is declared membership metadata with its bound; `anchor_minute` is cleared at finality (a trigger); `ch_reveal` keeps only a deletion deadline; §9 lists every declared activity-time column and the introspection test fails on a new one. `INVITE_ISSUED` and `LEAVE` use the handshake TTL (§5.8; X-7 extended; L8-C14). Mutants ME-C5, ME-C6.

### 19.22 Declarations: exception lists, TTL per timer version, "no content from before", slot moves, namespace shape (PRV-L1 … PRV-L4, FEA-M3 remainder)

**Defects and rules.** (a) *Exception lists (PRV-L1, FEA-M3).* §7.7 declared the CO-9 retry after a lost self-update, but LC-6, T40, P-C6 and NI-CH1 did not; and the rotating committer's funding follows only a **won** commit, which depends on received candidates. LC-6 now covers both, and T40, P-C6 and §7.7 repeat it. NI-CH2 held only up to the commit's store while every own commit froze the committer's posts; with §19.13 it holds after the store for non-removing commits and is compared only up to the store for removing ones. T27 and T31 are amended for funded channel namespaces and the rotating committer's funding (§11.2). (b) *One TTL per generation (PRV-L2).* A timer change between 7 d, 30 d and off moves the TTL inside one namespace, and a join request keeps the handshake TTL frozen in its invite: JC-4 and P-C3 are stated per timer version, and the stale-invite TTL is declared (LC-1). (c) *"History none" (PRV-L3).* The joiner opens the generation's earlier join traffic with the invite's `JK_g` and learns the rotation count, the epoch and the blank leaves: T39 and P-C5 state the exception, L8-C3 declares the rest, and the UI and Q11 say "no content from before". (d) *Slot moves and shape (PRV-L4).* The L7-D26 residue for channel credentials is declared (L8-C23, §7.2); CS-S1's "same shape as DMs" is restricted to single blobs, since listener counts and the large class's 16 KiB floor tell channel namespaces apart (LC-1, LC-2).

### 19.23 Feasibility: sequencing, the T2 world, layering, `libcrux-aes`, drain end, reload oracle, tokens, read pairs, arm64 (FEA-M5, FEA-M6, FEA-M8, FEA-L1 … FEA-L6)

**Verified and corrected.**
- *Sequencing (FEA-M6).* ADR-25 and ADR-30′ … ADR-39 are proposed and await the owner (`docs/STATUS.md`, ADR row); Phase 9's exit gate is open and its nightly T2 sweep has never been green. **Q39** asks the order (default: S0 now, S1 after the ADR decision, closure before Phase 9 allowed and marked). §7.9's change to the T2 world (`ghost/issuer/crates/service/tests/t2/world.rs`) had no owner: it is in S6, and a green T2 sweep is not a Phase 10 exit item.
- *Layering (FEA-M8).* `:crypto-bridge` has no project dependency and its build file says it must not gain `:storage` or `:messaging`; `:sync` api-exports `:storage` and `:network` (`ghost/android/sync/build.gradle.kts:31-32`). The bridge stays a leaf; `:messaging` maps to `:sync` types (§10.1).
- *`libcrux-aes` (FEA-M5).* `libcrux-aead` 0.0.9 with default features pulls `libcrux-aes` 0.0.9, whose `build.rs` reads the SIMD switches without `rerun-if-env-changed`; the clean lists in `ci.yml:254`, `dm-emulator.yml:149,165,172` and `dm-privacy-gate.yml:114,197` name only `libcrux-ml-kem`, `-sha3` and `-intrinsics`. G-M9 and the lists gain `-p libcrux-aes`; §13.3's versions are corrected (only `hmac-drbg` is 0.0.1). **Confirmed by V10-7 (S0-8):** simd256 → still simd256 after the variables changed → portable after a clean → still portable after the reverse change; the rule is extracted into `libcrux-portable.sh`.
- *Drain end (FEA-L3).* `Namespaces.remove` is false while ops or fetched, unconsumed rows exist (`Namespaces.kt:55-61`): `CH-SWEEP` marks the drained namespace's deferred rows consumed first (§6.5).
- *Reload oracle (FEA-L4).* No public accessor exposes the secret tree: the oracle compares the public accessors and checks persistence behaviourally (§3.4 point 6).
- *Tokens (FEA-L2).* At 9 funded channels a funder keeps 5 tokens per slot per week for rotations (4 with Phase 11's `M_0`) (§7.4).
- *Read pairs (FEA-L1).* The 64-pair bound holds outside the six weeks after a slot-table change; inside them it can be passed (declared, L8-C24; §7.7, Q4).
- *arm64 (FEA-L6).* The device step on two arm64 phones is a named owner action before distribution (§12.11, §15 item 13, Q20).
- *E-13 (FEA-L5)* was already re-read and quoted in round 10b.

### 19.24 Channel media: the Phase 11 contract (coordination; C-17)

**Finding (not from the reviews).** Spec FR-3.4 requires posts with "media up to 50 MiB" (P0; spec p.8, text extracted with `pdftotext`), accepted by "Boundary-size, invalid-media and timestamp-signature tests"; the plan's row keeps only the timestamp (plan:262; F-9). Round 10b left media to Phase 11 (§0.4); the Phase 11 design (branch `p11-design` at `116cfcd`, read-only here) leaves the channel binding to Phase 10: its §4.3 proposes that POST TLV 0x0003 carry the descriptor's 84 fixed bytes with the POST's text as the caption and that the MLS crate implement the key-slot rule; its §5.6 lists what Phase 10 must decide (a relay-blind media namespace with credentials, automatic download, fan-out egress, the key slot, the timer mapping, what a removed member keeps); its Q17 asks that channel media be "designed and gated with Phase 10"; its Q38 reserves ADR-44 … 47, T42–T47, L9 and the migration after Phase 10's 6 and 7, which this design keeps.

**Resolution.** The formats accommodate the pointer now (§5.5, §5.10): the TLV's value and size, the optional caption, the floor-sized pointer post, the key-slot rule in `encrypt` and `receive` (arguments reserved, not in P0's G-M6 list), the channel's `dest_id`, the TTL mapping and the removed member's keys. The media namespace, its read pairs, quota and egress, and FR-3.4's media acceptance tests are the channel-media slice's; **Q38** decides whether Phase 10's exit waits for it (default: no; FR-3.4 recorded as partly open; Phase 11's Q17 aligned at merge). No identifier of this document collides with Phase 11's: ADR-40 … 43, T36–T41, L8 and migrations 6–7 stay here.

### 19.25 Bookkeeping of round 10c

### 19.26 S0 corrections (normative; wins over §19.1 … §19.25)

**What happened.** Slice S0 ran the checks V10-1 … V10-14 for real on the owner's workstation (`docs/design/faza10-s0-evidence.md`; spike `ghost/client-core/mls-spike`, evidence tooling outside the ghost workspace) and proposed corrections S0-1 … S0-14. Two adversarial reviews then examined the evidence: an **honesty** lens (does each number and claim hold on re-run?) and a **design-impact** lens (what does each finding do to the design, and is anything missing?). Every finding was checked by re-running the measurement it challenged; the evidence document's Appendix C lists each with its verdict. All were confirmed, except: the impact lens's reading that the runners sat idle with jobs queued (about 1 320 minutes, "the laptop off") is **rejected** — with every trigger crawled, a job waited with no runner busy for one minute in nine days, and the long waits are scheduled jobs queued behind push-triggered ones, as the honesty lens found; its "89 % of runner-minutes between 07:00 and 20:00 UTC" holds for scheduled jobs only (65.5 % over every trigger); its optional monotonic `commit_minute` is **declined** with its reason (CO-2). The corrections below carry the reviews' changes; **S0-12 and S0-15 are new protocol rules and go to the protocol lens of the next adversarial review** before Q1 is answered.
