### 19.27 Targeted protocol review of S0-12 and S0-15 (2026-09-28)

**Precedence (normative).** This section wins over §19.1 … §19.26 (and over the sections they touch) for the two rules it reviews — the trusted-day KeyPackage-lifetime predicate (S0-12) and the committers anchor (S0-15) — and for nothing else. It is the targeted review §19.26 records as owed before S1 ("**S0-12 and S0-15 are new protocol rules and go to the protocol lens of the next adversarial review** before Q1 is answered"). Each rule was attacked against OpenMLS 0.9.0's real behaviour at the pinned tag `3a3e35de…`; the spike carries a probe for every finding (`ghost/client-core/mls-spike/core/tests/r10d_s0_12_lifetimes.rs`, `r10d_s0_15_anchor.rs`; run 2026-09-28, evidence E-61 … E-64). Findings are `TR-n`.

**Sources verified from primary material (accessed 2026-09-28).** RFC 9420 §7.2 (`https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9420.txt`): "Applications MUST define a maximum total lifetime that is acceptable for a LeafNode, and reject any LeafNode where the total lifetime is longer than this duration. In order to avoid disagreements about whether a LeafNode has a valid lifetime, the clients in a group SHOULD maintain time synchronization (e.g., using [NTP])." §7.3: sending → "the client MUST verify that the current time is within the range of the lifetime field"; **receiving** → "it is RECOMMENDED that the client verifies … (This check is not mandatory because the LeafNode might have expired in the time between when the message was sent and when it was received.)". §12.4.3.1: "Verify the signature on the GroupInfo object. … The public key is taken from the LeafNode of the ratchet tree with leaf index `signer`." OpenMLS 0.9.0 sources: `key_packages/key_package_in.rs:206-208` (`life_time.validate()` → `lifetime.rs:87` → `validate_with_time(SystemTime::now())`); `lifetime.rs:116` `has_acceptable_range` **called nowhere**; `MlsGroup::unprotect_message`, `PublicGroup::parse_message` and `UnverifiedMessage::verify` are all `pub(crate)` (no public API returns decrypted-but-unvalidated content); `ProcessedWelcome::unverified_group_info()` and `VerifiableGroupInfo::verify_no_out` are **public**; `keys_for_welcome` deletes the joiner's KeyPackage inside `ProcessedWelcome::new_from_welcome` (`creation.rs:706-713`).

#### S0-12: the trusted-day lifetime predicate

**TR-1 (the "both branches" rule is not computable on the refusal branch; corrects CO-2, CO-4).** S0-12 has the member evaluate "one predicate on the member's trusted day, **on both branches of OpenMLS's answer**." A received commit is a PrivateMessage; OpenMLS decrypts and validates it in one `pub(crate)` step (`process_message`), and its KeyPackage-lifetime check runs **before** the update path, the proposal set and everything GHOST checks (V10-11, E-52). The two branches are not symmetric:

- **Accept branch** (device clock within the KeyPackage's range): `process_message` returns the staged commit, so GHOST reads each Add's KeyPackage and its bounds and applies its trusted-day predicate. **Measured (E-61):** the staged commit exposes the sender leaf and the Add count. This branch works as S0-12 says.
- **Refusal branch** (device clock outside the range): `process_message` returns only `Err(ValidationError(KeyPackageVerifyError(LifetimeError(Expired|NotValidYet{not_after|not_before, now}))))`. **Measured (E-61):** the error carries **one** `not_after` — the first failing KeyPackage of the commit — and **no sender, no proposal list, no rank, no second bound**. There is no public OpenMLS API that returns the decrypted-but-unvalidated framed content (`unprotect_message`/`parse_message`/`verify` are `pub(crate)`), and the seal's `open` recovers only the PrivateMessage's cleartext header (epoch, content type) and its authenticated data (`GhostCommitAad`, which names each Add's `kp_ref` but **not** the KeyPackage bytes or their bounds, and carries **no** MLS sender). So on the refusal branch GHOST can compute **neither the trusted-day predicate** (no bounds) **nor the candidate's rank** (no sender) **nor CO-1's "no proposal" test** (no proposal list). S0-12's "lifetime-pending: ranked by `(rank, hash)`, blocks the settle while it would win, becomes invalid when the trusted day stops accepting the KeyPackage" is therefore only partly implementable: the rank is unknown, and "the trusted day stops accepting" can never be observed (the bounds are never learned), so the escape to "invalid" is dead.

*Correction (normative; §6.4 CO-2, CO-4).* The trusted-day predicate is evaluated **only on the accept branch**, where the staged commit exposes the Adds. A `LifetimeError` refusal (the typed error is the discriminator, distinguishing a lifetime refusal from any other `process_message` error) makes the candidate **lifetime-pending with its rank treated as the lowest (255)**, so a decidable valid candidate of any real committer always outranks it: the settle proceeds on the decidable winner, and the lifetime-pending candidate blocks the settle (`clock_blocked`) **only when no decidable valid candidate is held for the epoch**. It is never merged, never counted in CO-3's successor counts, and leaves that state only when the member's own device clock moves so OpenMLS accepts it (GHOST then reads the bounds and decides) or another candidate wins. This converges: two honest members whose device clocks straddle the KeyPackage's bound either both merge the decidable winner or, if the junk commit is the only candidate, both remain in epoch e (one `clock_blocked`, one having found it invalid on decryption) — neither merges, so no divergence.

**TR-2 (an insider stall the refusal branch opens; new mutant MM-46).** Because the refusal branch hides the sender and the proposal list, a **member of any rank** (rank 255 included) can build a commit carrying an Add of an **expired** KeyPackage, seal it under `K_ep(e)` (mode 0x11, which every member can produce), and deposit it. Every member whose device clock is past the KeyPackage's `not_after` reaches the refusal branch and cannot tell this junk (a rank-255-with-proposal commit, invalid by CO-1) from a valid committer commit its own clock happens to reject. Under round 10c's / S0-12's "blocks the settle while it would win," a low-ground hash on the junk commit would stall the epoch. TR-1's correction closes it: the refusal-branch candidate is lowest-priority, so a real committer's decidable commit always wins and the junk never blocks; the stall is confined to the honest case where the member's **own** device clock is wrong and there is nothing else to merge — the declared L8-C20/R-14 residue. The blob is not "unopenable foreign" (it opens the seal, then MLS refuses), so CO-11/CO-12 do not fire on it; the new lowest-priority rule is what bounds it.

**TR-3 (the constant lifetime removes OpenMLS's device-clock dependency entirely; Q42, recommended default).** OpenMLS validates a received Add's KeyPackage lifetime against the device's **wall clock** (`SystemTime::now()`), **not** GHOST's trusted clock, and — against RFC 9420 §7.3, which makes the receive-side check RECOMMENDED "because the LeafNode might have expired … between when the message was sent and when it was received" — OpenMLS makes it **mandatory** (switchable only by a crate-internal test flag). The trusted clock is bounded (σ = 3 d, from Arti/NTP, F7:58); the device **wall** clock has no such bound, and under Tor-only, with NTP often unreachable, an honest phone's wall clock can be days wrong — so an honest member can reach the refusal branch on a perfectly valid commit and stall (TR-1). GHOST does not otherwise use the lifetime field: freshness and expiry are enforced by `GhostKpBind.day` (checked on the **trusted** day by the admitting committer and, through the AAD, by every member, §6.2.4) and by idle eviction. The lifetime field's only effect in GHOST is to arm OpenMLS's unavoidable wall-clock gate.

**Measured (E-62):** a canonical KeyPackage built with the **constant lifetime `[0, 2⁶⁴ − 1]`** is **395 bytes** — identical to the day-aligned one (`Lifetime` is two fixed `u64`s), so the canonical size (S0-4) and G-M7's golden are unchanged; it is accepted by the committer's `KeyPackageIn::validate`, by every receiver of the Add at any later time, and by the **default** `JoinBuilder` (lifetime validation on) over a tree whose KeyPackage leaves — the creator's included — all carry it; a 4-member channel built this way has equal epoch authenticators, and beside it a finite-lifetime Add is still refused once expired. With the constant lifetime **OpenMLS's lifetime gate never fires**, so the refusal branch (TR-1), the `clock_blocked` state, the L8-C20 malicious-committer split and R-14 all disappear; GHOST's own trusted-day check on `GhostKpBind.day` (evaluated on the always-reached accept branch) keeps freshness, and its residual honest-skew split (a `GhostKpBind.day` a malicious committer places at the edge of two members' `[commit_day − 7, commit_day + 3]` windows) is the same 2σ trusted-day effect any trusted-day rule has, detected by CO-11/CO-12 — but it is GHOST's deterministic check on **decryptable** data on **one** branch, never OpenMLS's undecidable wall-clock refusal. RFC 9420 §7.2's "maximum total lifetime" is an application obligation GHOST discharges through `GhostKpBind` (its own reject-if-too-old rule), consistent with the RFC leaving the mechanism to the application; GHOST is a closed, suite-locked deployment with no MLS interop other than the passive-client vectors (which build no KeyPackages), so no peer implementation rejects the wide lifetime.

**Q42 (default yes): adopt the constant lifetime `[0, 2⁶⁴ − 1]` for the canonical KeyPackage** (§6.2.4 (B), ADR-40 point 7), removing OpenMLS's device-wall-clock dependency, the `clock_blocked` state, L8-C20 and R-14, with freshness left to `GhostKpBind.day` on the trusted clock. If the owner keeps finite lifetimes, TR-1's refusal-branch correction (lowest-priority lifetime-pending) is the baseline and L8-C20/R-14 stand. Either way S0-12's "ranked as valid" and "becomes invalid when the trusted day stops accepting" are withdrawn (unimplementable on the refusal branch, TR-1).

#### S0-15: the committers anchor

**TR-4 (a committer-set change makes capture guaranteed, not a race; sharpens L8-C25, Q41).** S0-15's anchor names the committers **at the invite's issue**; L8-C25 declares that a committer named then, even one removed since, can still forge a Welcome the invitee accepts. The review makes the exposure concrete and worse than "can still": when a commit **removes a committer** it (i) changes `committers`, so CO-5 **voids the invite** and the real committers **never issue its Welcome**, and (ii) opens generation g + 1 (a Remove rotates, D6) while the removed committer keeps `JK_g` and can deposit into the draining `ns_g` for 14 days. The invitee, whose invite names generation g and who listens on `ns_g`, therefore receives **only** the removed committer's forged Welcome — the real side produces none — so capture is **guaranteed** for anyone who acts on the (now-voided) invite, not a race. **Measured (E-63):** the removed moderator, from a group of its own under the channel id with the anchored committers list, produces a Welcome whose signer is itself (an anchored committer) and whose digest matches the invite's; the invitee's anchor **accepts** it and its KeyPackage is consumed.

*Correction (normative; §6.2.5, §6.11, CO-12).* A captured invitee holds a group with `group_id = channel_id` whose only member is the forger, sealed under keys the real channel does not share, so it receives no real traffic — it is a **silent orphan** in the sense of §19.15. The recovery machinery already exists and is now named for this case: a joiner has no roster history, so a channel that produces **no authenticated message from another member and no committer commit within one handshake TTL after the join** raises `stale_suspected` and, past the generation's drain, `reinvite_needed` (§6.7), and a fresh restore invite (whose digest names the current committers) re-admits it with a fresh KeyPackage (the consumed one does not matter). The joiner-side timer is shorter than a member's 9 days (a fresh joiner legitimately expects traffic sooner); the exact value is set at S7 with the other liveness timers. The sponsor's Phase 13 UI warns that changing a channel's moderators invalidates outstanding invites and that they must be re-shared. L8-C25 and Q41 are updated: capture is **guaranteed** once a committer is removed, and recovery is `reinvite_needed` — not silent forever.

**TR-5 (the same for a rejoiner's anchor).** A rejoiner's anchor is the committers of its **last final epoch**; a committer removed on the live branch since the rejoiner went offline is still in that set, so it can forge a rejoin Welcome the rejoiner accepts. **Measured (E-63):** a removed moderator forges a rejoin Welcome whose signer is among the stale member's last-known committers, and the anchor accepts it. Same class as TR-4, same recovery (`stale_suspected` → `reinvite_needed`, then a restore invite); L8-C25 covers it.

**TR-6 (the digest must be over the committer *set*, not the ordered list; corrects §5.2, §5.9; new mutant MM-45).** The committers digest of §5.2 is computed over the committers **in list order**. The anchor uses only **set membership** ("the GroupInfo signer is one of the named committers"), so the order carries no security. But an **ordered** digest changes when the list is merely **reordered** — a **transfer of rank 0** (§19.13) reorders `committers` without changing the set — so CO-5 **voids every outstanding invite** on a transfer of rank 0, an availability cost with no security benefit. **Measured (E-64):** reordering two committers changes the ordered digest and leaves the set digest (over the committers sorted bytewise) unchanged, while dropping a committer changes both.

*Correction (normative; §5.2, §5.9, §6.2.4).* The committers digest is `SHA-256("ghost/v1/ch/committers" ‖ channel_id ‖ u8(k) ‖ sort(committers))` — the committer keys **sorted bytewise ascending**, so the digest binds the **set** of keys that may sign a Welcome, order-independent. A transfer of rank 0 (a reorder) no longer voids invites; an add or removal of a committer (a set change) still voids them (CO-5) and still requires an admitting commit to leave the set unchanged (CO-2). This is a change to a compatibility-contract derivation (§5.2), pinned by the `ch_derive.txt` vectors; it supersedes §5.2's ordered digest.

**TR-7 (the anchor must be checked on the *unverified* GroupInfo, before the ratchet-tree validation; corrects §6.2.5, fn 5; new mutant MM-44).** S0-15's anchor as written reads the joined context's committers and the GroupInfo signer from a **built** `StagedWelcome`, i.e. after `JoinBuilder::build()` has validated the **entire ratchet tree** (every leaf signature, parent hashes). A holder of `JK_g` — an invite holder, a member removed at the end of g — can deposit forged Welcome blobs into the generation namespace; the invitee opens each (mode 0x13) and, without an earlier check, pays a full tree validation per forgery. **Measured (E-64), forged Welcome refused: pre-check vs full `build()`:** at 32 members **0.84 ms vs 6.02 ms**, at 100 members **1.52 ms vs 24.48 ms** (medians of 9; forged and real Welcomes the same size, 9 135 B and 26 143 B) — a 7–16× CPU amplification on the invitee per forged blob, ×(15–25) on a low-end phone (≈ 0.4–0.6 s each). The pre-check verifies the GroupInfo signature against ≤ 8 anchored committer keys and does no tree validation.

*Correction (normative; §6.2.5, §10.2 fn 5).* `welcomeJoin` checks the anchor on the **unverified** GroupInfo before `JoinBuilder::build()`: after `ProcessedWelcome::new_from_welcome` (which HPKE-opens the group secrets and AEAD-opens the GroupInfo — no tree validation), it reads `unverified_group_info().group_context()`'s `GhostChannelContext.committers`, checks their set digest equals the invite's `committers_digest` (a rejoiner: the digest of its last final epoch's committers), and verifies the GroupInfo signature with `VerifiableGroupInfo::verify_no_out` (public) under each anchored committer key; only if one verifies does it call `build()` and apply the join. `new_from_welcome` deletes the joiner's KeyPackage (`creation.rs:706-713`), so the whole call runs under value semantics and its change-set is discarded on any failure — the KeyPackage survives a refused Welcome (the V10-8/S0-15 property is unchanged; only the order of the checks moves the tree validation after the cheap anchor). The measurement (E-64) confirms the pre-check refuses both forgeries and accepts the real Welcome with the KeyPackage intact.

#### Disposition, tests, mutants

| Finding | Verdict | Correction | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| TR-1 lifetime "both branches" not computable on the refusal branch | CONFIRMED (E-61) | trusted-day predicate on the accept branch only; a `LifetimeError` refusal → lifetime-pending at lowest rank, blocks the settle only as the sole candidate | CO-2, CO-4, T41, CS-C3 |
| TR-2 insider expired-KeyPackage stall | CONFIRMED (E-61) | closed by TR-1's lowest-priority rule; confined to the honest own-wrong-clock case (L8-C20, R-14) | CO-2, CO-4, §12.3, MM-46 |
| TR-3 constant lifetime removes the device-clock gate | CONFIRMED (E-62) | Q42 (default yes): constant lifetime `[0, 2⁶⁴ − 1]`; freshness via `GhostKpBind.day`; removes `clock_blocked`, L8-C20, R-14 | §3.5, §3.6, §6.2.4, CO-2, ADR-40 point 7, Q42 |
| TR-4 committer-set change → guaranteed capture | CONFIRMED (E-63) | recovery named (`stale_suspected` → `reinvite_needed`, restore invite); L8-C25, Q41 updated | §6.2.5, §6.11, CO-12, L8-C25, Q41, MM-47 |
| TR-5 rejoiner anchor captured by a since-removed committer | CONFIRMED (E-63) | same recovery; L8-C25 | §6.7, §6.2.5, L8-C25 |
| TR-6 ordered digest voids invites on a transfer of rank 0 | CONFIRMED (E-64) | set digest (committers sorted bytewise) | §5.2, §5.9, §6.2.4, MM-45 |
| TR-7 anchor checked after full tree validation (CPU DoS) | CONFIRMED (E-64) | pre-check on the unverified GroupInfo before `build()` | §6.2.5, §10.2 fn 5, MM-44 |

**Mutants (registered with killers; extend §12.9).** **MM-44** the anchor checked only after `JoinBuilder::build()` (killer: the TR-7 probe / JN, a forged Welcome is refused before any tree validation, measured pre-check cost). **MM-45** the committers digest over the ordered list, not the sorted set (killer: the TR-6 vector — a transfer of rank 0 must not change the digest, a committer add/remove must). **MM-46** a `LifetimeError`-refused commit ranked, merged, counted, or blocking the settle while a decidable valid candidate is held (killer: SCN-CM's insider expired-KeyPackage-commit adversary + EM-3 with device clocks that straddle a bound; the refusal-branch probe). **MM-47** a captured joiner never reaches `reinvite_needed` (killer: the TR-4 capture → `stale_suspected` scenario). **Tests:** the seven probes above move into `ghost-client-mls`'s S2/S4/S5 suites and G-M7; EM-3 (§12.3) gains device clocks and the refusal-branch adversary; the `ModelRelay` FAKE_WELCOME mode (§12.4) gains the removed-committer variant and the CPU-amplification measurement; JN (§12.6) gains the pre-check-before-build assertion and the set-digest reorder case.

### 19.28 Channel password (owner requirement 2026-09-27)

**Precedence (normative).** This section wins over §19.1 … §19.27 and over the sections it touches for what it names: the channel password that protects **joining**. It designs the owner's 2026-09-27 requirement — the channel administrator (owner, rank 0) can set, change or remove a password so that joining needs **both** the single-use invite (D10, unchanged) **and** the password, shared out of band; the password does **not** lock local viewing. Design only; the evidence is measured in the spike (`ghost/client-core/mls-spike/core/tests/r10d_password.rs`, run 2026-09-28, E-65 … E-68), which adds `argon2` 0.6.0 as a **dev-dependency of the spike's own `[workspace]`** only (never a shipped or host graph).

#### 19.28.1 Mechanism comparison and choice

Three mechanisms, judged on offline guessing (leaked link, seized device, relays), who issues invites, the effect of a password change, interaction with the committers anchor / `INVITE_ISSUED` / admission evidence / rejoin (Q33) / restore (Q19), relay metadata, CP-09, licences, low-end cost, and the Phase 13 UX.

| Criterion | (a) password-sealed invite (Argon2id) | (b) proof in the join request vs a stored verifier | (c) PAKE over relays (CPace / SPAKE2 / OPAQUE) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Offline guess from a **leaked link** | yes — the link carries the salt and the sealed secrets; Argon2id-bounded (measured below) | no — the link carries no password material | no — the link carries no password material |
| Offline guess from a **seized member/committer device** | **no verifier is stored anywhere** — a seized device gives nothing beyond membership | **yes** — the verifier is in the policy every member holds; any one seized member device leaks it | augmented (OPAQUE): **yes** (server-compromise dictionary, precomputation-resistant); balanced (CPace/SPAKE2): no verifier, but every member needs the password |
| Offline guess from **relays** | no — the invite is out of band; the join request is a normal sealed blob | no | PAKEs resist passive observation by design |
| Who issues; does a sponsor need the password | the sponsor **needs the password** to seal (a compromised member without it cannot mint invites) | the sponsor issues **without** the password (checked at admission) | the invitee runs the PAKE; the committer (server) needs the verifier / the password |
| A password change | affects **future** invites; old sealed links stop at admission via `pw_version` (below) | a clean policy commit; applies to all future joins at once | a new verifier / re-registration |
| Committers anchor / `INVITE_ISSUED` / admission evidence | **composes unchanged** — the password gates reading the link; admission is by `invite_sig` as in D10/§19.2 | the proof is the committer's admission **policy** (B), like invite freshness (§19.2) | interactive; a PAKE transcript in the commit's authenticated data or a committer-only check |
| rejoin (Q33) / restore (Q19) | skip the password (re-admission of a known pseudonym), Q48 | same | same |
| Relay metadata / indistinguishability | out-of-band invite; padded to one size (measured equal) → a password channel is **not** distinguishable at relays or by link size | the join request carries a proof; pad to one size | new relay round trips → a **reflex** (T40) and new observable |
| CP-09 (no novel crypto, vetted crates) | Argon2id (RFC 9106) + HKDF + ChaCha20-Poly1305 — all standard, all already present | a **safe** (non-replayable) proof needs a challenge or a PAKE → drifts to (c) or novel crypto; a naive stored-hash proof is replayable by any `JK_g` holder | a PAKE is a large, **interactive** addition; the only audited Rust crate is `opaque-ke` (NCC Group, 2021, at an old version) |
| Licences (permissive, pin by hash) | `argon2`/`blake2`/`password-hash` MIT OR Apache-2.0, or BouncyCastle (already a dependency) on Kotlin | as (a) plus the verifier scheme | `spake2` 0.4.0 "have not yet received any formal … security reviews" (verified 2026-09-28); `pake-cpace` 0.1.7 unaudited; `opaque-ke` 4.0.1 Apache-2.0/MIT |
| Standards maturity | Argon2id: **RFC 9106** (Standards-track-adjacent, widely deployed) | — | CPace: draft-irtf-cfrg-cpace-21, "Sent to the RFC Editor," **not yet an RFC** (verified 2026-09-28); SPAKE2: RFC 9382 Informational ("was not selected" in the CFRG competition); OPAQUE: RFC 9807 (Informational, 2025) |
| Low-end cost | one Argon2id at join (measured; ~2–3 s on a low-end phone at the chosen profile) | one Argon2id (if the verifier is Argon2id) | several round trips, each a relay session |

**Decision: (a), the password-sealed invite with Argon2id.** It is the only option that (i) obeys CP-09 with primitives already in the graph and adds **no** interactive protocol, **no** new relay message and **no** reflex (T40); (ii) stores **no** verifier anywhere, so it opens **no** seized-device offline-guessing surface (unlike (b) and augmented (c)); (iii) composes unchanged with D10, the S0-15 committers anchor, `INVITE_ISSUED` and the admission-evidence split (§19.2) — the password gates only the reading of the link; and (iv) leaves a password channel indistinguishable at relays. Its cost — offline guessing from a **leaked link** — is the narrowest surface (a link is a bearer secret shared with few people, single-use, expiring ≤ 7 days) and is Argon2id-hardened; it is declared (L8-C26). (b) moves the guessing surface to every member's device (worse); (c) adds an interactive PAKE that fights the async, batched, no-reflex admission model and has no vetted-and-current audited crate.

#### 19.28.2 What the password does NOT protect (normative)

- A **member can still share content** — screenshots, forwarding, re-posting; the password restrains no member.
- A **committer can admit anyone** — the password gates the link, not admission; a malicious committer admits any KeyPackage regardless (CO-2 is the committer's policy), and any holder who decrypts an invite can pass the decrypted secrets to a non-password-holder.
- A **weak password falls to offline guessing from a leaked link** (Argon2id-bounded; a link is a bearer secret).
- It does **not** lock local viewing (the requirement is explicit) and adds **no** cryptographic access control to posts (every member reads every post; the password gates only joining).

#### 19.28.3 Byte formats (normative; TLV numbers do not clash with §5)

**`ChannelInviteV2` (`ghost://channel/`, replaces V1; version byte 2).**

```
header (cleartext in the link) = version(1) = 0x02 || kdf_code(1) || pw_version(u16_be) || salt(16)     // 20 bytes
sealed  = ChaCha20-Poly1305(k, nonce = 0^12, AD, inner)                                                  // |inner| + 16
inner   = the ChannelInviteV1 body of §5.9 (suite … name), padded to a fixed 303 bytes
AD      = "ghost/v1/ch/invite" || 0x02 || kdf_code || u16_be(pw_version) || salt                         (not transmitted)
k       = HKDF-SHA256(ikm, salt = salt, info = "ghost/v1/ch/invite-seal" || kdf_code, L = 32)
ikm     = Argon2id(password_NFKD, salt, profile(kdf_code))   for kdf_code >= 1   (a password invite)
        = salt (16 bytes)                                     for kdf_code = 0    (an open invite, key from the salt alone)
link    = "ghost://channel/" || base32(header || sealed), lowercase, unpadded
```

- **One size for open and password invites:** 20 + 303 + 16 = **339 bytes** whatever the `kdf_code` and whatever the channel name (the inner body is padded to 303), so a link does not reveal password-vs-open or the name; base32 is ≈ 543 characters after the 16-character prefix. **Measured (E-67):** an open and a password invite are byte-for-byte the same length; no 8-byte run of the inner body appears anywhere in the link; a leaked link exposes only the 20-byte header (version, `kdf_code`, `pw_version`, a random salt). (The spike measured a 336-byte simplified header of version+salt; the specified header adds `kdf_code(1)` + `pw_version(2)`.)
- `kdf_code`: 0 = open (V1's effect, new only in format); 1 = the P0 Argon2id profile (Q44). Future profiles take further codes, so the KDF is agile without a format change.
- `pw_version` binds the invite to a password epoch: it is in the header, in the seal's AD (so a link sealed under epoch N opens only with epoch N's password), and in `INVITE_ISSUED` (below) for the committer's check.

**`GhostChannelContext` (§5.3) gains a password policy — no verifier.** `flags` bit 2 = **join requires a password** (0 in an open channel; set/cleared by the owner); a new `uint16 pw_version` after `idle_evict_days`, bumped by the owner on every password change. **The password itself and any value derived from it are never in the context** (that is the whole point of (a) over (b)); the context records only *that* the channel is password-protected and *which* epoch. Setting a password = set the flag + bump `pw_version` + distribute the password out of band; changing = bump `pw_version` + re-distribute; removing = clear the flag. Each is a GroupContextExtensions commit from rank 0 that every member processes (ADR-27 point 8's "the policy is itself a message shown to everyone"), and the validity checks of §5.3 gain: `pw_version` monotonically non-decreasing across a commit.

**`INVITE_ISSUED` (§5.5, kind 0x03) gains TLV `0x0015 pw_version` (u16, the epoch the invite was sealed under; authenticated by the post's MLS signature; absent = 0 = open).** 0x0015 is free (§5.5 uses 0x0010 … 0x0014). No other TLV changes.

#### 19.28.4 Protocol steps (normative)

1. **Owner sets/changes/removes** the password: a policy commit toggling `flags` bit 2 and bumping `pw_version` (§19.28.3); the owner distributes the new password out of band. Removing clears the flag; future invites are open.
2. **Sponsor issues** in a password channel (`CH-INVITE`): a member with `members_may_invite` (or a committer) who **holds the current password** (Q46) draws `salt`, derives `ikm = Argon2id(password, salt, profile)`, builds `ChannelInviteV2` with `kdf_code`, `pw_version = context.pw_version` and the sealed body, and posts `INVITE_ISSUED(…, pw_version)`; the UI reminds it to share the password out of band, separately from the link.
3. **Invitee accepts** (`CH-ACCEPT`): parses the V2 header; for `kdf_code ≥ 1` it prompts for the password, runs Argon2id over `salt`, derives `k` and opens the sealed body (a wrong password or a tampered link → AEAD failure → "wrong password", nothing written); then proceeds exactly as §6.2.2 with the decrypted V1 body. The join request (§5.6) is **unchanged** — the password leaves no trace in it.
4. **Committer admits** (§6.2.4): unchanged, plus one policy check (B) — `INVITE_ISSUED.pw_version == context.pw_version`; a stale epoch (a password change since the invite) is refused (a change voids old links at admission). A rejoin/restore carries `pw_version` 0 and is not subject to the check (Q48).

#### 19.28.5 KDF placement, parameters, schema, JNI

- **Where Argon2id runs (Q47, default Kotlin).** The password → 32-byte `ikm` step runs in **Kotlin via BouncyCastle's `Argon2BytesGenerator`** (bcprov-jdk18on 1.85.2, already a dependency of `:identity`, `:network` and `:messaging`), like the existing Kotlin key derivations (`KeyDerivation.kt`); the derived key crosses JNI once and Rust does only HKDF + the ChaCha20-Poly1305 seal (fn 14). This keeps `argon2` and its `blake2`/`base64ct`/`password-hash`/`phc` out of the shipped Rust graph and the allowlist. A shared **RFC 9106 §5.3 Argon2id vector** (`protocol/test-vectors/ch_password.txt`) is replayed by BouncyCastle (Kotlin) and by the spike's `argon2` crate (Rust) so the two agree; **measured (E-65):** the RustCrypto `argon2` 0.6.0 reproduces the RFC 9106 §5.3 tag `0d640df5…6b01e659` exactly. The alternative — Argon2id in Rust (`argon2`, +4 crates to G-M1) — is Q47's other option.
- **Parameters (Q44).** RFC 9106 §4 profiles, **measured on this host (Intel Core Ultra 9 275HX, single-threaded; medians of 7, E-66):** 64 MiB/t=3/p=4 (RFC second-recommended) **354 ms**; 64 MiB/t=1/p=4 **126 ms**; 32 MiB/t=3/p=4 **143 ms**; 19 MiB/t=2/p=1 (OWASP baseline) **62 ms**; 256 MiB/t=1/p=4 **472 ms**. `argon2` without its `parallel` feature (and BouncyCastle likewise, single-threaded here) computes the p lanes serially, so p raises memory-hardness at a compute cost even without threads. Joining is a rare, deliberate, foreground action, so a KDF of a few seconds is acceptable and raises the leaked-link guessing cost. **Default: m = 64 MiB, t = 1, p = 4** (`kdf_code = 1`), ≈ 126 ms here → an estimated **2–3 s on a low-end phone** (×15–25, not measured); Bitwarden warns above 64 MiB on iOS-autofill only, which does not apply to an Android foreground join, so 64 MiB is safe. S2's device step re-measures on arm64; if > ~3–4 s the owner drops to a lighter profile via `kdf_code`. Floors: `t ≥ 1`, `p ≥ 1`, `m ≥ 19 456` KiB (OWASP baseline), enforced by the codec (MM-PW3).
- **Schema (migration 7).** `ch_channel` gains `pw_required` (0/1) and `pw_version` (u16, default 0), mirrored from the context for the UI; `ch_invite_issued` gains `pw_version` (u16). **No password, verifier or Argon2id output is stored at rest** — the sponsor re-enters the password per issue (or the OS keystore holds it if the user opts in, Phase 13); `ch_invite_own` keeps only the link (as today). A physical-scan canary (T3) asserts no password and no Argon2id key survives in the database, the WAL or native memory after a join or an issue (MM-PW5).
- **JNI (the closed `MlsNative` surface).** Only fn 14 changes: `inviteBuild(h, channelId, gEnv, fields, kdfCode, pwVersion, salt, pwKey32?)` seals under `HKDF(pwKey32 for a password invite | salt for an open invite)`; `inviteParse(bytes, pwKey32?)` opens it (a wrong key → `NON_CANONICAL`/AEAD failure). `pwKey32` is the BouncyCastle Argon2id output (or null/zero for an open invite); it is a per-invite secret Kotlin computes, like the media slice's reserved fn-10 arguments. No new function; the surface list (G-M6) grows by these two arguments only.

#### 19.28.6 Tests and mutants

**Tests.** Vectors: the RFC 9106 §5.3 Argon2id vector replayed by BouncyCastle and by Rust (E-65); `ch_invite.txt` extended to V2 (open and password, wrong password, tampered link, `pw_version` mismatch). A **leaked-link oracle (E-67):** a link without the password yields no `ns`, `read_base`, `deposit_base` or `join_key`, hence no join request and **no membership and no generation secret** (the inner body does not appear in the link; the open key, a wrong password and a tampered link are all refused). A **wrong-password** case (AEAD failure, nothing written). A **password-change** case (a `pw_version` bump; a committer refuses an invite of an older `pw_version`; the JN suite). **Fuzzing** of `inviteParse` (arbitrary bytes and arbitrary `pwKey32`: never panics, wrong length refused, AEAD failure clean) joins `mls-fuzz` (§12.10).

**Mutants (registered with killers; extend §12.9).** **MM-PW1** the invite key derived without the password in a password channel (e.g. from the salt only) — killer: the leaked-link oracle (the link opens without the password). **MM-PW2** the committer skips the `pw_version` check — killer: the password-change JN case (a stale invite is admitted). **MM-PW3** Argon2id below the parameter floor (`t = 0`, `m` below the baseline) — killer: the codec floor assertion and the RFC 9106 vector. **MM-PW4** the invite size or shape differs open-vs-password (a distinguishable link, or a distinguishable relay blob) — killer: the size-equality test (E-67) and JC-2 (no password material at relays). **MM-PW5** the password or the Argon2id key persisted at rest or left in native memory — killer: the T3 physical-scan canary. A password channel adds no relay observable (the invite is out of band; the join request and `INVITE_ISSUED` are the existing shapes), so JC-1 … JC-7 and NI-CH1 … NI-CH4 are unchanged — the world's password channels must give the same relay view as its open channels (a new privacy assertion, folded into JC-2 and NI-CH1).

#### 19.28.7 Effort and identifiers

Effort (F9 pace, §14): the password adds **+2.0 working days** (S2 +0.75: the V2 invite codec, the seal, the Kotlin/BC KDF and the shared vector; S4 +0.75: `pw_version` in the context, `INVITE_ISSUED` and the committer check, the schema, the JN and leaked-link tests; S9/S11 +0.5: the mutants and the fuzz target), and the §19.27 review corrections **+0.75 day** (S5 +0.5: the refusal-branch predicate and the EM-3 device-clock adversary; S4 +0.25: the anchor pre-check reorder and the set digest) — **+2.75 days in all, §14's 40.75 → 43.5** (Q42, if adopted, removes the `clock_blocked` machinery and trims S5 by ≈ 0.25, not netted here). New identifiers: TR-1 … TR-7; Q42 … Q48; `ChannelInviteV2`, `kdf_code`, `pw_version`; `INVITE_ISSUED` TLV 0x0015; `GhostChannelContext` `flags` bit 2 and `pw_version`; MM-44 … MM-47, MM-PW1 … MM-PW5; L8-C26; E-61 … E-68. The residues go to L8-C26 (Appendix B.3).
